# Optional Skills, Optional MCPs, Plugin Packs & the Plugin Ecosystem

This shard is the exhaustive inventory of everything Hermes Agent v2026.8.31 ships **but does not
activate by default**: the 137 `optional-skills/**/SKILL.md` bundles, the 65 Nous-approved
`optional-mcps/*/manifest.yaml` catalog entries, the first-party plugins under `plugins/` that are
neither platform adapters nor model providers, and the machinery that installs them (community
plugin index, plugin packs, capability consent, MCP catalog installer + tool checklist).
Deliberately left to sibling shards: `plugins/platforms/*` (platform adapters) and
`plugins/model-providers/*` (model providers) → CLI/platform shards; the full `hermes plugins` /
`hermes skills` / `hermes mcp` CLI flag matrix → CLI shards; the Plugin/Skill/MCP **pages** of the web
dashboard and desktop app → web/desktop shards; the plugin Python API in depth (`ctx.*`, hook
signatures, `hermes_cli/plugins.py` internals) → developer shard; the 83 built-in tools and 59
toolsets → tools shard.

---

## A. Ecosystem mechanics — how optional things get in

### Optional skills catalog (`optional-skills/`)  `id: optional.skills-catalog`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** Repo/install tree `optional-skills/<category>/[<subcategory>/]<skill>/SKILL.md`; discoverable from the CLI via `hermes skills browse --source official` and `hermes skills search <q>`; documented at `website/docs/reference/optional-skills-catalog.md` → page title "Optional Skills Catalog", intro line "Optional skills ship with hermes-agent under `optional-skills/` but are **not active by default**."
- **What it does:** Ships 137 curated skill bundles inside the Hermes distribution that are present on disk but never loaded into the agent until the user explicitly installs one. Installing copies the bundle into the active skills directory so the agent's skill loader can see it.
- **How it works:** The tree is resolved by `hermes_constants.py:338 get_optional_skills_dir()` (packaged installs may relocate it; fallback `get_hermes_home()/optional-skills`). Each leaf directory holds a `SKILL.md` with YAML frontmatter (`name`, `description`, `version`, `author`, `license`, `platforms`, optional `dependencies`/`prerequisites`/`required_commands`/`compatibility`/`setup`, and a `metadata.hermes` block with `tags`, `category`, `homepage`, `related_skills`, `requires_toolsets`) followed by a Markdown procedure body; optional sibling folders `scripts/`, `references/`, `templates/`, `examples/`, `prompts/`, `tests/`, `workflows/`. `hermes_cli/skills_hub.py` treats these as source `"official"` with trust rank 200 (`skills_hub.py:404`), sorts them first in browse/search results (`skills_hub.py:458-461`) and prints `★ N official optional skill(s) from Nous Research` (`skills_hub.py:484`). Nested identifiers are supported: every segment between `official` and the final slug is kept as the category path (`skills_hub.py:672-675`), which is why `official/mlops/training/trl-fine-tuning` works.
- **Inputs / options:** Install identifier form `official/<category>/<skill>` or `official/<category>/<sub>/<skill>`; browse filter `--source official`; repair/restore via `hermes skills repair-official <name> [--restore]` (`skills_hub.py:1477-1496`, delegates to `tools/skills_sync.py restore_official_optional_skill`).
- **Outputs / side effects:** A copy of the bundle under the active skills dir; the skill then participates in normal skill loading, `hermes skills list`, `/skills` slash command, trust/audit commands.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_HOME` (locates `optional-skills/` for packaged installs); skills config surface `hermes_cli/skills_config.py`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Installing an official optional skill prints the banner "This is an official optional skill maintained by Nous Research." (`skills_hub.py:761`) and uses scan source `"official"` (`skills_hub.py:705-706`). Per-source result cap for official is 100 (`skills_hub.py:919`). **Reconciliation finding:** the shipped tree has 137 `SKILL.md` files but `website/docs/reference/optional-skills-catalog.md` lists only 136 rows — `autonomous-ai-agents/agent-merge-conflict-arbiter` is present on disk and absent from the catalog doc.
- **Rebuild notes:** Ship skills as inert directories keyed by `<category>/<name>` with a frontmatter+Markdown contract; resolve them through the same search/install pipeline as remote skills but flag them `official` so they outrank community results. A better version would validate every bundled SKILL.md against a JSON Schema in CI and auto-generate the catalog doc from the tree so the two can never drift (as they have here by one row).

### Install an optional skill  `id: optional.skills-install`
- **Surface:** CLI
- **Where:** `hermes skills install official/<category>/<skill>` — help text: "Skill identifier (e.g. openai/skills/skill-creator) or a direct HTTP(S) URL to a SKILL.md file".
- **What it does:** Copies one bundled optional skill (or a remote skill) into the user's active skill set.
- **How it works:** `hermes_cli/skills_hub.py` resolves the identifier; an exact match with `source == "official"` short-circuits ambiguity and prints "Resolved to: <identifier> (official catalog)" (`skills_hub.py:73-77`). The bundle is scanned (scan source `official`), the user confirms, and files are copied into the active skills directory under the auto-detected category path.
- **Inputs / options:** positional `identifier`; `--category CATEGORY` (category folder to install into); `--name NAME` (override the skill name — useful when installing from a URL whose SKILL.md has no `name:` frontmatter); `--force` (install despite blocked scan verdict); `--yes`/`-y` (skip confirmation prompt; needed in TUI mode); `-h/--help`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** New skill directory on disk; skill becomes visible to the agent on next session.
- **Config / env:** `plugins.scan_on_install`-style scanning applies to skills through the skills scanner; `HERMES_HOME` decides destination.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Blocked scan verdicts require `--force`. Non-interactive/TUI needs `--yes`. Uninstall is `hermes skills uninstall <skill-name>`.
- **Rebuild notes:** Identifier → bundle resolution + malware scan + confirm + copy. A better version would install into a content-addressed store with a lockfile so upgrades and rollbacks are atomic.

### MCP catalog (`optional-mcps/`) — Nous-approved server manifests  `id: optional.mcp-catalog`
- **Surface:** Config
- **Where:** `optional-mcps/<name>/manifest.yaml`; listed by `hermes mcp catalog`, browsed in `hermes mcp picker`, installed by `hermes mcp install <name>` (or `official/<name>`). Every manifest opens with the comment "# Nous-approved MCP catalog entry.\n# Presence in this directory = approval. Merged via PR review."
- **What it does:** Ships 65 curated MCP server definitions that are disabled until installed; installing writes an `mcp_servers.<name>` block into `~/.hermes/config.yaml` and walks the user through credentials and tool selection.
- **How it works:** `hermes_cli/mcp_catalog.py`. `_catalog_root()` (`mcp_catalog.py:161`) resolves `optional-mcps/` via `hermes_constants.get_optional_mcps_dir()`. `_parse_manifest()` (`mcp_catalog.py:190`) validates: `manifest_version` must equal `1`; `name` must match `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$`; `description` required; `transport.type` ∈ {`stdio`,`http`} (stdio requires `command`, http requires `url`); `transport.args` list; `transport.env` string→string map (never secrets); `auth.type` ∈ {`api_key`,`oauth`,`none`}; `auth.env[]` entries `{name (identifier regex), prompt, required=true, secret=true, default}`; `tools.default_enabled` and `tools.default_excluded` are mutually exclusive lists of strings; `suggest.keywords`/`suggest.hosts` non-empty lowercase lists; `install.type` must be `git` with `url` + pinned `ref` + optional `bootstrap[]`. A hard rule at `mcp_catalog.py:270-283`: an `http` + `api_key` entry must declare the exact env key `_env_key_for_server(name)` in `auth.env`, or install would emit a literal-placeholder Authorization header. `list_catalog()` skips invalid manifests but records them in `catalog_diagnostics()` as `("<name>", "future_manifest"|"invalid", msg)` so the picker can say "your Hermes is out of date".
- **Inputs / options:** manifest fields as above; `${INSTALL_DIR}` placeholder substituted into `transport.command`/`transport.args` for git-installed servers (`mcp_catalog.py:57`, `_expand_install_dir`).
- **Outputs / side effects:** After install: `mcp_servers.<name>` in config.yaml (`command`/`args`/`env` for stdio; `url` + `auth: oauth` or `headers` bearer for http), plus `enabled: true|false` and a `tools.include`/`tools.exclude` filter; secrets in `~/.hermes/.env`; git servers cloned to `~/.hermes/mcp-installs/<name>`.
- **Config / env:** `mcp_servers.*` in `config.yaml`; secrets in `~/.hermes/.env`; `HERMES_HOME` for the install root.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Catalog policy (module docstring `mcp_catalog.py:1-25`): entries are added only by merging a PR — presence = approval, no community tier, no trust signals. Pins follow pyproject supply-chain rules — exact versions for `uvx pkg==X` / `npx pkg@X`, full commit SHAs for git, pinned release ≥ 2 weeks old at pin time. MCPs are **never auto-updated**; the user re-runs `hermes mcp install <name>` after a repo update. Non-secret env also goes to `.env` to keep one credential store. GitHub is intentionally excluded from the catalog and must not be suggested (its hosted MCP needs a per-host OAuth app; the bundled `github/*` gh-CLI skills are preferred) — `mcp_catalog.py:128-135`.
- **Rebuild notes:** A validated YAML manifest per server + a single installer that (1) optionally clones, (2) prompts declared env, (3) writes the client config block, (4) probes and filters tools, (5) prints post-install notes. A better version would sign manifests, record the resolved server `serverInfo.version` at install, and diff tool surfaces on re-install so surprise new tools are surfaced rather than silently auto-enabled.

### MCP catalog install flow  `id: optional.mcp-install-flow`
- **Surface:** CLI
- **Where:** `hermes mcp install <identifier>` — "Catalog entry name (or `official/<name>`)"; also reachable by toggling an entry in `hermes mcp picker`.
- **What it does:** Runs a catalog entry end-to-end: clone (if needed) → credentials → write server config → probe + choose tools → print post-install notes.
- **How it works:** `install_entry()` (`mcp_catalog.py:900-...`) in six steps documented in its docstring. Step 1 `_do_git_install()` wipes and re-clones `~/.hermes/mcp-installs/<name>`; it detects SHA-shaped refs (`^[0-9a-f]{7,40}$`) upfront because `git clone --branch` rejects SHAs, otherwise tries `git clone --depth 1 --branch <ref>` and falls back to full clone + `git checkout <ref>`; `noninteractive_git_env()` prevents credential-prompt hangs; `bootstrap` commands run through the shell in the clone dir with output streamed and any non-zero exit raising `CatalogError`. Step 2 `_prompt_env_vars()` prints "  ✓ <NAME> already set in .env" for existing values, otherwise prompts (password-masked when `secret: true`) and calls `save_env_value()`. Step 3 for `auth.type: oauth` with a `provider:` prints "This MCP uses <provider> OAuth. Run `hermes auth <provider>` ..."; without a provider prints "This MCP uses native OAuth 2.1; tokens will be acquired on first connection (browser flow)." Step 4 `_build_server_config()` + `_save_mcp_server()` (rejects "suspicious command/args configuration"). Step 5 `_apply_tool_selection()`. Step 6 prints "  ✓ Installed '<name>' (enabled|disabled). Start a new Hermes session to load its tools." then the manifest's `post_install` text, dimmed, line by line.
- **Inputs / options:** positional `identifier` (`official/` prefix stripped by `get_entry()`); `enable=True` keyword from the picker/CLI decides the `enabled` field.
- **Outputs / side effects:** config.yaml `mcp_servers.<name>`; `.env` secrets; clone dir; stdout transcript.
- **Config / env:** writes `mcp_servers.<name>.{command,args,env,url,auth,headers,enabled,tools}`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** `uninstall_entry(name, purge_install_dir=True)` deletes the config block and the clone. Re-install is destructive to the clone (fresh checkout each time, "manifest version is the source of truth") but *preserves* the user's tool filter (see next entry).
- **Rebuild notes:** Keep install idempotent and re-runnable; treat the manifest as the source of truth for transport, the user's config as the source of truth for filters.

### MCP tool selection — probe + curses checklist, include/exclude modes  `id: optional.mcp-tool-selection`
- **Surface:** CLI
- **Where:** During `hermes mcp install <name>`, and again any time via `hermes mcp configure <name>` ("Server name to configure").
- **What it does:** After installing a server, Hermes connects to it, lists its tools, and lets you check exactly which ones the agent may see — so a 3,000-tool server does not flood the context.
- **How it works:** `_apply_tool_selection()` (`mcp_catalog.py:717-...`). Exclude-mode short-circuit: if the manifest declares `tools.default_excluded` and there is no prior include list, the exclude list (tool names **or glob patterns**) is written verbatim with no probe at all — message "  Applied manifest exclude list (N entries); everything else stays enabled." A prior user-edited exclude list wins over the manifest ("  Kept your existing exclude list (N entries)."). Otherwise it prints "  Probing '<name>' for available tools..." and calls `_probe_single_server`. Probe failure priority: prior include selection → prior exclude list → manifest `default_enabled` → no filter, each with its own yellow message pointing at `hermes mcp configure <name>`. Probe success with zero tools writes no filter and says "  Server reported no tools." Otherwise the pre-checked set is prior selection → manifest `default_enabled` → all; non-TTY sessions apply the same priority without a UI. Interactive TTY shows `curses_checklist("Select tools for '<name>' (SPACE toggle, ENTER confirm)", labels, pre_indices)` where each label is `"<tool>  —  <description truncated to 60 chars + '...'>"`, preceded by "  Found N tool(s). Pre-checked: M."
- **Inputs / options:** SPACE toggles a row, ENTER confirms; checking everything clears the filter entirely; unchecking everything writes an empty `tools.include`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** `mcp_servers.<name>.tools.include: [...]` (writing include drops any `exclude`) or `mcp_servers.<name>.tools.exclude: [...]` (drops any `include`), or removal of the whole `tools` block. Messages: "  ✓ All N tools enabled (no filter — new tools the server adds later will be auto-enabled).", "  ✓ K/N tools enabled.", "  No tools selected. Run `hermes mcp configure <name>` to change."
- **Config / env:** `mcp_servers.<name>.tools.include`, `mcp_servers.<name>.tools.exclude`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** All-selected deliberately means *no filter*, so future server-side tools auto-enable; to pin, unselect one tool to force include-mode. Exclude-mode is intended for huge auto-generated (OpenAPI-derived) surfaces where an include list would be thousands of lines and freeze out new endpoints.
- **Rebuild notes:** Two filter modes (allow-list vs deny-list with globs), a probe with graceful degradation, and explicit precedence for the user's prior choice. A better version would diff the probed surface against the stored filter on every connect and prompt once when new tools appear.

### MCP composer suggestions ("brand pills")  `id: optional.mcp-suggest`
- **Surface:** Desktop app
- **Where:** Desktop composer — a suggestion to install a catalog entry appears while typing or after pasting a link.
- **What it does:** When your draft message contains a catalog entry's keyword as a whole word, or you paste a link whose hostname ends with one of its host suffixes, the UI may offer to install that MCP.
- **How it works:** `SuggestSpec` (`mcp_catalog.py:118-146`): `keywords` are lowercase whole-word/phrase triggers matched against the draft; `hosts` are hostname **suffixes** matched against pasted links. Parsing normalizes keywords to lowercase and strips a leading dot from hosts, so UIs match without re-normalizing. Purely advisory — the install itself always flows through the ordinary validated `install_entry()` path.
- **Inputs / options:** Per-manifest `suggest.keywords[]` and `suggest.hosts[]`. Examples shipped: `atlassian` → keywords `jira`, `confluence`, `atlassian`, `bitbucket`; hosts `atlassian.net`, `atlassian.com`, `jira.com`. `alltrails` → keywords `hiking`, `trail`, `alltrails`. `grafana` → keywords `grafana`, `promql`, `logql`, `observability`, `dashboard`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A UI affordance only; accepting runs the normal install.
- **Config / env:** n/a (manifest-declared).
- **Edge cases / guards:** `suggest` requires at least one keyword or host or the manifest is rejected. Entries with `suggest: null` (cloudflare, comfy-cloud, n8n, unreal-engine) never produce a pill.
- **Rebuild notes:** Keep suggestion metadata in the same manifest as the install definition; never let a suggestion bypass the consent/credential path.

### Community plugin index  `id: optional.plugin-index`
- **Surface:** CLI
- **Where:** `hermes plugins search [term]`; also used to resolve bare plugin names in `hermes plugins install <name>` and in pack entries.
- **What it does:** A static JSON index of community plugins, fetched from GitHub and cached locally, so users can discover plugins by name/description/tag and install them at a pinned ref.
- **How it works:** `hermes_cli/plugin_index.py`. Default URL `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NousResearch/hermes-plugin-index/main/index.json` (`plugin_index.py:29-31`), overridable via config key `plugins.index_url` (`get_index_url()`, `plugin_index.py:96-107`). Cache at `HERMES_HOME/cache/plugin_index.json` with TTL 24 h (`INDEX_CACHE_TTL`), written atomically. `load_index(refresh=False, offline=False)` order: fresh cache → remote → stale cache → bundled seed, returning `(entries, source)` with source ∈ {`remote`,`cache`,`seed`}. Fetch uses httpx, 10 s timeout, follow redirects, and refuses payloads over 5 MiB. Bundled seed at `hermes_cli/data/plugin_index.json` (`schema_version: 1`, `generated_at`, `plugins[]`). Entry fields: `name`, `description`, `author`, `tags[]`, `repo` ("owner/name", must contain exactly one `/`), `ref` (pinned tag or commit SHA), `subdir` (monorepo path), `homepage`, `capabilities[]`, `api_version`, `added_at`; `install_identifier` = `repo` or `repo/subdir`. Scoring (`_score_entry`): exact name 100, name substring 80, exact tag 70, tag substring 55, description substring 50, author substring 40, plus `difflib` name ratio × 60 when ratio ≥ 0.6 for typo tolerance; results sort by `(-score, name)`. `resolve_name()` returns a unique case-insensitive exact match, else the partial candidates (empty = nothing similar, >1 = ambiguous).
- **Inputs / options:** `hermes plugins search` flags — positional `term` (fuzzy over name/description/tags; omit to browse the whole index), `--json` (machine-readable), `--capability CAP` (filter by declared capability, e.g. `tools`, `platform`, `commands`), `--refresh` (bypass cache and re-fetch), `-h/--help`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Console table or JSON; writes/refreshes the cache file. Every listing carries the footer "Indexed ≠ audited: inclusion in the index is a metadata review only, not a code audit. Review a plugin before enabling it." (`SECURITY_FOOTER`, `plugin_index.py:42-46`).
- **Config / env:** `plugins.index_url`; `HERMES_HOME` for the cache.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Malformed entries (non-dict, missing name, `repo` without exactly one slash) are skipped with a debug log. Config-override lookup failures never break search. The index is discovery metadata only; install keeps its own consent/review flow and pins an immutable ref.
- **Rebuild notes:** Static JSON + TTL cache + offline seed is the whole design; the fuzzy scorer is 20 lines. A better version would carry per-entry signature/attestation and a "last verified against api_version" field so the client can hide plugins that cannot load.

### Seeded community plugin index entries  `id: optional.plugin-index-seed`
- **Surface:** CLI
- **Where:** Bundled file `hermes_cli/data/plugin_index.json`; visible as search results when offline.
- **What it does:** Provides the offline fallback list of community plugins and doubles as the machine-readable format reference.
- **How it works:** JSON document `{schema_version: 1, generated_at: "2026-08-12T00:00:00Z", plugins: [...]}`. Shipped entries include: **hermes-media-studio** (NousResearch, tags media/image-gen/video-gen/dashboard/desktop, repo `NousResearch/hermes-media-studio`, ref `e8d59971d2b7901405b39dac7b03bdd616272d0d`, capabilities `tools`,`dashboard`, api_version 1) — "Media Studio — generative media workspace plugin for Hermes Desktop (fal + Krea, durable job queue, library)."; **hermes-telegram-business** (repo `NousResearch/hermes-telegram-business`, ref `e905f3bc5eeaa5a9dab9bc5155601b3ebec75757`, capabilities `platform`) — "Observe-with-approval Telegram Business Mode (secretary bot) plugin — every drafted reply requires owner approval before it reaches the customer."; **plugin-llm-example** (repo `NousResearch/hermes-example-plugins`, subdir `plugin-llm-example`, ref `38fe0fb53eff98d477f807432e965429e665ca33`, capabilities `commands`,`llm`) — "Reference plugin showing host-owned structured LLM access via ctx.llm.complete_structured(). Registers a /receipt-extract slash command."; **plugin-llm-async-example** (same repo/ref, subdir `plugin-llm-async-example`) — "Reference plugin demonstrating async host-owned LLM access from plugin code — the asyncio counterpart to plugin-llm-example."; **hermes-plugin-chrome-profiles** (author anpicasso, tags browser/chrome/cdp/tools) — "Switch Hermes browser tools between Chrome profiles via CDP."
- **Inputs / options:** n/a (data file).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Read-only fallback.
- **Config / env:** n/a.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Unreadable seed logs a warning and yields an empty list.
- **Rebuild notes:** Ship the seed as the same schema as the remote index so one parser serves both.

### Plugin packs — `hermes plugins pack`  `id: optional.plugin-packs`
- **Surface:** CLI
- **Where:** `hermes plugins pack {install,export,show}` — group help: "Install, export, or inspect plugin packs — a single YAML file pinning a set of plugins to exact commit SHAs, with optional non-secret config seeds. Installing a pack fans out to ordinary pinned installs; capability consent stays per-plugin."
- **What it does:** Lets one YAML file (`hermes-pack.yaml`) describe a reproducible set of plugins plus non-secret config seeds, so a whole setup can be shared, reviewed, installed, and re-exported.
- **How it works:** `hermes_cli/plugin_packs.py`. `parse_pack()` accepts either a top-level mapping or the nested `pack: {…}` sugar; requires `name` and a non-empty `plugins` list; each entry needs `name` (community-index name) **or** `repo`/`source` (`github:` prefix stripped, `owner/repo` or git URL), an optional `subdir`, and a `ref` (aliased `version`) that must match `^[0-9a-fA-F]{40}$` — tags and branches are rejected with "refs must be exact 40-character commit SHAs (tags and branch names are rejected — pin the commit for reproducible installs)." `config:` maps plugin id → `plugins.entries.<id>` seeds, validated by `validate_config_seed()` which rejects non-mappings, the reserved keys `granted_capabilities`/`capabilities_consent`, any `allow_*` key ("packs cannot pre-grant capabilities or trust gates"), and any secret-shaped key matching `(?i)(token|secret|passw(or)?d|api[_-]?key|private[_-]?key|credential|auth)` ("secrets never travel in packs. Declare the secret in the plugin's requires_env instead"). `skills:` is parsed and displayed but **not installed** — a documented seam. `load_pack()` accepts a local path or an `https://` URL (other schemes rejected: "Pack URLs must use https:// (or pass a local file path)."), with a 1 MiB cap and 15 s fetch timeout. `resolve_pack_plugins()` turns bare names into `owner/repo` through the community index, carrying per-entry `resolve_error` ("not found in the community index" / "ambiguous in the community index" / "community index unavailable: …") instead of raising. `render_pack_review()` prints a rich table with columns **Plugin | Source | Pinned ref | Capabilities (declared)** (ref truncated to 12 chars, capabilities shown as "(shown at install)" when unknown), then "Config seeds (plugins.entries.<id>, non-secret):" lines, then a yellow "Pack lists skills (NOT auto-installed yet): …" with the dim hint "Install them manually, e.g. `hermes skills install <id>`.", then the footer "Installing a pack runs third-party code × N plugins. Each plugin's declared capabilities still require individual consent after install — a pack never bulk-grants." `install_pack_plugins()` fans out to `_install_plugin_core(identifier, force, ref)`, then `_prompt_plugin_env_vars(manifest)`, then enables the plugin (adds to enabled set, removes from disabled), then runs `_run_capability_consent(console, name, declared, context="install")` — the exact same per-plugin flow as a single install — then seeds config (existing user values always win: "  plugins.entries.<id>.<key> already set — keeping your value." / "  Seeded plugins.entries.<id> from pack."). `export_pack()` reads install metadata, keeps only plugins with real Git provenance and a 40-char revision, sanitizes each `plugins.entries.<id>` block (drops reserved/`allow_*`/secret-shaped keys, keeps scalars/lists/dicts) and emits YAML; plugins without provenance become `# WARNING (not exported): <id>: no Git provenance (local-only or pre-metadata install) — listed as a comment, not installable from this pack.`
- **Inputs / options:** `pack show <path-or-url>` — dry-run parse + review, prints "N entr(y|ies) could not be resolved — install would skip them and exit non-zero." and "Dry run only. Install with `hermes plugins pack install ...`."; `pack install <path-or-url> [--force]` — mandatory review then the prompt "Install N plugin(s) from pack '<name>'? [y/N] "; `pack export [--enabled-only] [--name NAME]` — YAML on stdout, warnings on stderr, default pack name `my-hermes-pack`. Pack YAML keys: `name`, `description`, `author`, `version`, `plugins[].{name,repo|source,ref|version,subdir}`, `config.<plugin-id>.<key>`, `skills[]`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** N plugin installs under the plugins dir + install metadata; `plugins.enabled`/`plugins.disabled` updated; `plugins.entries.<id>` seeded; summary "Pack '<name>': K installed, M failed."; on success the hint "Restart the gateway for the plugins to take effect:" / "  hermes gateway restart"; exits non-zero if any plugin failed.
- **Config / env:** `plugins.entries.<id>.*`, `plugins.enabled`, `plugins.disabled`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** No `--yes` in v1 — a non-interactive session is refused: "Error: Pack install requires an interactive terminal to review and confirm the pack contents (no --yes in v1)." Answering anything but `y`/`yes` prints "Aborted — nothing installed." and exits 1. Per-plugin failures never abort the fan-out. Unknown `pack` action prints "Error: Usage: hermes plugins pack {install|export|show}".
- **Rebuild notes:** Pack = manifest of `(source, exact SHA)` pairs + non-secret seeds; installation is *only* a loop over the existing single-install path. The invariants that matter: exact SHAs, no secrets, no bulk capability grants, user config always wins. A better version would also lock skills and MCP catalog entries in the same file and produce a machine-verifiable install receipt.

### Plugin capability consent  `id: optional.plugin-capabilities`
- **Surface:** CLI
- **Where:** `hermes plugins capabilities [name]` — "Show each plugin's declared capabilities (from plugin.yaml) against what the user has granted. Capabilities are a consent and audit layer over host API surfaces — NOT a sandbox."
- **What it does:** Shows, per plugin, which host-API capabilities it declares and which the user has actually granted, and is the flow that runs right after any install (single or via pack).
- **How it works:** Declared capabilities come from the plugin's `plugin.yaml`; granted state is stored under `plugins.entries.<id>.granted_capabilities` / `capabilities_consent` (both reserved keys that packs may never set — `plugin_packs.py:57-60`). `hermes_cli/plugin_capabilities.py` (393 lines) defines the capability vocabulary; `hermes_cli/plugins_cmd.py:_run_capability_consent(console, plugin_id, declared, context=…)` runs the interactive grant. Capability names appear in the index as `capabilities: ["tools", "dashboard", "platform", "commands", "llm"]` and are filterable via `hermes plugins search --capability CAP`.
- **Inputs / options:** optional positional `name` (omit to list all plugins with capabilities); `-h/--help`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Console table of declared vs granted; consent writes the granted set into config.
- **Config / env:** `plugins.entries.<id>.granted_capabilities`, `plugins.entries.<id>.capabilities_consent`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Explicitly **not a sandbox** — a consent and audit layer only. Deprecated `allow_*` trust gates are still recognised as reserved keys and refused in pack seeds.
- **Rebuild notes:** Declare capabilities in the manifest, ask once at install, record the grant next to the plugin's config, and make the grant impossible to set by any non-interactive path.

### Plugin lifecycle commands (index-aware)  `id: optional.plugins-lifecycle`
- **Surface:** CLI
- **Where:** `hermes plugins <sub>` — group help: "Install, update, remove, list, or validate native Hermes plugins and portable Agent Plugins v1 packages. Portable packages install disabled."
- **What it does:** Installs, enables, disables, updates, inspects and validates plugins — including the bundled first-party ones documented in section B.
- **How it works:** Dispatch in `hermes_cli/plugins_cmd.py` (3178 lines) over the loader in `hermes_cli/plugins.py` (7182 lines). Enabled/disabled sets live in config (`plugins.enabled`, `plugins.disabled`; read at `plugins.py:643` and `plugins_cmd.py:1266`). Post-install scanning is gated by `plugins.scan_on_install` (default true, `plugins_cmd.py:96`).
- **Inputs / options:** Sub-commands exactly as listed by `--help`: `install`, `search`, `update` ("Pull latest changes for an installed plugin"), `remove` (aliases `rm`, `uninstall`), `list` (alias `ls`), `enable` ("Enable a disabled plugin"), `disable` ("Disable a plugin without removing it"), `capabilities`, `doctor` ("Validate a plugin with the real runtime contracts"), `pack`, `show` (alias `info`, "Show details for a single plugin (including emits/listens)"). `install` flags: positional `identifier` ("Git URL, owner/repo shorthand (e.g. anpicasso/hermes-plugin-chrome-profiles), or a bare plugin name resolved through the community index (see `hermes plugins search`)"), `--force`/`-f` ("Remove existing plugin and reinstall"), `--ref COMMIT_SHA` ("Install exactly one immutable 40-character Git commit SHA"), `--enable` ("Auto-enable the plugin after install (skip confirmation prompt)"), `--no-enable` ("Install disabled (skip confirmation prompt); enable later with `hermes plugins enable <name>`").
- **Outputs / side effects:** Plugin directories, install metadata (source + revision), config enable/disable lists, capability grants.
- **Config / env:** `plugins.enabled`, `plugins.disabled`, `plugins.entries.*`, `plugins.index_url`, `plugins.scan_on_install`, `plugins.hook_callback_timeout` (default 30 s), `plugins.security`, `plugins.memories`, `plugins.portal`, `plugins.project`, `plugins.honcho`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Portable "Agent Plugins v1" packages always install **disabled**. `--enable`/`--no-enable` are mutually exclusive.
- **Rebuild notes:** Keep the enabled set in config rather than on disk so a plugin directory can exist without running.

### Bundled plugin manifest (`plugin.yaml`) for first-party plugins  `id: optional.plugin-manifest`
- **Surface:** Config
- **Where:** `plugins/<group>/<name>/plugin.yaml` (grouped backends) or `plugins/<name>/plugin.yaml` (standalones).
- **What it does:** Declares each bundled plugin's identity, kind, dependencies, hooks and what it provides, so the loader can register it without importing the code first.
- **How it works:** Observed field set across the 40 first-party manifests: `name`, `version`, `description`, `author`, `kind` (`backend` | `standalone`), `platforms[]` (`linux`/`macos`/`windows`), `requires_env[]`, `pip_dependencies[]`, `external_dependencies[]` (`{name, install, check}`), `hooks[]`, `provides_tools[]`, `provides_web_providers[]`, `provides_browser_providers[]`. Grouped directories (`image_gen/`, `video_gen/`, `web/`, `browser/`, `memory/`, `dashboard_auth/`, `cron_providers/`, `observability/`) namespace their children; standalone plugins sit flat.
- **Inputs / options:** the fields above.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Drives `hermes plugins list`, `show`, `capabilities`, `doctor`, and provider registries.
- **Config / env:** referenced env names are prompted at install (`requires_env`).
- **Edge cases / guards:** `platforms` omits Windows for `google_meet` (linux/macos only); `teams_pipeline` lists all three.
- **Rebuild notes:** One declarative manifest per plugin, read without executing plugin code, is what makes listing/validating cheap.

### Dashboard plugin manifest (`manifest.json`)  `id: optional.dashboard-plugin-manifest`
- **Surface:** Web dashboard
- **Where:** `plugins/<name>/dashboard/manifest.json` (shipped by `kanban` and `hermes-achievements`).
- **What it does:** Declares a plugin's dashboard tab: its route, label, icon, position, bundled JS/CSS, and backend API module.
- **How it works:** Fields: `name`, `label` (tab text), `description`, `icon` (lucide icon name), `version`, `tab: {path, position}` where position uses the form `after:<existing-tab>`, `entry` (`dist/index.js`), `css` (`dist/style.css`), `api` (`plugin_api.py`). The dashboard mounts the API module's `APIRouter` at `/api/plugins/<name>/`; routes go through `web_server.auth_middleware` exactly like core routes (session bearer token, or the session cookie set when loading the dashboard HTML; the token is injected into pages as `window.__HERMES_SESSION_TOKEN__`). WebSocket endpoints take the token as a `?token=` query parameter because browsers cannot set `Authorization` on an upgrade request (`plugins/kanban/dashboard/plugin_api.py:1-35`).
- **Inputs / options:** shipped values — kanban: `label "Kanban"`, icon `Package`, path `/kanban`, position `after:skills`, version 1.0.0; hermes-achievements: `label "Achievements"`, icon `Star`, path `/achievements`, position `after:analytics`, version 0.4.0.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A new dashboard tab plus a mounted API namespace.
- **Config / env:** n/a.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Because plugin routes share the dashboard auth middleware, `hermes dashboard --host 0.0.0.0` does not expose them unauthenticated; auth is still single-user (anyone with the URL+token has full access).
- **Rebuild notes:** A tiny JSON contract (route + bundle + api module) is enough to make third-party dashboard tabs first-class; put them behind the same auth middleware from day one.

---

## B. First-party plugins shipped in `plugins/`

(Platform adapters `plugins/platforms/*` and model providers `plugins/model-providers/*` belong to sibling shards; everything else in `plugins/` is below.)

### Per-plugin persistent storage convention  `id: optional.plugin-storage`
- **Surface:** Core
- **Where:** `plugins/plugin_storage.py`; data lands in `<HERMES_HOME>/plugin-data/<plugin-name>/`.
- **What it does:** Gives every plugin one durable, user-owned data directory (and an optional SQLite DB) that survives `hermes plugins update` and `hermes plugins remove`.
- **How it works:** `plugin_data_dir(name)` returns `<hermes home>/plugin-data/<name>/`; `plugin_db(name)` opens `<data dir>/data.db` via `sqlite3`. The module docstring states the rationale: the plugin *install* dir `<hermes home>/plugins/<name>/` is deleted by `remove` and git-pulled into by `update`, so state parked there dies with the code.
- **Inputs / options:** `plugin_data_dir(name) -> Path`, `plugin_db(name) -> sqlite3.Connection`; the name is sanitized by a regex before use.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Creates the directory / DB file on first use.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_HOME`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Secrets are deliberately excluded from this convention — credentials go through `agent.secret_scope` / `.env`.
- **Rebuild notes:** Separate code dir from data dir and hand plugins a helper, or they will all pick the wrong one.

### Plugin concurrency helpers  `id: optional.plugin-utils`
- **Surface:** Core
- **Where:** `plugins/plugin_utils.py` (135 lines), imported by plugin authors.
- **What it does:** Provides two thread-safe lazy-initialization primitives so plugins do not hand-roll broken double-checked locking.
- **How it works:** `lazy_singleton` — decorator for a zero-argument accessor; `SingletonSlot` — manual slot for accessors that build different instances per config/key. Stdlib `threading` only, so importing them pulls in no host modules. The docstring documents the reachable race: multi-threaded agent sessions (delegated tool calls, background workers, the self-improvement fork) share one process.
- **Inputs / options:** `@lazy_singleton` over a `def get_client()`; `SingletonSlot().get(key, factory)`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** none.
- **Config / env:** n/a.
- **Edge cases / guards:** n/a.
- **Rebuild notes:** Two dozen lines that prevent a whole class of resource leaks.

### Context engine plugin slot  `id: optional.plugin-context-engine`
- **Surface:** Config
- **Where:** `plugins/context_engine/<name>/` — selected via `context.engine` in `config.yaml` (default `"compressor"`).
- **What it does:** Defines a pluggable slot where exactly one alternative context-management engine can replace the built-in ContextCompressor.
- **How it works:** `plugins/context_engine/__init__.py` (285 lines). `discover_context_engines()` scans immediate subdirectories (skipping names starting with `_` or `.`), requires an `__init__.py`, reads `description` from an optional `plugin.yaml` **without importing** the engine, then does a lightweight availability probe by loading the engine and calling `is_available()` if present; returns `[(name, description, is_available)]`. `load_context_engine(name)` returns a `ContextEngine` instance. Engines live in-repo and are "always available without user installation"; only ONE can be active at a time.
- **Inputs / options:** `context.engine = "compressor" | "<plugin dir name>"`; the docstring's example is `load_context_engine("lcm")`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Swaps the agent's context-compression implementation.
- **Config / env:** `context.engine` (default `"compressor"`).
- **Edge cases / guards:** **v2026.8.31 ships zero engine subdirectories** — `plugins/context_engine/` contains only `__init__.py`, so the slot exists but the built-in compressor is the only choice until a user or third party drops an engine directory in.
- **Rebuild notes:** Discovery-without-import plus an `is_available()` probe is the pattern; a better version would let more than one engine coexist and pick per profile.

### Browser backend: Browser Use  `id: optional.plugin-browser-browser-use`
- **Surface:** Provider
- **Where:** `plugins/browser/browser_use/plugin.yaml` (`name: browser-browser-use`, v1.0.0, `kind: backend`, `provides_browser_providers: [browser-use]`); selected via `browser.backend`.
- **What it does:** Routes Hermes's browser tools to the Browser Use cloud browser.
- **How it works:** Registers the `browser-use` browser provider. Manifest description: "Browser Use (https://browser-use.com) cloud browser backend. Supports both direct BROWSER_USE_API_KEY and the managed Nous tool gateway. Also powers the 'Nous Subscription' UX flow that bills usage to a Nous subscription." Provider implementation in `plugins/browser/browser_use/provider.py`.
- **Inputs / options:** `browser.backend = "browser-use"`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Remote browser sessions; usage billed to the API key or the Nous subscription.
- **Config / env:** `BROWSER_USE_API_KEY`; shared browser knobs `browser.inactivity_timeout` (120), `browser.command_timeout` (30), `browser.snapshot_threshold` (15000), `browser.record_sessions`, `browser.headed`, `browser.allow_private_urls`, `browser.engine` ("auto"), `browser.dialog_policy` ("must_respond"), `browser.dialog_timeout_s` (300).
- **Edge cases / guards:** Two credential paths (direct key vs managed gateway).
- **Rebuild notes:** `provides_browser_providers` in the manifest + a `provider.py` implementing the browser interface is the entire contract.

### Browser backend: Browserbase  `id: optional.plugin-browser-browserbase`
- **Surface:** Provider
- **Where:** `plugins/browser/browserbase/plugin.yaml` (`name: browser-browserbase`, v1.0.0, backend, provides `browserbase`).
- **What it does:** Routes browser tools to Browserbase cloud browsers with stealth, proxies and keep-alive sessions.
- **How it works:** Manifest description: "Browserbase (https://browserbase.com) cloud browser backend. Requires BROWSERBASE_API_KEY + BROWSERBASE_PROJECT_ID. Supports stealth, proxies, and keep-alive sessions; auto-falls-back when paid features are unavailable."
- **Inputs / options:** `browser.backend = "browserbase"`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Remote sessions in the Browserbase project.
- **Config / env:** `BROWSERBASE_API_KEY`, `BROWSERBASE_PROJECT_ID`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Auto-falls-back when paid features (stealth/proxy) are unavailable on the plan.
- **Rebuild notes:** Degrade gracefully rather than erroring when a plan lacks a feature.

### Browser backend: Firecrawl  `id: optional.plugin-browser-firecrawl`
- **Surface:** Provider
- **Where:** `plugins/browser/firecrawl/plugin.yaml` (`name: browser-firecrawl`, v1.0.0, backend, provides `firecrawl`).
- **What it does:** Routes browser tools to Firecrawl's cloud browsing endpoints.
- **How it works:** Manifest description: "Firecrawl (https://firecrawl.dev) cloud browser backend. Requires FIRECRAWL_API_KEY. Distinct from the firecrawl WEB search/extract plugin — the two share an API key but operate on different endpoints."
- **Inputs / options:** `browser.backend = "firecrawl"`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Remote page loads via Firecrawl.
- **Config / env:** `FIRECRAWL_API_KEY`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Do not confuse with `web/firecrawl` (search/extract) — same key, different endpoints.
- **Rebuild notes:** Namespacing providers by capability (`browser-` vs `web-`) avoids exactly this collision.

### Cron provider: Chronos (NAS-mediated managed cron)  `id: optional.plugin-cron-chronos`
- **Surface:** Provider
- **Where:** `plugins/cron_providers/chronos/` (`plugin.yaml` name `chronos`, v1.0.0, author Nous Research); activated by `cron.provider = "chronos"`.
- **What it does:** Lets a scale-to-zero hosted agent still fire cron jobs by delegating the "wake me at time T" trigger to Nous infrastructure, which calls the agent back over an authenticated webhook.
- **How it works:** Manifest description: "Chronos — NAS-mediated managed cron provider for scale-to-zero hosted agents. Delegates the 'wake me at time T' trigger to Nous infrastructure so an idle gateway can scale to zero and still fire cron jobs. The agent computes each job's next-fire time and asks NAS to arm a one-shot; NAS calls the agent back at fire time over an authenticated webhook. Inert unless cron.provider=chronos." Files: `_nas_client.py` (arm/disarm one-shots against NAS), `verify.py` (inbound fire-token verification). `verify.py` verifies a NAS-minted short-lived JWT on `POST /api/cron/fire` **before any job runs** — the security boundary for remotely-triggered execution. It requires the purpose claim `cron_fire` so a general agent JWT cannot be replayed against the fire endpoint. Crypto is delegated to PyJWT. The verifier is pluggable via `get_fire_verifier()` so a direct per-job cron-key escape hatch can swap in later. A process-wide cache of `PyJWKClient` instances keyed by JWKS URL exists because a fresh client per fire forced a synchronous JWKS GET on every fire, which under bursts fanned out into simultaneous fetches, got rate-limited (HTTP 403), and made the agent answer 401 — and when merely slow, blocked the event loop past the 202 deadline.
- **Inputs / options:** n/a (no user-facing sub-commands of its own; driven by the cron subsystem).
- **Outputs / side effects:** One-shot arms registered with NAS; inbound webhook fires that start cron jobs.
- **Config / env:** `cron.provider` (default `""`), `cron.chronos.portal_url` (default `https://portal.nousresearch.com`), `cron.chronos.callback_url`, `cron.chronos.expected_audience`, `cron.chronos.nas_jwks_url`; general cron keys `cron.allow_agent_scheduling` (false), `cron.preflight` (true), `cron.model_drift_guard` (true), `cron.model`, `cron.model_provider`, `cron.wrap_response` (true), `cron.mirror_delivery` (false), `cron.max_parallel_jobs`, `cron.output_retention` (50), `cron.script_timeout_seconds` (3600), `cron.session_db_timeout_seconds` (10), `cron.media_send_timeout_seconds` (300).
- **Edge cases / guards:** Completely inert unless `cron.provider=chronos`. Tokens without the `cron_fire` purpose claim are rejected.
- **Rebuild notes:** Delegate only the *trigger* to the cloud, keep job definitions and execution local, and gate the callback on a purpose-scoped JWT. A better version would sign the arm request too, so a compromised NAS cannot invent jobs.

### Dashboard auth provider: basic (username/password)  `id: optional.plugin-dashboard-auth-basic`
- **Surface:** Web dashboard
- **Where:** `plugins/dashboard_auth/basic/plugin.yaml` (`name: basic`, v1.0.0, backend, `requires_env: [HERMES_DASHBOARD_BASIC_AUTH_USERNAME]`).
- **What it does:** Puts a plain username/password login in front of the dashboard for self-hosters who do not want an OAuth IdP.
- **How it works:** Manifest description: "Dashboard auth provider — username/password (no OAuth IDP). A self-hosted 'just put a password on my dashboard' provider. Activates when dashboard.basic_auth.username plus a password (or password_hash) are configured via config.yaml (canonical surface) or the HERMES_DASHBOARD_BASIC_AUTH_* env vars. Sessions are stateless HMAC-signed tokens minted by the provider; password hashing uses stdlib scrypt (no third-party dependency). Set dashboard.basic_auth.secret for restart-surviving / multi-worker sessions."
- **Inputs / options:** `dashboard.basic_auth.username`, `dashboard.basic_auth.password`, `dashboard.basic_auth.password_hash`, `dashboard.basic_auth.secret`, `dashboard.basic_auth.session_ttl_seconds` (default 0).
- **Outputs / side effects:** HMAC-signed session tokens; dashboard requires login.
- **Config / env:** the keys above plus `HERMES_DASHBOARD_BASIC_AUTH_USERNAME` and siblings.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Without `dashboard.basic_auth.secret` sessions do not survive restart and break across workers.
- **Rebuild notes:** scrypt from stdlib + stateless HMAC sessions is a dependency-free way to add a password gate.

### Dashboard auth provider: drain (shared bearer secret)  `id: optional.plugin-dashboard-auth-drain`
- **Surface:** Web dashboard
- **Where:** `plugins/dashboard_auth/drain/plugin.yaml` (`name: drain`, v1.0.0, backend, `requires_env: [HERMES_DASHBOARD_DRAIN_SECRET]`); protects `/api/gateway/drain`.
- **What it does:** Lets provisioning infrastructure call the gateway's drain-control endpoint non-interactively with a shared bearer secret.
- **How it works:** Manifest description: "Dashboard auth provider — non-interactive shared-bearer-secret for the gateway drain-control endpoint. The first consumer of the generic token-auth capability (supports_token/verify_token). nous-account-service provisions a per-agent unique secret via HERMES_DASHBOARD_DRAIN_SECRET; this provider verifies an inbound Authorization bearer token against it with a constant-time compare and registers /api/gateway/drain as token-authable. Fails CLOSED: a weak/short/low-entropy secret (< 256 bits) is rejected at registration and the endpoint stays disabled. No-op when the env var is unset."
- **Inputs / options:** `dashboard.drain_auth.scope` (default `"drain"`), `dashboard.drain_auth.min_secret_chars` (default 43).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Registers one endpoint as token-authable.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_DASHBOARD_DRAIN_SECRET`; `dashboard.drain_auth.*`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Fails closed on a weak secret (< 256 bits ≈ 43 base64 chars); no-op when the env var is unset; constant-time comparison.
- **Rebuild notes:** A `supports_token`/`verify_token` capability on the auth-provider interface makes machine callers a first-class case without weakening the browser path.

### Dashboard auth provider: Nous Portal OAuth  `id: optional.plugin-dashboard-auth-nous`
- **Surface:** Web dashboard
- **Where:** `plugins/dashboard_auth/nous/plugin.yaml` (`name: nous`, v1.0.0, backend, `requires_env: [HERMES_DASHBOARD_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID]`).
- **What it does:** Signs dashboard users in through Nous Portal with OAuth 2.0 authorization-code + PKCE.
- **How it works:** Manifest description: "Dashboard auth provider — OAuth 2.0 (authorization-code + PKCE) against Nous Portal. Auto-activates when a client_id is configured via either dashboard.oauth.client_id in config.yaml (canonical surface) or HERMES_DASHBOARD_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID env var (operator override; Portal injects this at Fly.io provisioning). dashboard.oauth.portal_url / HERMES_DASHBOARD_PORTAL_URL are optional and default to https://portal.nousresearch.com."
- **Inputs / options:** `dashboard.oauth.client_id`, `dashboard.oauth.portal_url`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Browser OAuth redirect + session.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_DASHBOARD_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`, `HERMES_DASHBOARD_PORTAL_URL`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Auto-activates purely on the presence of a client_id; env overrides config.
- **Rebuild notes:** Config as canonical surface with an env override is the pattern all four providers follow.

### Dashboard auth provider: generic self-hosted OIDC  `id: optional.plugin-dashboard-auth-self-hosted`
- **Surface:** Web dashboard
- **Where:** `plugins/dashboard_auth/self_hosted/plugin.yaml` (`name: self-hosted`, v1.0.0, backend, `requires_env: [HERMES_DASHBOARD_OIDC_ISSUER, HERMES_DASHBOARD_OIDC_CLIENT_ID]`).
- **What it does:** Signs dashboard users in through any conformant OpenID Connect provider.
- **How it works:** Manifest description: "Dashboard auth provider — generic self-hosted OpenID Connect (authorization-code + PKCE, public client). Works against any conformant OIDC identity provider (Authentik, Keycloak, Zitadel, Authelia, Auth0, Okta, Google, …) via OIDC discovery. Auto-activates when an issuer + client_id are configured, either under dashboard.oauth.self_hosted.{issuer,client_id} in config.yaml (canonical surface) or via the HERMES_DASHBOARD_OIDC_ISSUER + HERMES_DASHBOARD_OIDC_CLIENT_ID env vars (operator override / secret injection). Scopes default to 'openid profile email'. Verifies the OIDC ID token (RS256/ES256) against the discovered jwks_uri."
- **Inputs / options:** `dashboard.oauth.self_hosted.issuer`, `dashboard.oauth.self_hosted.client_id`, scope override.
- **Outputs / side effects:** OIDC login session.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_DASHBOARD_OIDC_ISSUER`, `HERMES_DASHBOARD_OIDC_CLIENT_ID`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Public client (no secret); ID token signature verified against the discovered JWKS; RS256/ES256 only.
- **Rebuild notes:** Discovery-driven OIDC covers every self-hosted IdP with one provider.

### disk-cleanup  `id: optional.plugin-disk-cleanup`
- **Surface:** Gateway/Telegram
- **Where:** `plugins/disk-cleanup/` (`plugin.yaml` v2.0.0, author "@LVT382009 (original), NousResearch (plugin port)"); slash command `/disk-cleanup`.
- **What it does:** Automatically tracks ephemeral files the agent creates (test scripts, temp outputs, cron logs, stale Chrome profiles) and deletes them on a schedule, without the agent having to remember to call a tool.
- **How it works:** Two hooks (`hooks: [post_tool_call, on_session_end]`). `post_tool_call`: when `write_file` / `terminal` / `patch` creates a file matching `test_*`, `tmp_*` or `*.test.*` inside `HERMES_HOME`, it is silently tracked as category `test` / `temp` / `cron-output`. `on_session_end`: if any test files were auto-tracked this turn, run `quick` cleanup with no prompts. Deletion rules — `test`: every session end, never confirmed; `temp`: >7 days since tracked, never confirmed; `cron-output`: >14 days, never confirmed; empty dirs under HERMES_HOME: always, never confirmed; `research`: >30 days and beyond the 10 newest, always confirmed (deep only); `chrome-profile`: >14 days, always confirmed (deep only); files >500 MB: never auto, always confirmed (deep only). State lives in `$HERMES_HOME/disk-cleanup/tracked.json`.
- **Inputs / options:** `/disk-cleanup status` (breakdown + top-10 largest), `/disk-cleanup dry-run` (preview without deleting), `/disk-cleanup quick` (run safe cleanup now), `/disk-cleanup deep` (quick + list items needing prompt), `/disk-cleanup track <path> <category>` (manual tracking), `/disk-cleanup forget <path>` (stop tracking).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Files deleted from disk; `tracked.json` updated; a status report in chat.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_HOME`; plugin enabled via `plugins.enabled`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** `is_safe_path()` rejects anything outside `HERMES_HOME` or `/tmp/hermes-*`; Windows mounts (`/mnt/c` etc.) rejected; the state dir `$HERMES_HOME/disk-cleanup/` excludes itself; `$HERMES_HOME/logs/`, `memories/`, `sessions/`, `skills/`, `plugins/` and config files are never tracked; backup/restore is scoped to `tracked.json` — the plugin never touches agent logs.
- **Rebuild notes:** Track-on-write + age-bucketed deletion + a hard path allowlist. A better version would compute reclaimable bytes before asking and offer per-category retention config.

### google_meet  `id: optional.plugin-google-meet`
- **Surface:** Tool
- **Where:** `plugins/google_meet/` (`plugin.yaml` v0.2.0, `kind: standalone`, `platforms: [linux, macos]`); CLI `hermes meet <sub>`; tools `meet_join`, `meet_leave`, `meet_status`, `meet_transcript`, `meet_say`; hook `on_session_end`.
- **What it does:** Joins a Google Meet call in a headless Chromium, scrapes the live captions into a transcript, optionally speaks into the call in realtime, and can run the bot on a different machine than the gateway.
- **How it works:** Manifest description: "Join a Google Meet call, transcribe live captions, speak in realtime, and follow up afterwards. v1 transcribe-only is the default; v2 realtime duplex audio via OpenAI Realtime + BlackHole/PulseAudio ships with mode='realtime'; v3 remote node host lets the bot run on a different machine than the gateway (gateway on Linux, Chrome+signed-in profile on the user's Mac). Explicit-by-design: only joins meet.google.com URLs passed in — no calendar scanning, no auto-dial." Files: `meet_bot.py` (Playwright bot), `process_manager.py` (subprocess lifecycle), `audio_bridge.py` (virtual audio device), `realtime/openai_client.py` (OpenAI Realtime session), `node/{server,client,protocol,registry,cli}.py` (remote node host), `tools.py` (tool schemas + handlers), `cli.py`, `SKILL.md`, `README.md`. `meet_join` spawns a headless Chromium subprocess that runs in parallel with the agent loop and returns immediately; `meet_say` queues text to the bot's OpenAI Realtime session and streams the generated audio into Chrome's fake microphone via a virtual audio device (PulseAudio null-sink on Linux, BlackHole on macOS), returning immediately with a couple of seconds of speech lag.
- **Inputs / options:** **Tools** — `meet_join{url (required, full https://meet.google.com/... URL), mode ∈ {transcribe (default, listen-only), realtime}, guest_name (default "Hermes Agent"), duration (e.g. "30m","2h","90s"; omit to stay until meet_leave), headed (bool, debug only, default false), node (registered remote node name, or "auto" for the sole registered node; default local)}`; `meet_status{node}`; `meet_transcript{last (int ≥1), node}`; `meet_leave{node}`; `meet_say{text (required), node}`. **CLI** `hermes meet` — `setup` ("Preflight: playwright, chromium, auth"); `install` ("Install prerequisites (pip deps, Chromium, platform audio tools)") with `--realtime` ("Also install realtime audio tools (pulseaudio-utils on Linux, BlackHole+ffmpeg on macOS). Uses sudo/brew, prompts before invoking either.") and `--yes`/`-y` ("Answer yes to all prompts (use with care; will run sudo apt-get or brew without asking)."); `auth` ("Sign in to Google and save session state"); `join <url>` with `--guest-name` (default "Hermes Agent"), `--duration`, `--headed`, `--mode {transcribe,realtime}`, `--node`; `status`; `transcript [--last N]`; `say <text> [--node]`; `stop`; `node` sub-group — `run [--host 0.0.0.0] [--port 18789] [--display-name hermes-meet-node]`, `list`, `approve <name> <url> <token>`, `remove <name>`, `status <name>`, `ping <name>` (alias of status).
- **Outputs / side effects:** A Chromium subprocess; a transcript file; audio played into the meeting in realtime mode; saved Google session state; registered remote nodes.
- **Config / env:** OpenAI Realtime API key for `mode=realtime`; Google session state file (`_auth_state_path()`).
- **Edge cases / guards:** Only `meet.google.com` URLs are accepted; no calendar scanning; no auto-dial. `meet_join`'s own description reminds the agent: "you should announce yourself in the meeting (there is no automatic consent announcement)." `meet_leave` is safe with no active meeting (returns `ok=false` with a reason). `meet_say` requires the meeting to have been joined with `mode='realtime'`. Windows is not a supported platform. If the node module fails to import, the `node` subparser stays present and prints "hermes meet node: module unavailable (<err>)".
- **Rebuild notes:** Headless browser + caption scraping + a virtual audio device is the whole realtime stack; the remote-node split exists because the signed-in Chrome profile usually lives on a different machine than a headless gateway. A better version would use the Meet API for transcripts where licensing allows and speak a consent announcement automatically on join.

### hermes-achievements (dashboard plugin)  `id: optional.plugin-achievements`
- **Surface:** Web dashboard
- **Where:** `plugins/hermes-achievements/`; dashboard tab label **"Achievements"** at route `/achievements`, icon `Star`, positioned `after:analytics`; API mounted at `/api/plugins/hermes-achievements/`.
- **What it does:** Awards Steam-style tiered badges by scanning your real local Hermes session history, and shows them on a dashboard tab with shareable cards.
- **How it works:** Vendored from `PCinkusz/hermes-achievements`; auto-registers as a dashboard tab on first `hermes dashboard` launch, no separate install step. `dashboard/manifest.json` declares `label "Achievements"`, `description "Steam-style achievements for vibe coding and agentic Hermes workflows."`, `icon "Star"`, `version 0.4.0`, `entry dist/index.js`, `css dist/style.css`, `api plugin_api.py`. `dashboard/plugin_api.py` (1092 lines) exposes an `APIRouter` with a snapshot cache + incremental checkpoint scan for fast warm loads. Achievements track: autonomous tool chains, debugging and recovery patterns, vibe-coding file edits, Hermes-native skills/memory/cron/plugin usage, web research and browser automation, model/provider workflows, and lifestyle patterns such as weekend or night sessions. Three visible states — **Unlocked** (earned at least one tier), **Discovered** (known achievement, progress visible, not earned yet), **Secret** (hidden until Hermes detects the first related signal). Tier ladder: `Copper → Silver → Gold → Diamond → Olympian`. Each card has a collapsible **"What counts"** section showing the exact tracked metric. v0.2.x expanded the catalog past 60 achievements including model/provider badges **Five-Model Flight**, **Provider Polyglot**, **Claude Confidant**, **Gemini Cartographer**, **Open Weights Pilgrim**; other named examples: **Let Him Cook**, **Toolchain Maxxer**, **Red Text Connoisseur**, **Port 3000 Is Taken**, **This Was Supposed To Be Quick**, **One More Small Change**, **Skillsmith**, **Memory Keeper**, **Context Dragon**, **Plugin Goblin**, **Rabbit Hole Certified**. Share cards (added 2026-05-04, vendored in v0.4.0) render a 1200×630 PNG client-side on canvas — no backend, no network — with Download and Copy-to-clipboard actions, sized for X/Twitter, Discord, LinkedIn and Bluesky link previews.
- **Inputs / options:** API routes (`plugin_api.py`): `GET /achievements` (l.1030), `GET /scan-status` (l.1042), `GET /recent-unlocks` (l.1047), `GET /sessions/{session_id}/badges` (l.1053), `POST /rescan` (l.1068), `POST /reset-state` (l.1073). UI: the **Share** button on an unlocked card, the collapsible **What counts** section.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Cached achievement state; PNG share cards saved/copied by the browser.
- **Config / env:** Reads local Hermes session history under `HERMES_HOME`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Routes sit behind the dashboard session-token middleware. Tests in `tests/test_achievement_engine.py`. Screenshots in `docs/assets/` use temporary demo tier data.
- **Rebuild notes:** Derive badges from session logs with an incremental checkpoint scan and a snapshot cache; render share images client-side so no image service is needed.

### kanban (dashboard plugin + dispatcher)  `id: optional.plugin-kanban`
- **Surface:** Web dashboard
- **Where:** `plugins/kanban/`; dashboard tab label **"Kanban"** at `/kanban`, icon `Package`, positioned `after:skills`; API at `/api/plugins/kanban/`; systemd unit `plugins/kanban/systemd/hermes-kanban-dispatcher.service`.
- **What it does:** A multi-agent collaboration board — drag-drop cards across columns, read comment threads, and see which profile is running what — backed by the same SQLite tables the CLI and the gateway `/kanban` command use.
- **How it works:** `dashboard/manifest.json`: `label "Kanban"`, `description "Multi-agent collaboration board — drag-drop cards across columns, read comment threads, see which profile is running what"`, `icon "Package"`, `version 1.0.0`, `entry dist/index.js`, `css dist/style.css`, `api plugin_api.py`. `dashboard/plugin_api.py` (3100 lines) is "intentionally thin: every handler is a small wrapper around `hermes_cli.kanban_db` or a direct SQL query. Writes use the same code paths the CLI and gateway `/kanban` command use, so the three surfaces cannot drift." Live updates come from the `/events` WebSocket, which tails the append-only `task_events` table on a short poll interval (WAL mode lets reads run alongside the dispatcher's IMMEDIATE write transactions).
- **Inputs / options:** REST routes (`plugin_api.py` line numbers): `GET /board` 381; `GET /tasks/{task_id}` 520; `POST /tasks` 624; `GET /tasks/{task_id}/attachments` 699; `POST /tasks/{task_id}/attachments` 715; `GET /attachments/{attachment_id}` 785; `DELETE /attachments/{attachment_id}` 812; `PATCH /tasks/{task_id}` 870; `DELETE /tasks/{task_id}` 1063; `POST /tasks/{task_id}/comments` 1240; `POST /links` 1266; `DELETE /links` 1279; `POST /tasks/bulk` 1317; `GET /diagnostics` 1464; `GET /workers/active` 1552; `GET /runs/{run_id}` 1613; `GET /runs/{run_id}/inspect` 1635; `POST /runs/{run_id}/terminate` 1707; `POST /tasks/{task_id}/reclaim` 1763; `POST /tasks/{task_id}/specify` 1801; `POST /tasks/{task_id}/reassign` 1851; `POST /estimate` 1910; `POST /tasks/{task_id}/estimate` 1917; `GET /config` 2018; `GET /home-channels` 2107; `POST /tasks/{task_id}/home-subscribe/{platform}` 2141; `DELETE /tasks/{task_id}/home-subscribe/{platform}` 2176; `GET /stats` 2205; `GET /assignees` 2221; `GET /tasks/{task_id}/log` 2242; `POST /dispatch` 2282; `GET /model-options` 2307; `GET /projects` 2455; `GET /boards` 2483; `POST /boards` 2526; `PATCH /boards/{slug}` 2559; `DELETE /boards/{slug}` 2599; `POST /boards/{slug}/export` 2609; `POST /boards/import` 2646; `POST /boards/{slug}/switch` 2673; `GET /profiles` 2713; `PATCH /profiles/{profile_name}` 2743; `POST /profiles/{profile_name}/describe-auto` 2776; `POST /tasks/{task_id}/decompose` 2812; `GET /orchestration` 2863; `PUT /orchestration` 2906; `WEBSOCKET /events` 2977.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Writes to the kanban SQLite DB (`task_events` append-only log), attachment files, dispatched agent runs, home-channel subscriptions.
- **Config / env:** `kanban.auto_subscribe_on_create` (true), `kanban.dispatch_in_gateway` (true), `kanban.review_dispatch` (true), `kanban.dispatch_interval_seconds` (60), `kanban.failure_limit` (2), `kanban.worker_log_rotate_bytes` (2097152), `kanban.worker_log_backup_count` (1), `kanban.orchestrator_profile` (""), `kanban.default_assignee` (""), `kanban.max_in_progress` (null), `kanban.max_in_progress_per_profile` (null), `kanban.auto_decompose` (true), `kanban.auto_decompose_per_tick` (3), `kanban.dispatch_stale_timeout_seconds` (14400), `kanban.reconcile_orphans` (true), `kanban.done_sub_retention_days` (30).
- **Edge cases / guards:** All `/api/plugins/...` requests require the session bearer token or the session cookie; the `/events` WebSocket takes `?token=` because browsers cannot set `Authorization` on an upgrade. That makes `hermes dashboard --host 0.0.0.0` safe on a LAN, though auth is still single-user.
- **Rebuild notes:** One DB, three thin surfaces (CLI, gateway command, dashboard API) over shared write functions; an append-only event table plus a polling WebSocket is a cheap way to get live updates without a message bus.

### Memory provider: ByteRover  `id: optional.plugin-memory-byterover`
- **Surface:** Provider
- **Where:** `plugins/memory/byterover/` (`plugin.yaml` v1.0.0); selected via `memory.provider = "byterover"`.
- **What it does:** Stores long-term memory in ByteRover's persistent knowledge tree with tiered retrieval.
- **How it works:** Manifest: description "ByteRover — persistent knowledge tree with tiered retrieval via the brv CLI."; `external_dependencies: [{name: brv, install: "curl -fsSL https://byterover.dev/install.sh | sh", check: "brv --version"}]`; `hooks: [on_pre_compress]` — memory is written just before context compression.
- **Inputs / options:** provider selection + its declarative `config_schema.py` fields.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Memory records in ByteRover; the `brv` binary installed on PATH.
- **Config / env:** `memory.provider`, `memory.memory_enabled` (true), `memory.user_profile_enabled` (true), `memory.write_approval` (false), `memory.memory_char_limit` (2200), `memory.user_char_limit` (1375), `memory.nudge_interval` (10).
- **Edge cases / guards:** Requires the external `brv` CLI; the declared `check` command is how the host verifies availability.
- **Rebuild notes:** `external_dependencies` with an `install` and a `check` command lets the host self-heal a missing binary.

### Memory provider: Honcho  `id: optional.plugin-memory-honcho`
- **Surface:** Provider
- **Where:** `plugins/memory/honcho/` (v1.0.0); `memory.provider = "honcho"`. Paired with the optional skill `autonomous-ai-agents/honcho`.
- **What it does:** AI-native cross-session user modeling with dialectic Q&A, semantic search and persistent conclusions.
- **How it works:** Manifest description "Honcho AI-native memory — cross-session user modeling with dialectic Q&A, semantic search, and persistent conclusions."; `pip_dependencies: [honcho-ai]`; `hooks: [on_session_end]`. Its config uses the `honcho_host_block` storage backend (`STORAGE_HONCHO_HOST_BLOCK` in `plugins/memory/config_schema.py:40`), i.e. a host block per profile rather than flat JSON. Config also surfaces under `plugins.honcho`.
- **Inputs / options:** Tools contributed when active: `honcho_profile`, `honcho_search`, `honcho_context`, `honcho_reasoning`, `honcho_conclude` (documented by the `honcho` optional skill); three orthogonal knobs — cadence (when), depth (how many), level (how hard); recall modes; peers (`peer: "ai"` targeting).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Peers/sessions/observations in Honcho cloud (`app.honcho.dev`) or a self-hosted base URL.
- **Config / env:** `memory.provider`, `plugins.honcho.*`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Cloud vs self-hosted selection at setup; multi-profile setups create a host block per profile (e.g. `hermes.coder`).
- **Rebuild notes:** Two storage backends (flat JSON vs host block) in one declarative schema keeps the generic renderer working for an unusual provider.
