### Declarative memory-provider config schema  `id: optional.plugin-memory-config-schema`
- **Surface:** Config
- **Where:** `plugins/memory/config_schema.py` (144 lines) + one `config_schema.py` per provider; rendered by the desktop UI and by `GET/PUT /api/memory/providers/{name}/config`.
- **What it does:** Lets each memory provider declare its configurable fields once, so one generic renderer and one generic endpoint pair drive every provider's settings UI with no bespoke components.
- **How it works:** Field kinds: `text`, `select`, `secret`, `bool`, `number`, `json` (`KIND_*` constants). Storage backends: `flat_json` (`STORAGE_FLAT_JSON`) and `honcho_host_block` (`STORAGE_HONCHO_HOST_BLOCK`); `web_server` dispatches read/write on `ProviderConfigSchema.storage`. `ProviderField` fields: `key`, `label`, `kind`, `default`, `description`, `placeholder`, `options` (tuple of `ProviderFieldOption{value,label,description}`), `env_key`, `aliases` (legacy config keys), `env_fallbacks` (legacy env names), `inline` (shown in the compact panel vs the full-config modal), `group` (bucket within the modal). Non-secret kinds persist through the storage backend under `key`; `secret` kinds persist to the env store under `env_key` and are **never read back over the API** — only an `is_set` flag is surfaced. Schema files are loaded **by path**, never via package import, because a provider `__init__.py` pulls in the agent runtime which must not load into the web server; a `config_schema.py` may import only from this module. The module is pure data — it imports nothing from the config/env layer.
- **Inputs / options:** the field attributes above.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Provider settings persisted to config or `.env`.
- **Config / env:** per-provider keys; secrets in `.env`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Secrets never round-trip; aliases/env_fallbacks exist so values written by older CLI/env setup are still read without per-provider code.
- **Rebuild notes:** Declaration-driven settings UIs are the difference between "adding a provider is a PR to the frontend" and "adding a provider is one data file". A better version would also declare validation rules and inter-field dependencies.

### Memory query rewriting  `id: optional.plugin-memory-query-rewrite`
- **Surface:** Core
- **Where:** `plugins/memory/query_rewrite.py` (139 lines); any memory provider can pass `rewrite_memory_query` as its query rewriter.
- **What it does:** Turns the latest raw user message into a clean retrieval query before it is sent to the memory backend.
- **How it works:** Provider-agnostic. Task key `memory_query_rewrite`; model and timeout configured under `auxiliary.memory_query_rewrite` in `config.yaml`. Guards: input truncated at 4,000 chars (`_MAX_INPUT_CHARS`), output capped at 320 chars (`_MAX_QUERY_CHARS`); a leading `retrieval query:` / `memory query:` / `query:` / `question:` prefix is stripped (`_OUTPUT_PREFIX_RE`); heuristics detect question openers (`what|which|who|where|when|why|how|is|are|was|were|do|does|did|has|have|had|can|could|would|should|may|might`) and memory-grounding words (`user|their|they|them|previous|prior|past|history|preference|…`).
- **Inputs / options:** the latest user message; `auxiliary.memory_query_rewrite.{model,timeout}`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A rewritten query string handed to the provider.
- **Config / env:** `auxiliary.memory_query_rewrite.*`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Hard input/output caps keep the auxiliary call cheap and bounded.
- **Rebuild notes:** One shared rewriter beats per-provider prompt engineering.

### observability/langfuse  `id: optional.plugin-observability-langfuse`
- **Surface:** Tool
- **Where:** `plugins/observability/langfuse/`; enable with `hermes plugins enable observability/langfuse` or interactively via `hermes tools` → **"Langfuse Observability"**.
- **What it does:** Traces conversations, LLM calls and tool usage into Langfuse (cloud or self-hosted) so you can inspect every turn.
- **How it works:** Manifest v1.0.0, `requires_env: [HERMES_LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY, HERMES_LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY]`, and eleven hooks: `pre_api_request`, `post_api_request`, `api_request_error`, `pre_llm_call`, `post_llm_call`, `pre_tool_call`, `post_tool_call`, `on_session_finalize`, `on_session_end`, `subagent_start`, `subagent_stop`. A turn becomes a "Hermes turn" trace with LLM-call child spans; generation observations include the Hermes system prompt when the provider uses a separate `system` param (Anthropic Messages API) — visible as `role: system` inside an **LLM call** child span, truncated by `HERMES_LANGFUSE_MAX_CHARS`. Failed model requests (`api_request_error`) close their generation with `level=ERROR`, status code, retry counters and a capture-mode-scrubbed error message; non-retryable failures also finish the turn trace. Session end/finalize closes still-open traces and flushes queued events so interrupted or tool-only turns do not dangle.
- **Inputs / options:** Enable: `pip install langfuse` + `hermes plugins enable observability/langfuse`, or the `hermes tools` wizard which walks credentials + SDK install + enable. Disable: `hermes plugins disable observability/langfuse`. Verify: `hermes plugins list` shows "enabled", then `hermes chat -q "hello"`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Traces/spans in Langfuse; every trace records `metadata.capture_mode`.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY` (`pk-lf-…`), `HERMES_LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY` (`sk-lf-…`), `HERMES_LANGFUSE_BASE_URL` (default `https://cloud.langfuse.com`), `HERMES_LANGFUSE_ENV` (environment tag), `HERMES_LANGFUSE_RELEASE` (release tag), `HERMES_LANGFUSE_SAMPLE_RATE` (e.g. 0.5), `HERMES_LANGFUSE_MAX_CHARS` (default 12000), `HERMES_LANGFUSE_CAPTURE` (`metadata` | `sanitized` (default) | `full`), `HERMES_LANGFUSE_DEBUG`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Without the SDK or credentials the hooks no-op silently — fails open. Capture modes: `metadata` replaces each content field with a shape/size stub `{"omitted": true, "type": "text", "chars": N}`; `sanitized` redacts secret patterns (API keys, tokens, JWTs, private keys, `password=`-style assignments) **before** truncating; `full` is raw content, explicit opt-in, and "traces will contain whatever passed through the conversation, including injected memory and file contents". Structural metadata (IDs, roles, tool names, token usage, cost, timing) is always captured. The README warns `sanitized` is "pattern-based defense in depth, not a DLP guarantee" and recommends `metadata` for personal or shared projects.
- **Rebuild notes:** Hook every lifecycle point, make content capture a three-level policy, redact before truncating, and fail open when unconfigured.

### security-guidance  `id: optional.plugin-security-guidance`
- **Surface:** Tool
- **Where:** `plugins/security-guidance/`; enable with `hermes plugins enable security-guidance` or by adding it to `plugins.enabled` in `~/.hermes/config.yaml`.
- **What it does:** Scans content the agent is about to write for 25 known-dangerous code patterns and appends a "⚠️ Security guidance" block to the tool result so the model can self-correct on the next turn.
- **How it works:** Hooks `transform_tool_result` and `pre_tool_call`. Triggers on `write_file`, `patch`, and `skill_manage`. `patterns.py` (368 lines) is byte-for-byte upstream Anthropic `claude-plugins-official` `patterns.py` at commit 0bde168 (2026-05-26), Apache-2.0; Hermes wiring lives in `__init__.py` (259 lines). Each rule is `{ruleName, path_check|path_filter, substrings|regex, reminder}` — per-extension filters mean Python rules skip `.js`, JS rules skip `.py`, and every rule skips `.md/.mdx/.txt/.rst/.json/.yaml/.yml` (`_DOC_EXTS`); `_JS_EXTS` = `.js .jsx .ts .tsx .mjs .cjs .mts .cts .vue .svelte`, `_PY_EXTS` = `.py .pyi .ipynb`. Lookbehind assertions exclude method calls so `model.eval()` and `redis.eval()` do not trip the `eval(` rule. A frozen `RuleId(IntEnum)` maps each rule to a stable number 1–25 and `rule_names_to_mask()` packs matched rules into a bitmask emitted through the PostToolUse metrics field (the metrics schema allows only bool|number, so names cannot be sent); user-defined patterns (`user:` prefix) have no static RuleId and are excluded from the mask. An import-time assert fails loudly (and in pytest on every PR) if `SECURITY_PATTERNS` and `_RULE_NAME_TO_ID` desync.
- **Inputs / options:** The 25 rules by `ruleName`: `github_actions_workflow` (1), `child_process_exec` (2), `new_function_injection` (3), `eval_injection` (4), `react_dangerously_set_html` (5), `document_write_xss` (6), `innerHTML_xss` (7), `pickle_deserialization` (8), `os_system_injection` (9), `python_subprocess_shell` (10), `go_exec_shell_injection` (11), `unsafe_yaml_load` (12), `node_createcipher_no_iv` (13), `aes_ecb_mode` (14), `tls_verification_disabled` (15), `marshal_loads` (16), `shelve_open` (17), `xml_unsafe_parse` (18), `pickle_variants_load` (19), `outerHTML_xss` (20), `insertAdjacentHTML_xss` (21), `script_src_without_sri` (22), `torch_unsafe_load` (23), `yaml_unsafe_load_variants` (24), `pickle_wrapper_load` (25). Documented categories: unsafe deserialization (`pickle.load`, `cPickle/cloudpickle/dill.load`, `marshal.loads`, `shelve.open`, `yaml.load`, `yaml.unsafe_load`, `torch.load` without `weights_only=True`, `joblib.load`, `pandas.read_pickle`, `numpy.load(allow_pickle=True)`), command injection (`os.system`, `subprocess(..., shell=True)`, JS `child_process.exec`, Go `exec.Command("sh"…)`), code injection (`eval(`, JS `new Function(...)`), XSS sinks (`.innerHTML =`, `.outerHTML =`, `.insertAdjacentHTML(`, `document.write`, React `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`), crypto footguns (AES ECB, Node `crypto.createCipher` without IV, TLS verification disabled — `verify=False`, `rejectUnauthorized: false`, `InsecureSkipVerify: true`), XXE (`xml.etree`, `minidom`, `xml.sax` without `defusedxml`), supply chain (`<script src="https://…"` without `integrity=` SRI), CI/CD injection (GitHub Actions workflow files using `${{ github.event.* }}` in `run:`).
- **Outputs / side effects:** A `⚠️ Security guidance` block appended to the tool result (the file is still written), plus a rule bitmask in PostToolUse metrics. In block mode the write is refused with the warning as the reason plus "To override, unset SECURITY_GUIDANCE_BLOCK and retry."
- **Config / env:** `SECURITY_GUIDANCE_BLOCK=1` (refuse the write entirely — `1|true|yes|on`), `SECURITY_GUIDANCE_DISABLE=1` (kill switch: plugin loads but does nothing). Default (neither set): warn.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Warn-by-default because the false-positive rate is "mediocre but tolerable". Only layer 1 of Anthropic's three-layer design is ported — LLM diff review on turn end and agentic commit review are not (the agent can run those via `delegate_task`). LICENSE + NOTICE ship alongside for Apache-2.0 compliance.
- **Rebuild notes:** Static pattern matching with per-language path filters, zero LLM tokens, non-blocking by default, and a stable numeric rule id for telemetry. A better version would rank findings by confidence and suppress a rule once the model has documented why the construct is safe.

### spotify  `id: optional.plugin-spotify`
- **Surface:** Tool
- **Where:** `plugins/spotify/` (`plugin.yaml` v1.0.0, `kind: backend`); auth via `hermes auth spotify`; tools appear in the `spotify` toolset.
- **What it does:** Adds seven Spotify tools — playback control, device listing, queue, search, playlists, albums and library — over the Spotify Web API with PKCE OAuth.
- **How it works:** Manifest description: "Native Spotify integration — 7 tools (playback, devices, queue, search, playlists, albums, library) using Spotify Web API + PKCE OAuth. Auth via `hermes auth spotify`. Tools gate on `providers.spotify` in ~/.hermes/auth.json." Bundled + `kind: backend` means it auto-loads on startup like image_gen backends — no user opt-in, no `plugins.enabled` entry. `register(ctx)` loops a `_TOOLS` tuple registering each `(name, schema, handler, emoji)` pair; handlers are gated by `_check_spotify_available()` so unauthenticated tools stay *registered* (visible in `hermes tools`) but refuse dispatch.
- **Inputs / options:** `provides_tools` / `_TOOLS`: `spotify_playback` (🎵), `spotify_devices` (🔈), `spotify_queue` (📻), `spotify_search` (🔎), `spotify_playlists` (📚), `spotify_albums` (💿), `spotify_library` (❤️).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Real playback control on the user's Spotify account.
- **Config / env:** `providers.spotify` in `~/.hermes/auth.json`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Tools are always registered so they are discoverable; the runtime check is what blocks unauthenticated calls.
- **Rebuild notes:** Register-then-gate keeps discoverability without lying about availability; the plugin docstring is explicit that `plugins/` is for third-party service integrations while `tools/` is reserved for foundational capabilities.

### teams_pipeline (Microsoft Teams meeting pipeline)  `id: optional.plugin-teams-pipeline`
- **Surface:** CLI
- **Where:** `plugins/teams_pipeline/` (`plugin.yaml` v0.1.0, `kind: standalone`, `platforms: [linux, macos, windows]`); operator CLI sub-commands.
- **What it does:** Produces transcript-first meeting summaries from Microsoft Teams via Microsoft Graph, with durable job state and operator commands for subscriptions, replay and diagnostics.
- **How it works:** Manifest description: "Microsoft Teams meeting pipeline plugin with durable runtime state and operator CLI flows for Graph-backed transcript-first meeting summaries." Modules: `pipeline.py` (the run), `meetings.py` (artifact resolution), `subscriptions.py` (Graph change-notification subscriptions), `store.py` (durable job store, `--store-path` overrides its location), `runtime.py`, `models.py`, `cli.py`.
- **Inputs / options:** `list` (alias `ls`, "List recent Teams pipeline jobs") `--limit 20`, `--status ""`, `--store-path ""`; `show <job_id> [--store-path]` ("Show a stored Teams pipeline job"); `run` (alias `replay`) `<job_id> [--store-path]` ("Replay a stored Teams pipeline job"); `fetch` (alias `test`) ("Dry-run meeting artifact resolution") `--meeting-id`, `--join-web-url`, `--tenant-id`, `--call-record-id`, plus one more selector flag; `subscriptions` (alias `subs`, "List Graph subscriptions") `--store-path`; `subscribe` ("Create a Microsoft Graph subscription") `--resource` (required), `--notification-url` (required), `--change-type`, `--expiration`, `--client-state`, `--lifecycle-notification-url`, `--latest-supported-tls-version v1_2`, `--store-path`; `renew-subscription <subscription_id> --expiration <ts> [--store-path]`; `delete-subscription <subscription_id> [--store-path]`; `maintain-subscriptions` ("Renew near-expiry managed subscriptions") `--renew-within-hours 24`, `--extend-hours 24`, `--dry-run`, `--client-state`, `--store-path`; `token-health` (alias `token`, "Inspect Graph token health") `--force-refresh`; `validate` ("Validate Teams pipeline configuration snapshot") `--store-path`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Durable job records in the store; Graph subscriptions created/renewed/deleted; meeting summaries.
- **Config / env:** Microsoft Graph credentials/tenant configuration; store path.
- **Edge cases / guards:** `--dry-run` on subscription maintenance; `validate` checks the config snapshot before a run; `token-health --force-refresh` diagnoses expired Graph tokens.
- **Rebuild notes:** Durable job store + replay + subscription maintenance is the minimum for a webhook-driven meeting pipeline; make every mutating operator command dry-runnable.

---

## C. Optional MCP catalog — 65 Nous-approved server manifests (`optional-mcps/`)

Every entry ships **disabled**. Each is one `manifest.yaml`; the shared install/probe/tool-filter machinery is documented in `optional.mcp-catalog`, `optional.mcp-install-flow`, `optional.mcp-tool-selection` and `optional.mcp-suggest` above and is not repeated per entry.

### MCP catalog: Airtable  `id: optional.mcp-airtable`
- **Surface:** Tool
- **Where:** `optional-mcps/airtable/manifest.yaml`; listed by `hermes mcp catalog`, toggled in `hermes mcp picker`, installed with `hermes mcp install airtable`, re-filtered with `hermes mcp configure airtable`, re-authed with `hermes mcp login airtable`.
- **What it does:** Bases, tables, and records from your Airtable workspace. Ships disabled; installing writes an `mcp_servers.airtable` block and loads its tools into the agent on the next session.
- **How it works:** Remote Streamable-HTTP MCP at `https://mcp.airtable.com/mcp` — Hermes never spawns a local process for this entry. Auth: native MCP OAuth 2.1 + Dynamic Client Registration (case 1). The MCP client + `mcp_oauth_manager` handle discovery, PKCE, token exchange and refresh; the browser flow fires on first probe/connect. `mcp_servers.airtable.auth: oauth` is the only marker written. No manifest tool hints: the install-time checklist starts with every probed tool pre-checked and the user prunes. Vendor documentation: https://support.airtable.com/articles/9897799762-using-the-airtable-mcp-server
- **Inputs / options:** `hermes mcp install airtable` (or `official/airtable`); `hermes mcp login airtable`; `hermes mcp reauth airtable`; `hermes mcp configure airtable` (re-run the tool checklist); `hermes mcp remove airtable`; config edits under `mcp_servers.airtable.{url|command|args|env|headers|enabled|tools.include|tools.exclude}`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** `mcp_servers.airtable` block in `~/.hermes/config.yaml` (with `enabled: true` and a tools filter); OAuth tokens held by `mcp_oauth_manager`; the server's tools appear in the agent's tool list after a session restart.
- **Config / env:** `mcp_servers.airtable.enabled`, `mcp_servers.airtable.tools.include` / `.tools.exclude`, `mcp_servers.airtable.url`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Post-install notes printed verbatim by the installer: "On first connection Hermes opens a browser to authorize with" / "Airtable (or run `hermes mcp login airtable`). Approve access," / "then restart the session so tools load." Composer suggestion triggers — keywords: `airtable`; host suffixes: `airtable.com`. Presence in `optional-mcps/` = Nous approval by PR review; never auto-updated — re-run `hermes mcp install` after a Hermes upgrade to pick up a new manifest.
- **Rebuild notes:** Reimplement as a manifest of `{name, description, source, transport, auth, tools, suggest, post_install}` consumed by one generic installer; for this entry the load-bearing parts are the URL + native OAuth marker and the tool filter. A better version would pin the server's advertised protocol/tool-set version and warn when it changes.

### MCP catalog: Algolia  `id: optional.mcp-algolia`
- **Surface:** Tool
- **Where:** `optional-mcps/algolia/manifest.yaml`; listed by `hermes mcp catalog`, toggled in `hermes mcp picker`, installed with `hermes mcp install algolia`, re-filtered with `hermes mcp configure algolia`, re-authed with `hermes mcp login algolia`.
- **What it does:** Algolia search: indices, analytics, and settings (read-only). Ships disabled; installing writes an `mcp_servers.algolia` block and loads its tools into the agent on the next session.
- **How it works:** Remote Streamable-HTTP MCP at `https://mcp.algolia.com/mcp` — Hermes never spawns a local process for this entry. Auth: native MCP OAuth 2.1 + Dynamic Client Registration (case 1). The MCP client + `mcp_oauth_manager` handle discovery, PKCE, token exchange and refresh; the browser flow fires on first probe/connect. `mcp_servers.algolia.auth: oauth` is the only marker written. ### MCP catalog: n8n  `id: optional.mcp-n8n`
- **Surface:** Tool
- **Where:** `optional-mcps/n8n/manifest.yaml`; listed by `hermes mcp catalog`, toggled in `hermes mcp picker`, installed with `hermes mcp install n8n`, re-filtered with `hermes mcp configure n8n`.
- **What it does:** Manage and inspect n8n workflows from Hermes (stdio bridge, no public port). Ships disabled; installing writes an `mcp_servers.n8n` block and loads its tools into the agent on the next session.
- **How it works:** stdio transport: `${INSTALL_DIR}/.venv/bin/python` with args `${INSTALL_DIR}/server.py`. Auth: API key. Prompted at install and saved to `~/.hermes/.env`: `N8N_BASE_URL` — prompt "n8n instance URL", default `http://127.0.0.1:5678`, required, non-secret (echoed); `N8N_API_KEY` — prompt "n8n API key (generate under Settings → API)", required, secret (masked). Bootstrap: `install.type: git`, clone `https://github.com/CyberSamuraiX/hermes-n8n-mcp.git` pinned at ref `7a9ae00795593aa1fdb4e61ecd640e8bfd0c3841` into `~/.hermes/mcp-installs/n8n`, then run `python3 -m venv .venv`; `.venv/bin/pip install -r requirements.txt`. `${INSTALL_DIR}` in the transport is substituted with that path. `tools.default_enabled` (8 pre-checked in the install checklist, and applied directly if the probe fails): `health`, `list_workflows`, `get_workflow`, `find_workflows`, `list_executions`, `get_execution`, `recent_failures`, `export_workflow`. Vendor documentation: https://github.com/CyberSamuraiX/hermes-n8n-mcp
- **Inputs / options:** `hermes mcp install n8n` (or `official/n8n`); `hermes mcp configure n8n` (re-run the tool checklist); `hermes mcp remove n8n`; config edits under `mcp_servers.n8n.{url|command|args|env|headers|enabled|tools.include|tools.exclude}`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** `mcp_servers.n8n` block in `~/.hermes/config.yaml` (with `enabled: true` and a tools filter); credentials appended to `~/.hermes/.env`; a git clone at `~/.hermes/mcp-installs/n8n`; the server's tools appear in the agent's tool list after a session restart.
- **Config / env:** `mcp_servers.n8n.enabled`, `mcp_servers.n8n.tools.include` / `.tools.exclude`, `mcp_servers.n8n.command` / `.args` / `.env`, `N8N_BASE_URL`, `N8N_API_KEY`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Post-install notes printed verbatim by the installer: "The n8n bridge expects to talk to a running n8n instance over the URL you" / "provided. Generate an API key in n8n under Settings → API." / "Workflow activate/deactivate calls are real mutations against your live n8n." / "Treat them carefully." / "Start a new Hermes session to load the n8n tools." No `suggest` block — this entry never produces a desktop composer pill. ---

## D. Optional skills — 137 bundled `SKILL.md` bundles (`optional-skills/`)

All install with `hermes skills install official/<path>` and uninstall with `hermes skills uninstall <name>`; that shared machinery is documented in `optional.skills-catalog` / `optional.skills-install` and is not repeated per entry. Every entry below quotes the skill's real frontmatter (`name`, `description`, `version`, `author`, `license`, `platforms`, `metadata.hermes.tags`) and its actual section structure.

### Skill: agent-merge-conflict-arbiter  `id: optional.skill-agent-merge-conflict-arbiter`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/autonomous-ai-agents/agent-merge-conflict-arbiter/SKILL.md`; install `hermes skills install official/autonomous-ai-agents/agent-merge-conflict-arbiter`. **Not listed in `website/docs/reference/optional-skills-catalog.md`.**
- **What it does:** Frontmatter description: "Neutral arbiter for merge conflicts between two agents." Gives a third agent a procedure for adjudicating a halted merge between two other agents' branches without either of them self-adjudicating.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Hermes Agent", MIT, platforms `[linux, macos, windows]`, tags `Multi-Agent, Git, Merge-Conflict, Kanban, Arbitration`, `related_skills: [hermes-agent]`. Prose-only (159 lines, no scripts). Sections: When to Use · Prerequisites · How to Run · Quick Reference · Procedure (1. Gather both sides, 2. Classify every conflicted hunk, 3. Resolve under the impartiality contract, 4. Verify, 5. Hand back) · Pitfalls · Verification. Intent sources it tells the arbiter to gather: kanban completion summaries via `terminal` running `hermes kanban show <task-id>`, PR bodies, or each branch's commit messages, plus the project's build/test command.
- **Inputs / options:** A repo checkout containing the halted merge, or the two branch names plus permission to run the merge; both sides' intent sources; the build/test command.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A resolved merge in the working tree plus a hand-back summary; no files written by the skill itself.
- **Config / env:** n/a.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Explicitly NOT for conflicts within a single agent's own work, and not for trivial lockfile/generated-file conflicts (regenerate those instead). The "impartiality contract" forbids the arbiter from favouring either side.
- **Rebuild notes:** A written adjudication procedure plus a hunk-classification step is the whole skill; the value is the impartiality framing. A better version would auto-fetch both agents' task summaries and emit a machine-checkable resolution rationale per hunk.

### Skill: antigravity-cli  `id: optional.skill-antigravity-cli`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/autonomous-ai-agents/antigravity-cli/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/autonomous-ai-agents/antigravity-cli`; catalog row "Operate the Antigravity CLI (agy): plugins, auth, sandbox."
- **What it does:** Teaches Hermes to drive Google's Antigravity coding CLI (`agy`) — installing it, running one-shots, debugging auth/sandbox/permissions, and managing its plugins.
- **How it works:** v0.2.0, authors "Tony Simons (asimons81), Hermes Agent", MIT, all three platforms, tags `Coding-Agent, Antigravity, CLI, Auth, Plugins, Sandbox`, `related_skills: [grok, codex, claude-code, hermes-agent]`. 242 lines + `references/cli-docs.md`. Sections: When to Use · Mental model · Prerequisites · How to Run · Delegation patterns (One-shot — preferred for scripted prompts and second opinions; Long/bounded runs via `process(action="poll"/"log"/"wait", session_id=<id>)`; Interactive multi-turn via PTY + tmux; Parallel instances for batch sub-issue / worktree fan-out; Output + bounding caveat that differs from Claude Code; Orchestration boundary) · Core paths · Quick Reference (Wrapper commands; Useful flags; Plugin subcommands `agy plugin --help`; Install flags `agy install --help`; In-session slash commands) · Settings and permissions (Common settings keys in `settings.json`; Permission modes; Sandbox behavior) · Authentication behavior · Plugins · Pitfalls · Verification · Support files.
- **Inputs / options:** `agy --print` / `agy -p` for non-interactive one-shots; `agy install` flags `--dir`, `--skip-aliases`, `--skip-path`; `agy plugin` subcommands; in-session slash commands; `settings.json` keys; permission modes.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Whatever `agy` writes in the workspace; background session ids tracked by Hermes's `process` tool.
- **Config / env:** None required — "No env vars or API keys required by this skill — Antigravity manages its own auth via the OS keyring / browser sign-in."
- **Edge cases / guards:** Verify with `command -v agy && agy --version` through the `terminal` tool. Output bounding differs from Claude Code (documented caveat). An explicit "Orchestration boundary" section states what Hermes should not delegate.
- **Rebuild notes:** Wrap a third-party agent CLI as: install check → one-shot mode → background+poll mode → PTY mode → parallel fan-out, plus its real flag list. A better version would parse `agy`'s structured output instead of scraping the TUI.

### Skill: blackbox  `id: optional.skill-blackbox`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/autonomous-ai-agents/blackbox/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/autonomous-ai-agents/blackbox`; catalog row "Delegate coding tasks to the Blackbox AI multi-model CLI."
- **What it does:** Delegates coding work to the Blackbox AI CLI, including background runs, checkpoint resume, PR reviews and multi-model comparison.
- **How it works:** v1.0.1, author "Hermes Agent (Nous Research)", MIT, all platforms, tags `Coding-Agent, Blackbox, Multi-Agent, Judge, Multi-Model`, `related_skills: [claude-code, codex, hermes-agent]`. 144 lines, prose only. Sections: Prerequisites · One-Shot Tasks · Background Mode (Long Tasks) — start in background with PTY, returns `session_id`, monitor progress, send input if Blackbox asks a question, kill if needed · Checkpoints & Resume (Blackbox prints a checkpoint tag after a task; resume with a follow-up task) · Session Commands · PR Reviews · Parallel Work (monitor all) · Multi-Model Mode · Key Flags · Vision Support · Token Limits · Rules.
- **Inputs / options:** `npm install -g @blackbox_ai/blackbox-cli` (binary `blackbox`); `blackbox configure` to enter the API key; `pty=true` on terminal calls; session commands, key flags, multi-model mode, vision inputs.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Code changes in the workspace; checkpoint tags; background session ids.
- **Config / env:** API key from app.blackbox.ai/dashboard, stored by `blackbox configure`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Requires Node.js 20+. Blackbox CLI is an interactive terminal app, so `pty=true` is mandatory. Documented token limits.
- **Rebuild notes:** Same wrapper shape as the other coding-agent skills; the distinguishing features are checkpoint-resume and multi-model judging.

### Skill: grok  `id: optional.skill-grok`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/autonomous-ai-agents/grok/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/autonomous-ai-agents/grok`; catalog row "Delegate coding to xAI Grok Build CLI (features, PRs)."
- **What it does:** Orchestrates the xAI Grok Build CLI from Hermes for feature building, refactors, PR reviews and batch issue fixing.
- **How it works:** v0.1.1, authors "Matt Maximo (MattMaximo), Hermes Agent", MIT, all platforms, tags `Coding-Agent, Grok, xAI, Code-Review, Refactoring, Automation`, `related_skills: [codex, claude-code, hermes-agent]`. 309 lines. Sections: When to use · Prerequisites · Two Orchestration Modes — **Mode 1 Headless (`-p`), non-interactive, PREFERRED**; **Mode 2 Interactive PTY** multi-turn TUI in tmux with capture-pane monitoring · Headless Deep Dive (Common Flags; Output Formats — structured result for parsing, auto-approve for autonomous building; Background Mode returning a `session_id`; Session Continuation — start with a self-assigned valid unused UUID, resume that exact session by UUID, or continue the most recent session in the directory with no UUID) · Read-Only Audit → Markdown Note Pattern · PR Review Patterns (Quick Review headless; Clone-to-temp Review, safe, no repo mutation; Post the review) · Parallel Issue Fixing with Worktrees (create worktrees → launch Grok headless in each in background → monitor → push and open PRs → cleanup) · Useful Subcommands & TUI Commands · Config (`~/.grok/config.toml`) · Pitfalls & Gotchas · Rules for Hermes Agents.
- **Inputs / options:** Install `npm install -g @xai-official/grok` (preferred) or `curl -fsSL https://x.ai/cli/install.sh | bash`; `grok login` (browser OAuth, token cached in `~/.grok/auth.json`, uses a SuperGrok or X Premium+ subscription); headless `-p`; output-format and auto-approve flags; UUID session ids; `~/.grok/config.toml`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Code changes, PR reviews posted, worktrees created and cleaned up, `~/.grok/auth.json`.
- **Config / env:** `~/.grok/config.toml`, `~/.grok/auth.json`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** The npm install path is recommended because the `x.ai` installer host is Cloudflare-walled in some environments. Clone-to-temp review avoids mutating the user's repo. Session UUIDs must be valid and unused.
- **Rebuild notes:** Headless-first with an explicit background/poll story and a worktree fan-out recipe. A better version would drive the CLI's JSON output end-to-end instead of the TUI.

### Skill: honcho  `id: optional.skill-honcho`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/autonomous-ai-agents/honcho/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/autonomous-ai-agents/honcho`; catalog row "Configure and troubleshoot Honcho memory for Hermes."
- **What it does:** Sets up, tunes and debugs the Honcho memory provider (see `optional.plugin-memory-honcho`), including multi-profile peer setups and the five Honcho tools.
- **How it works:** v2.0.0, author "Hermes Agent", MIT, all platforms, tags `Honcho, Memory, Profiles, Observation, Dialectic, User-Modeling, Session-Summary`, `homepage: https://docs.honcho.dev`, `related_skills: [hermes-agent]`, `prerequisites.pip: [honcho-ai]`. 432 lines. Sections: When to Use · Setup (Cloud — select "cloud", paste an API key from https://app.honcho.dev; Self-hosted — select "local", enter a base URL e.g. `http://localhost:8000`; Verify) · Architecture (Base Context Injection; Cold/Warm Prompt Selection; Peers; Observation; Sessions; Recall Modes) · Three Orthogonal Knobs (Cadence = when, Depth = how many, Level = how hard) · Multi-Profile Setup (create a profile with a Honcho peer — "creates host block hermes.coder, AI peer 'coder', inherits config from default"; backfill existing profiles; per-profile config) · Tools (`honcho_profile`, `honcho_search`, `honcho_context`, `honcho_reasoning`, `honcho_conclude`; Bidirectional peer targeting) · Agent Usage Patterns (on conversation start; when the user shares something to remember; when the user asks about past context; when to use `peer: "ai"`; when NOT to call tools; cadence awareness) · Config Reference (Key settings; Dialectic settings; Context budget and injection; Memory-context sanitization) · Troubleshooting ("Honcho not configured"; memory not persisting across sessions; profile not getting its own peer; observation changes in dashboard not reflected; messages truncated; context injection too large; session summary missing) · CLI Commands.
- **Inputs / options:** `hermes memory setup honcho`; `hermes honcho status` ("shows resolved config, connection test, peer info"); `hermes profile create coder --clone`; `hermes honcho sync` ("creates host blocks for all profiles that don't have one yet"). Tool calls such as `honcho_conclude conclusion="I tend to be verbose when explaining architecture" peer="ai"` (self-correction) and `honcho_reasoning query="How do I typically handle ambiguous requests?" peer="ai"` (self-analysis).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Honcho host blocks per profile, peers, sessions, observations and conclusions; context injected into prompts.
- **Config / env:** Honcho API key or self-hosted base URL; `plugins.honcho.*`; `memory.provider = honcho`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** All profiles share one workspace (user representation) while each builds its own AI identity; conclusions written by one profile are visible to others through the shared workspace. Explicit "when NOT to call tools" guidance to stop tool spam.
- **Rebuild notes:** Separating cadence/depth/level into three orthogonal knobs, and giving the agent a peer of its own for self-knowledge, are the two ideas worth copying.

### Skill: openhands  `id: optional.skill-openhands`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/autonomous-ai-agents/openhands/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/autonomous-ai-agents/openhands`; catalog row "Delegate coding to OpenHands CLI (model-agnostic, LiteLLM)."
- **What it does:** Delegates multi-step coding work to the OpenHands CLI, which can run on any LiteLLM-supported provider rather than a single vendor.
- **How it works:** v0.1.0, authors "Tim Koepsel (xzessmedia), Hermes Agent", MIT, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `Coding-Agent, OpenHands, Model-Agnostic, LiteLLM`, `related_skills: [claude-code, codex, opencode, hermes-agent]`. 150 lines. Sections: When to Use · Prerequisites · How to Run (One-shot task; Background for long tasks; Resume a previous conversation) · Real Flag List · JSON Event Schema · Pitfalls · Verification · Related.
- **Inputs / options:** Install `uv tool install openhands --python 3.12` (Python 3.12+ and `uv` required); verify `openhands --version` (documented as `OpenHands CLI 1.16.0` / `SDK v1.21.0`). Env for `--override-with-envs`: `export LLM_MODEL=openrouter/openai/gpt-4o-mini` (or any LiteLLM slug), `export LLM_API_KEY=$OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, `export LLM_BASE_URL=https://openrouter.ai/api/v1` (omit for native OpenAI), `export OPENHANDS_SUPPRESS_BANNER=1`. Plus the skill's "Real Flag List" and the documented JSON event schema for parsing runs.
- **Outputs / side effects:** File edits and shell commands inside the workspace; a resumable conversation.
- **Config / env:** `LLM_MODEL`, `LLM_API_KEY`, `LLM_BASE_URL`, `OPENHANDS_SUPPRESS_BANNER`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Not available on Windows. Routing guidance: "For Claude-native, prefer `claude-code`. For OpenAI-native, prefer `codex`. For Hermes-native subagents, use `delegate_task`."
- **Rebuild notes:** Document the *real* flag list and the JSON event schema — that is what makes a CLI machine-drivable.

### Skill: evm  `id: optional.skill-evm`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/blockchain/evm/SKILL.md` + `scripts/evm_client.py`; `hermes skills install official/blockchain/evm`; catalog row "Read-only EVM client: wallets, tokens, gas across 8 chains."
- **What it does:** Read-only EVM blockchain client — wallet portfolios, ERC-20 metadata and prices, transaction decoding, gas comparison, ENS, allowance auditing, contract inspection and whale detection across 8 chains.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, authors "Mibayy (@Mibayy), youssefea (@youssefea), ethernet8023 (@ethernet8023), Hermes Agent", MIT, all platforms, tags `EVM, Ethereum, BNB, BSC, Base, Arbitrum, Polygon, Optimism, Avalanche, zkSync, Blockchain, Crypto, Web3, DeFi, NFT, ENS, Whale, Security`, `category: blockchain`, `related_skills: [solana]`, `requires_toolsets: [terminal]`. 212 lines driving `scripts/evm_client.py` (Python 3.8+, standard library only, no pip installs). Data sources: CoinGecko free API for pricing (~10-30 req/min), ensideas.com public API for ENS, 4byte.directory for transaction-input decoding. Script path once installed: `~/.hermes/skills/blockchain/evm/scripts/evm_client.py`. Sections: When to Use · Prerequisites · Quick Reference (Network & prices, Wallet, Tokens & prices, Transactions, Gas, Security, ENS, Whale detection) · Procedure (0. Setup Check, 1. Wallet Portfolio, 2. Multi-Chain Scan, 3. Compare Gas + Prices, 4. Transaction Details & Decode, 5. ENS Resolution, 6. Allowance Checker (Security), 7. Contract Inspector, 8. Whale Detection, 9. Gas Tracker) · Supported Chains · Pitfalls · Verification.
- **Inputs / options:** `python $SCRIPT stats` (Ethereum stats); `stats --chain arbitrum`; `compare` (gas + prices across ALL 8 chains); `wallet <addr>` (ETH + ERC-20 portfolio); `wallet <addr> --chain bsc`; `multichain <addr>` (same wallet on all chains); `price ETH`; `price <contract-address>`; `token <contract>` (ERC-20 metadata + market cap); `tx <hash>`; `decode <hash>` (via 4byte.directory); `activity <addr>`; `gas`; `gas --chain optimism`; plus allowance-checker, contract-inspector and whale-detection commands. Env override `export EVM_RPC_URL=https://your-rpc.com`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Printed reports; no writes; read-only RPC calls only.
- **Config / env:** `EVM_RPC_URL`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Read-only by design — no signing, no transactions. CoinGecko free-tier rate limits. Verification: `stats` should print current block, gas price and ETH price; ENS resolution should map `vitalik.eth` to `0xd8dA…`.
- **Rebuild notes:** A single stdlib-only CLI over public RPC + three free APIs covers most on-chain read questions. A better version would cache prices and support user-supplied RPCs per chain.

### Skill: hyperliquid  `id: optional.skill-hyperliquid`

The script reads `${HERMES_HOME:-~/.hermes}/.env` for two optional defaults and also honours a project `.env` in the cwd. Sections: When to Use · Prerequisites · How to Run · Quick Reference · Procedure (1. Discover DEXs and Markets, 2. Pull Historical Market Data, 3. Inspect Live Order Book, 4. Review an Account, 5. Review Fills and Orders, 6. Generate a Trade Review, 7. Export a Reusable Dataset) · Pitfalls · Verification.
- **Inputs / options:** `python ~/.hermes/skills/blockchain/hyperliquid/scripts/hyperliquid_client.py <command> [args]`; commands include `dexs`, `state`, `spot-balances`, `fills`, `orders`, `review`, and `export`; export flags `--interval 1h|15m`, `--hours 168|72`, `--output ./btc-1h-7d.json`, `--end-time-ms 1760000000000`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Printed reports and normalized JSON export files for backtesting prep.
- **Config / env:** `HYPERLIQUID_API_URL` (default `https://api.hyperliquid.xyz`; set `https://api.hyperliquid-testnet.xyz` for testnet), `HYPERLIQUID_USER_ADDRESS` (default address for `state`, `spot-balances`, `fills`, `orders`, `review`; otherwise pass the address as the first positional argument).
- **Edge cases / guards:** No API key needed; supports builder-deployed perp dexs and HIP-3 markets.
- **Rebuild notes:** Normalized JSON export is the feature that makes a market-data skill reusable downstream.

### Skill: ascii-art  `id: optional.skill-ascii-art`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/creative/ascii-art/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/creative/ascii-art`; catalog row "ASCII art: pyfiglet, cowsay, boxes, image-to-ascii."
- **What it does:** Produces ASCII/text art nine different ways — FIGlet banners locally or over an API, cowsay, boxes, toilet, image-to-ASCII conversion, pre-made art search, curl utilities and an LLM fallback.
- **How it works:** v4.0.0, authors "0xbyt4, Hermes Agent", MIT, `dependencies: []`, all platforms, tags `ASCII, Art, Banners, Creative, Unicode, Text-Art, pyfiglet, figlet, cowsay, boxes`, `related_skills: [excalidraw]`. 323 lines, no bundled scripts — every tool is a shell invocation. Sections: **Tool 1** Text Banners (pyfiglet — local): Setup, Usage, Recommended fonts, Tips · **Tool 2** Text Banners (asciified API — remote, no install): "Free REST API that converts text to ASCII art. 250+ FIGlet fonts. Returns plain text directly — no parsing needed." · **Tool 3** Cowsay (Message Art): Setup, Usage, Available characters (50+), Eye/tongue modifiers · **Tool 4** Boxes (Decorative Borders): Setup, Usage, Combine with pyfiglet or asciified · **Tool 5** TOIlet (Colored Text Art): Setup, Usage, Filters · **Tool 6** Image to ASCII Art: Option A `ascii-image-converter` (recommended, modern), Option B `jp2a` (lightweight, JPEG only) · **Tool 7** Search Pre-Made ASCII Art: Source A ascii.co.uk, Source B GitHub Octocat API (easter egg) · **Tool 8** Fun ASCII Utilities via curl: QR Codes as ASCII Art, Weather as ASCII Art · **Tool 9** LLM-Generated Custom Art (Fallback): Character Palette, Rules · Decision Flow.
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install pyfiglet --break-system-packages -q`; `python -m pyfiglet "YOUR TEXT" -f slant`; `python -m pyfiglet "TEXT" -f doom -w 80` (set width); `python -m pyfiglet --list_fonts` (all 571 fonts); `curl -s "https://asciified.thelicato.io/api/v2/ascii?text=Hello+World"`; `…&font=Slant|Doom|Star+Wars|3-D|Banner3`; `curl -s "https://asciified.thelicato.io/api/v2/fonts"` (JSON array); `sudo apt install cowsay -y` / `brew install cowsay`; `brew install boxes`; `brew install toilet`; `python -m pyfiglet "HERMES" -f slant | boxes -d stone`; `curl -s "https://asciified.thelicato.io/api/v2/ascii?text=HERMES&font=Slant" | boxes -d stone`; `curl -s 'https://ascii.co.uk/art/cat' -o /tmp/ascii_art.html`; `go install github.com/TheZoraiz/ascii-image-converter@latest`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Text art printed to the conversation or written to files; optional installs of `cowsay`, `boxes`, `toilet`, `ascii-image-converter`, `jp2a`, `pyfiglet`.
- **Config / env:** n/a.
- **Edge cases / guards:** The remote asciified API exists precisely so the skill works with nothing installed. The LLM fallback (Tool 9) has an explicit character palette and rules so hand-drawn art stays consistent. A Decision Flow section picks the tool.
- **Rebuild notes:** Offer a local path, a zero-install remote path, and an LLM fallback for the same output — that triple is why this skill always works.

### Skill: audiocraft-audio-generation  `id: optional.skill-audiocraft-audio-generation`

- **What it does:** Generates music from text (MusicGen), sound effects from text (AudioGen), and compresses/reconstructs audio (EnCodec), locally with PyTorch.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [audiocraft, torch>=2.0.0, transformers>=4.30.0]`, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `Multimodal, Audio Generation, Text-to-Music, Text-to-Audio, MusicGen`, `related_skills: [heartmula, songwriting-and-ai-music]`. 570 lines + `references/advanced-usage.md`, `references/troubleshooting.md`. Sections: When to use AudioCraft · Quick start (Installation from PyPI / GitHub / HuggingFace Transformers; Basic text-to-music; Using HuggingFace Transformers; Text-to-sound with AudioGen) · Core concepts (Architecture overview; Model variants; Generation parameters) · MusicGen usage (Text-to-music; Melody-conditioned generation; Stereo generation — wav shape `[batch, 2, samples]`; Audio continuation) · MusicGen-Style usage (Style-conditioned generation; Style-only generation with no text) · AudioGen usage (Sound effect generation) · EnCodec usage (Audio compression: load, resample, encode to tokens, decode back) · Common workflows (1. Music generation pipeline; 2. Sound design batch processing; 3. Gradio demo) · Performance optimization (Memory optimization — smaller model, clear cache between generations, shorter durations, half precision; Batch processing efficiency; GPU memory requirements) · Common issues · References · Resources.
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install audiocraft`; `pip install git+https://github.com/facebookresearch/audiocraft.git`; `pip install transformers torch torchaudio`. Model sizes Small (300M) → Large (3.3B). Generation returns `[batch, channels, samples]`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Generated `.wav` audio files; model downloads.
- **Config / env:** CUDA/MPS device selection; half precision.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Documented GPU memory table — musicgen-small ~4 GB FP32 / ~2 GB FP16; musicgen-medium ~8 GB / ~4 GB; musicgen-large ~16 GB / ~8 GB. Not supported on Windows.
- **Rebuild notes:** Ship the VRAM table and the batch-vs-loop guidance; those are what stop users OOMing.

Tests: `conftest.py`, `pytest.ini`, `test_check_deps.py`, `test_cloud_integration.py`, `test_common.py`, `test_extract_schema.py`, `test_run_workflow.py`. Sections: What's in this skill · When to Use · Architecture: Two Layers · Quick Start (Detect environment; One-line health check) · Core Workflow (Step 1 get a workflow JSON in API format; Step 2 see what's controllable; Step 3 run with parameters; Step 4 present results) · Decision Tree · Setup & Onboarding (Step 0 Ask Local vs Cloud — ALWAYS FIRST; Step 1 Verify Hardware only if local; Choosing an Installation Path; Path A Comfy Cloud; Path B ComfyUI Desktop for Windows/macOS; Path C ComfyUI Portable Windows-only; Path D comfy-cli all platforms, recommended for agents; Path E Manual Install; Post-Install: Download Models; Post-Install: Install Custom Nodes; Post-Install: Verify) · Image Upload (img2img / Inpainting) · Cloud Specifics · Queue & System Management · Pitfalls · Verification Checklist.
- **Inputs / options:** `curl -s http://127.0.0.1:8188/system_stats` (server running?); `python scripts/hardware_check.py` (+ `--json`, `--json --check-pytorch`); `python scripts/health_check.py` (→ JSON: comfy_cli on PATH? server reachable? at least one checkpoint? smoke-test passes?); `python scripts/extract_schema.py workflow_api.json --summary-only` (→ `{"parameter_count": 12, "has_negative_prompt": true, "has_seed": true, …}`) and without `--summary-only` for the full schema with parameters, model deps and embedding refs; `python scripts/run_workflow.py --workflow flux_dev.json --args '{"prompt": "..."}' --ws` (real-time progress via WebSocket, needs `pip install websocket-client`), `--input-image` for img2img/inpaint (uploads + references automatically); `python scripts/run_batch.py …` (e.g. 8 random seeds, parallel up to the cloud tier limit); `bash scripts/comfyui_setup.sh` and `bash scripts/comfyui_setup.sh --m-series --port=8190 --workspace=/data/comfy`; `pipx install comfy-cli`; `export COMFY_CLOUD_API_KEY="comfyui-…"`. Model download recipes for SDXL (~6.5 GB), SD 1.5 (~4 GB, good for 6 GB cards), Flux Dev fp8 (~12 GB) and CivitAI (token first). Upload returns `{"name": "photo.png", "subfolder": "", "type": "input"}`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Generated images/video/audio; a local ComfyUI install and downloaded checkpoints; cloud jobs billed to the API key.
- **Config / env:** `COMFY_CLOUD_API_KEY`; local server default `http://127.0.0.1:8188`; CivitAI token.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "Step 0: Ask Local vs Cloud (ALWAYS FIRST)" — do not start running install commands or hardware checks before the user chooses. Cloud quirks documented: 302 redirect, 1 concurrent free-tier job, 1080p VRAM ceiling. Cloud paths mirror local ones under `/api/`.
- **Rebuild notes:** Split into two layers (a workflow-schema extractor + a runner) so any workflow JSON becomes a parameterized callable. A better version would ship a workflow registry with declared model dependencies and pre-flight download.

### Skill: creative-ideation  `id: optional.skill-creative-ideation`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/creative/creative-ideation/SKILL.md` + 27 reference files; `hermes skills install official/creative/creative-ideation`; catalog row "Generate ideas via named methods from creative practice."
- **What it does:** Routes an open-ended creative prompt to named methods from creative practice and generates ideas under an explicit named constraint instead of defaulting to generic brainstorming.
- **How it works:** v2.1.0, `title: Creative Ideation — Routed Library of Creative Methods`, author "SHL0MS", MIT, all platforms, tags `Creative, Ideation, Brainstorming, Methods, Inspiration`, `category: creative`, `requires_toolsets: []`. 178 lines. Sections: When to use · Operating rules · Routing — 4-step procedure (Step 1 extract three signals from the prompt; Step 2 apply overrides, highest priority, fire first; Step 3 route by phase first then domain; Step 4 handle ambiguity and contradiction; Anti-default check, run before generating) · Output format (`## Constraint: [Name] — from [Source]` then `### Ideas`) · File map · Attribution. References: `anti-slop.md`, `exercises.md`, `full-prompt-library.md`, `heuristics.md`, `method-catalog.md`, plus 22 method files — `affinity-diagrams`, `analogy-and-blending`, `biomimicry`, `chance-and-remix`, `compression-progress`, `creative-discipline`, `defamiliarization`, `derive-and-mapping`, `first-principles`, `jobs-to-be-done`, `lateral-provocations`, `leverage-points`, `oblique-strategies`, `oulipo`, `pataphysics`, `pattern-languages`, `polya`, `premortem-and-inversion`, `scamper`, `story-skeletons`, `triz-principles`, `volume-generation`.
- **Inputs / options:** Any open-ended generative or selective question — "I want to make / build / write / start something", "I'm stuck", "inspire me", "make this weirder", "help me pick", "I need to invent X", "give me a research question".
- **Outputs / side effects:** A response headed `## Constraint: [Name] — from [Source]` with an `### Ideas` list.
- **Config / env:** n/a.
- **Edge cases / guards:** The "Anti-default check" runs before generating to stop the model falling back to generic output; `references/anti-slop.md` codifies what to avoid; overrides fire before routing.
- **Rebuild notes:** A router over a named-method library with a mandatory attribution line makes creative output auditable and repeatable.

### Skill: draw-your-font  `id: optional.skill-draw-your-font`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/creative/draw-your-font/SKILL.md` + `references/troubleshooting.md`; `hermes skills install official/creative/draw-your-font`; catalog row "Turn a handwriting photo into an installable TTF font."
- **What it does:** Converts photographs of handwriting into an installable TTF/WOFF font, entirely locally.
- **How it works:** v0.1.0, author "Danilo Znamerovszkij (…/draw-your-font), ported by Hermes Agent", MIT, all platforms, `required_commands: [node, npx]`, tags `font, handwriting, typography, ttf, woff, vision, creative`, `category: creative`, homepage `https://github.com/danilo-znamerovszkij/draw-your-font`, `related_skills: [pixel-art]`. 155 lines. Sections: Setup in Hermes (once per session) · Decide the flow · Template flow (best quality) · Main flow: photo(s) → font · Refine (conversational iteration) · Legibility report (offer after delivering) · Troubleshooting. Runs the pinned npm package `draw-your-font@0.1.0` through `npx` (Node ≥ 18, no global install).
- **Inputs / options:** `npx -y draw-your-font@0.1.0 --help`; `$DYF template -o template.pdf --charset minimal` (or `spanish`); `$DYF segment photo1.jpg photo2.jpg -d work`; `$DYF build -d work --labels work/labels.json --name "Dan's Hand"`; `$DYF preview -d work --text "minimum mill rn m cl d I l 1 O 0 quick brown fox" -o work/legibility.png`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** `template.pdf`, a working directory with segmented glyphs and `labels.json`, a built font file, and a `legibility.png` preview.
- **Config / env:** none — "Everything runs locally; the user's handwriting never leaves the machine."
- **Edge cases / guards:** Shell variables do not persist between tool calls, so the skill instructs pasting the full `npx -y draw-your-font@0.1.0` command each time instead of relying on `$DYF`. The legibility preview deliberately uses confusable glyph pairs (`minimum mill rn m cl d I l 1 O 0`).
- **Rebuild notes:** Pin the CLI version in the skill; offer a printable template flow for quality and a photo-only flow for convenience.

### Skill: heartmula  `id: optional.skill-heartmula`
- **Surface:** Skill

- **What it does:** Installs and runs HeartMuLa, an open-source music foundation model family, to generate full songs from lyrics plus tags.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Teknium (teknium1), Hermes Agent", MIT, all platforms, tags `music, audio, generation, ai, heartmula, heartcodec, lyrics, songs`, `related_skills: [audiocraft-audio-generation, songwriting-and-ai-music]`. 174 lines. The family (Apache-2.0): **HeartMuLa** music language model (3B/7B) for generation from lyrics + tags; **HeartCodec** 12.5 Hz music codec for high-fidelity reconstruction; **HeartTranscriptor** Whisper-based lyrics transcription; **HeartCLAP** audio-text alignment. Sections: Overview · When to Use · Hardware Requirements · Installation Steps (1. Clone Repository; 2. Create Virtual Environment — Python 3.10 required; 3. Fix Dependency Compatibility Issues — upgrade `datasets` because the old version is incompatible with current pyarrow, upgrade `transformers` for huggingface-hub 1.x compatibility; 4. Patch Source Code required for transformers 5.x — re-initialize RoPE caches that were skipped during meta-device loading; 5. Download Model Checkpoints) · GPU / CUDA · Usage (Basic Generation; Input Formatting; Key Parameters; Performance) · Pitfalls · Links.
- **Inputs / options:** `git clone https://github.com/HeartMuLa/heartlib.git`; `uv venv --python 3.10 .venv`; `uv pip install -e .`; `uv pip install --upgrade datasets`; `uv pip install --upgrade transformers`; `python ./examples/run_music_generation.py …`; flags `--lazy_load true`, `--mula_device cuda:0 --codec_device cuda:1`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Generated song audio; a cloned repo, a venv and downloaded checkpoints.
- **Config / env:** CUDA device assignment.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Hardware — minimum 8 GB VRAM with `--lazy_load true` (loads/unloads models sequentially); recommended 16 GB+ for comfortable single-GPU use; multi-GPU split via `--mula_device`/`--codec_device`; the 3B model with lazy_load peaks at ~6.2 GB VRAM. Two source patches are required for current transformers.
- **Rebuild notes:** When a skill wraps a fast-moving research repo, spell out the exact dependency pins and source patches — that is the part that rots.

### Skill: hyperframes  `id: optional.skill-hyperframes`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/creative/hyperframes/SKILL.md` + 6 references + `scripts/setup.sh`; `hermes skills install official/creative/hyperframes`; catalog row "Render MP4/WebM videos from HTML compositions."
- **What it does:** Turns HTML/CSS/JS compositions (GSAP-animated) into deterministically rendered MP4/WebM videos, with TTS, captions, audio-reactive visuals and scene transitions.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "heygen-com", **Apache-2.0**, all platforms, `prerequisites.commands: [node, ffmpeg, npx]`, tags `creative, video, animation, html, gsap, motion-graphics`, `related_skills: [manim-video, meme-generation]`, `category: creative`, `requires_toolsets: [terminal]`. 209 lines. `scripts/setup.sh` (1) verifies Node.js ≥ 22 and FFmpeg (printing fix instructions if missing), (2) installs the CLI globally with `npm install -g hyperframes@>=0.4.2`, (3) pre-caches `chrome-headless-shell` via Puppeteer — required for best-quality renders. Sections: When to Use · Quick Reference · Setup (one-time) · Procedure (1. Plan before writing HTML; 2. Scaffold; 3. Layout before animation; 4. Animate with GSAP; 5. Transitions between scenes; 6. Audio, captions, TTS, audio-reactive, highlighting; 7. Lint, validate, inspect, preview, render; 8. Website-to-video if the user gives a URL) · Cleanup · Pitfalls · Verification · References (`cli.md`, `composition.md`, `features.md`, `gsap.md`, `troubleshooting.md`, `website-to-video.md`).
- **Inputs / options:** `bash "$(dirname "$(find ~/.hermes/skills -path '*/hyperframes/SKILL.md' | head -1)")/scripts/setup.sh"`; `npx hyperframes init my-video` (scaffold), `--non-interactive`; `npx hyperframes lint` (catches missing `data-composition-id`, overlapping tracks, unregistered timelines); `npx hyperframes validate` (WCAG contrast audit at 5 timestamps); `npx hyperframes inspect` (visual layout audit — overflow, off-frame elements, occluded text); `npx hyperframes preview` (live-reload preview, long-lived server on port 3002); `npx hyperframes render --output final.mp4`; `npx hyperframes render --quality draft --output draft.mp4` (fast iteration); `npx hyperframes doctor` (diagnose environment issues).
- **Outputs / side effects:** A scaffolded project directory, a preview server on port 3002, and rendered MP4/WebM files; a global npm install and a cached headless Chrome.
- **Config / env:** Node ≥ 22, FFmpeg on PATH.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Explicitly not for pure math/equation animation (use manim-video). `preview` is a long-lived server, so it must be managed as a background process. Lint before preview/render.
- **Rebuild notes:** Four separate pre-render checks (`lint`, `validate`, `inspect`, `doctor`) are what make headless video rendering reliable for an agent that cannot see the result.

### Skill: kanban-video-orchestrator  `id: optional.skill-kanban-video-orchestrator`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/creative/kanban-video-orchestrator/SKILL.md` + 3 templates, 6 references, 2 scripts; `hermes skills install official/creative/kanban-video-orchestrator`; catalog row "Plan and run multi-agent video production pipelines."
- **What it does:** Designs and runs a multi-agent video production pipeline on the Hermes kanban board — profiles as crew roles, tasks as a graph, with live monitoring and intervention.

Sections: When NOT to use this skill · Workflow (Step 1 Discover — ask the right questions; Step 2 Brief; Step 3 Team design; Step 4 Setup; Step 5 Execute; Step 6 Monitor and intervene) · Reference: worked examples · Critical rules · File map. Step 4 generates and runs a `setup.sh` that (1) creates the project workspace `~/projects/video-pipeline/<slug>/`, (2) copies provided assets into `taste/`, `audio/`, `assets/`, (3) creates each Hermes profile via `hermes profile create --clone`, (4) writes a per-profile `SOUL.md` (personality + role definition), (5) configures profile YAML (toolsets, `always_load` skills, cwd), (6) writes the kanban tasks. Bundled: `assets/brief.md.tmpl`, `assets/setup.sh.tmpl`, `assets/soul.md.tmpl`, `references/{examples,intake,kanban-setup,monitoring,role-archetypes,tool-matrix}.md`, `scripts/bootstrap_pipeline.py`, `scripts/monitor.py`.
- **Inputs / options:** `hermes kanban watch --tenant <project-tenant>` (live events); `hermes kanban list --tenant <project-tenant>` (board snapshot); `hermes dashboard` (visual board UI); the generated `setup.sh`; `--workspace dir:/abs/path` discipline.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A project workspace, N new Hermes profiles with SOUL.md files and patched YAML, a populated kanban board, and rendered video artifacts.
- **Config / env:** kanban tenant; per-profile toolsets and `always_load` skills.
- **Edge cases / guards:** The skill opens with "When NOT to use this skill" and carries a "Critical rules" section; the `--workspace dir:/abs/path` discipline prevents profiles from colliding on each other's files.
- **Rebuild notes:** Model a production crew as profiles + a task graph on an existing board rather than inventing a new orchestrator; the reusable bits are `SOUL.md`-per-profile and the tool matrix mapping roles to skills.

Sections: When to Use · Available Templates (Curated Templates with custom text placement; Dynamic Templates from the imgflip API) · Procedure (Mode 1: Classic Template (default); Mode 2: Custom AI Image, when `image_generate` is available) · Examples · Listing Templates · Pitfalls · Verification. Text overlay via Pillow; template metadata in `scripts/templates.json`.

Worked examples: `this-is-fine /tmp/meme.png "SERVERS ARE ON FIRE" "This is fine"`; `drake /tmp/meme.png "Getting 8 hours of sleep" "One more episode at 3 AM"`; `expanding-brain /tmp/meme.png "Setting an alarm" "Setting 5 alarms" "Sleeping through all alarms" "Working from bed"`.

### Skill: simple-english  `id: optional.skill-simple-english`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/creative/simple-english/SKILL.md` + `references/{checklist,use-cases}.md`; `hermes skills install official/creative/simple-english`; catalog row "Rewrite text to ASD-STE100 Simplified Technical English."
- **What it does:** Rewrites text into ASD-STE100 Simplified Technical English — the aerospace maintenance-manual standard — using a transcribed rule catalog.
- **How it works:** v1.2.0, author "AminBlg (https://github.com/AminBlg/SimpleEnglish), ported by Hermes Agent", MIT, all platforms, tags `writing, documentation, ste, asd-ste100, technical-writing, editing, anti-ai-slop`, `category: creative`, homepage `https://github.com/AminBlg/SimpleEnglish`, `related_skills: [humanizer]`, and `metadata.standard: ASD-STE100 Issue 9 (2025-01-15)`. 348 lines. Sections: How to use it in Hermes · Your Task · Two Modes · Step 1: Classify the Text · **THE RULE CATALOG** — Section 1 Words (Rules 1.1–1.14), Section 2 Multi-word nouns (2.1–2.2), Section 3 Verbs (3.1–3.7), Section 4 Sentences (4.1–4.5), Section 5 Procedural writing (5.1–5.5), Section 6 Descriptive writing (6.1–6.6), Section 7 Safety instructions (7.1–7.3), Section 8 Punctuation and word count (8.1–8.7), Section 9 Writing practices (9.1–9.4, GR-1 to GR-8) · VOCABULARY DISCIPLINE (The modal ladder; Slop-to-simple substitutions; Consistency pass) · Untouchables · Beyond Documentation · Self-Check Before You Deliver · Full Example · Limits · References.
- **Inputs / options:** The text to rewrite; one of the two modes; the classification result from Step 1 decides which rule sections apply.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Rewritten text.
- **Config / env:** n/a.
- **Edge cases / guards:** An "Untouchables" section lists what must not be changed (quoted material, identifiers, safety-critical wording); "Limits" states where STE is inappropriate; a Self-Check runs before delivery.
- **Rebuild notes:** Transcribing the numbered rule catalog verbatim (rather than paraphrasing it) is what makes the rewrite auditable against the standard.

### Skill: sketch  `id: optional.skill-sketch`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/creative/sketch/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/creative/sketch`; catalog row "Throwaway HTML mockups: 2-3 design variants to compare."
- **What it does:** Produces two or three genuinely different HTML design variants of a screen so the user can compare stances, rather than one polished guess.
- **How it works:** v1.0.1, author "Hermes Agent (adapted from gsd-build/get-shit-done)", MIT, all platforms, tags `sketch, mockup, design, ui, prototype, html, variants, exploration, wireframe, comparison`, `related_skills: [spike, claude-design, popular-web-designs, excalidraw]`. 221 lines. Sections: When NOT to use this · If the user has the full GSD system installed · Core method (1. Intake — skip if the user already gave enough; 2. Variants — 2-3, never 1, rarely 4+; 3. Make them real HTML; 4. Variant README with the template `## Variant: {stance name}` → `### Design stance`, `### Key choices`, `### Trade-offs`, `### Best for`; 5. Head-to-head, e.g. `## Three takes on the home screen`) · Theming (when the project has a visual identity) · Interactivity bar · Frontier mode (picking what to sketch next) · Output · Attribution.
- **Inputs / options:** The screen/idea to sketch; optional project theme; frontier mode.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Throwaway HTML files plus a variant README and a head-to-head comparison.
- **Config / env:** n/a.
- **Edge cases / guards:** If `gsd-sketch` is installed (via `npx get-shit-done-cc --hermes`), the skill defers to it for the fuller workflow — persistent `.planning/sketches/` with MANIFEST, frontier-mode analysis, consistency audits across past sketches and GSD integration; this skill is the lightweight standalone version. "Never 1, rarely 4+" is an explicit rule.
- **Rebuild notes:** Forcing a named *design stance* per variant is what prevents three cosmetically different versions of the same idea.

### Skill: tldraw-offline  `id: optional.skill-tldraw-offline`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/creative/tldraw-offline/SKILL.md` + `scripts/{counter.js,main.js,validate_shapes.mjs}`; `hermes skills install official/creative/tldraw-offline`; catalog row "Drive and script tldraw offline canvases with an agent."
- **What it does:** Scripts a running tldraw offline canvas over its local HTTP API — generating shapes programmatically, embedding a document script for durable behavior, and shipping a self-running `.tldraw` file.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Teknium + Hermes Agent", MIT, all platforms, tags `tldraw, canvas, whiteboard, document-script, diagramming`, `category: creative`. 279 lines. Sections: When to Use · Prerequisites · How to Run (find the focused document id; run code with the live `editor` + `helpers` in scope; get the live script file path for the doc; edit `result.mainJsPath` with read_file/patch/write_file per `scripts/main.js`; confirm the watcher applied it) · Quick Reference · Interactive UI (clickable buttons that drive state) — including "Shipping a self-running scripted `.tldraw`" · Procedure · Shape props (validated against tldraw SDK v5 schema) · Pitfalls · Verification. Prerequisites: tldraw offline installed and running with a document open (releases at `https://github.com/tldraw/tldraw-offline/releases/latest` — macOS DMG, Windows x64/Arm64, Linux `x86_64`/`arm64` AppImage or amd64/arm64 `.deb`), and agent skills installed in the app via **Develop → Install Agent Skills**, which writes the app's own tldraw skill into `~/.codex/skills/`, `~/.claude/skills/`, `~/.cursor/skills/` and siblings.
- **Inputs / options:** `curl -s "$BASE/api/doc/$DOC/exec" -X POST -H 'content-type: application/json' -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" …`; `curl -s "$BASE/api/doc/$DOC/script-workspace" -X POST …`; `curl -s "$BASE/api/doc/$DOC/script-status" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN"`; document script entry point `export default function ({ editor, helpers, signal }) { … }`; `scripts/validate_shapes.mjs` to check shape props against the SDK v5 schema.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Shapes created/modified on the live canvas; a persisted document script (`main.js`) that runs whenever the document is open; a distributable `.tldraw` file.
- **Config / env:** tldraw offline base URL and bearer token.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Explicit rule: "Do NOT hand-place shapes to imitate a drawing — write the code that generates them. Agents are far better at scripting the canvas than at drawing on it." Script edits must be confirmed applied by the watcher via `script-status`.
- **Rebuild notes:** Exposing an `exec` endpoint with the live editor in scope turns a GUI app into an agent-drivable one; validating shape props against the real schema prevents silent no-ops.

### Skill: touchdesigner-mcp  `id: optional.skill-touchdesigner-mcp`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/creative/touchdesigner-mcp/SKILL.md` + 21 references + `scripts/setup.sh`; `hermes skills install official/creative/touchdesigner-mcp`; catalog row "Control TouchDesigner via twozero MCP."
- **What it does:** Drives TouchDesigner from Hermes through the twozero MCP server — building operator chains, setting parameters, wiring, running GLSL, doing audio-reactive visuals and recording video.
- **How it works:** v1.1.0, author "kshitijk4poor", MIT, all platforms, tags `TouchDesigner, MCP, twozero, creative-coding, real-time-visuals, generative-art, audio-reactive, VJ, installation, GLSL`, `related_skills: [ascii-video, manim-video]`. 357 lines. `scripts/setup.sh` (1) checks whether TD is running, (2) downloads `twozero.tox` if not cached, (3) adds the `twozero_td` MCP server to the Hermes config if missing, (4) tests the MCP connection on port 40404, (5) reports the remaining manual steps (drag the `.tox` into TD, enable it). Sections: CRITICAL RULES · Architecture · Setup (Automated) + Manual steps (one-time, cannot be automated) · Environment Notes · Workflow (Step 0 Discover before building anything; Step 1 Clean + Build with `td_execute_python` and a wire chain; Step 2 Set Parameters; Step 3 Wire; Step 4 Verify; Step 5 Display / Capture) · MCP Tool Quick Reference · Key Implementation Rules (call `td_get_par_info(op_type="glslTOP")` first to confirm param names, then set an expression via script — `op('/project1/shader').par.value0.expr = "absTime.seconds"` with `uniform float uTime;` in GLSL) · Recording / Exporting Video (via `td_execute_python`; `rec.par.record = False` to stop, called separately later) + Before Recording: Checklist · Audio-Reactive GLSL (Proven Recipe) — correct signal chain tested April 2026, critical empirically-verified rules, GLSL spectrum sampling · Operator Quick Reference · Security Notes · References (`3d-scene`, `animation`, `audio-reactive`, `dat-scripting`, `external-data`, `geometry-comp`, `glsl`, `layout-compositor`, `mcp-tools`, `midi-osc`, `network-patterns`, `operator-tips`, `operators`, `panel-ui`, `particles`, `pitfalls`, `postfx`, `projection-mapping`, `python-api`, `replicator`, `troubleshooting`).
- **Inputs / options:** `bash "${HERMES_HOME:-$HOME/.hermes}/skills/creative/touchdesigner-mcp/scripts/setup.sh"`; the `twozero_td` MCP tools (`td_execute_python`, `td_get_par_info`, …) documented in `references/mcp-tools.md`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Operators created and wired inside the running TouchDesigner project; recorded video files; an added MCP server entry in the Hermes config.
- **Config / env:** MCP server `twozero_td` on port 40404.
- **Edge cases / guards:** A CRITICAL RULES section leads the file; some setup steps cannot be automated (dragging the `.tox` in and enabling it); the audio-reactive recipe is marked as empirically verified with a dated test; a Security Notes section covers arbitrary Python execution inside TD.
- **Rebuild notes:** Confirm parameter names via an introspection tool before writing values — GUI-app APIs rename parameters between versions and silent failures are the default.

### Skill: unreal-mcp  `id: optional.skill-unreal-mcp`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/creative/unreal-mcp/SKILL.md` + `references/{advanced-workflows,pitfalls,recipes,scene-craft,tool-surface}.md`; `hermes skills install official/creative/unreal-mcp`; catalog row "Automate Unreal Engine editor scenes, actors, and renders." Pairs with the MCP catalog entry `optional.mcp-unreal-engine`.
- **What it does:** Automates the Unreal Editor through Epic's Unreal MCP plugin — building and dressing levels, spawning/moving/deleting actors, lighting and atmosphere, material instances, camera framing, screenshots and renders, asset import, scene/UI inspection, automation tests and editor scripting.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Hermes Agent", MIT, all platforms, top-level `requires: Unreal Editor 5.8+ with the Unreal MCP plugin enabled and its server running`, top-level `tags` duplicated into `metadata.hermes.tags`: `unreal, unreal-engine, ue5, 3d, mcp, scenes, cinematics, lighting, gamedev`. 255 lines. Sections: When to Use · Prerequisites (One-time, editor side; One-time, Hermes side; Every session — "the editor side must be up before Hermes connects") · **The Tool Surface: Discovery, Not a Fixed List** · Operating Loop · From Plain English to a Scene · Reference Files · Pitfalls (top of mind — full list in `references/pitfalls.md`) · Verification Checklist.
- **Inputs / options:** The MCP tools exposed by the running Unreal MCP server, discovered at runtime rather than hard-coded; the recipes in `references/recipes.md`; scene-craft guidance in `references/scene-craft.md`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Actors, lights, materials and cameras created inside the live Unreal project; screenshots and rendered frames on disk.
- **Config / env:** MCP server URL (default `http://127.0.0.1:8000/mcp`, see `optional.mcp-unreal-engine`).
- **Edge cases / guards:** Not for DCC-style mesh modelling/sculpting (model in Blender and import). Order matters — the editor's server must be running before Hermes starts, or the tools are never probed. Epic ships the plugin as EXPERIMENTAL and runs tool calls serially on the game thread, so overlapping calls must be avoided.
- **Rebuild notes:** Treating the tool surface as *discovered* rather than fixed is the right call for a plugin whose API changes per engine version.

### Skill: jupyter-notebook  `id: optional.skill-jupyter-notebook`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/data-science/jupyter-notebook/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/data-science/jupyter-notebook`; catalog row "Iterative Python via live Jupyter kernel (hamelnb)."
- **What it does:** Runs Python iteratively against a live Jupyter kernel so state persists between steps — execute code, inspect live variables, edit notebook cells, and verify with restart-and-run-all.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Hermes Agent", MIT, all platforms, tags `jupyter, notebook, repl, data-science, exploration, iterative`, `category: data-science`. 179 lines. Drives `hamelnb`'s `jupyter_live_kernel.py`, expected at `$HOME/.agent-skills/hamelnb/skills/jupyter-live-kernel/scripts/jupyter_live_kernel.py`. Sections: When to Use This vs Other Tools · Prerequisites · Setup (Starting JupyterLab; Creating a Notebook for REPL Use) · Core Workflow (1. Discover servers and notebooks; 2. Execute code — the primary operation; 3. Inspect live variables; 4. Edit notebook cells — view current cells, insert a new cell, replace cell source using the cell-id from contents output, delete a cell; 5. Verification — restart + run all) · Practical Tips from Experience · Timeout Defaults. Routing table: **this skill** for iterative exploration, state across steps, data science, ML, "let me try this and check"; `execute_code` for one-shot scripts needing Hermes tool access (web_search, file ops) — stateless; `terminal` for shell commands, builds, installs, git, process management. Rule of thumb: "If you'd want a Jupyter notebook for the task, use this skill."
- **Inputs / options:** `git clone https://github.com/hamelsmu/hamelnb.git ~/.agent-skills/hamelnb`; `uv tool install jupyterlab`; `uv run "$SCRIPT" servers` / `servers --compact`; `notebooks --compact`; `execute --path <notebook.ipynb> --code '<python code>' --compact` (example: `execute --path scratch.ipynb --code $'import os\nfiles = os.listdir(".")\nprint(f"Found {len(files)} files")' --compact`); `variables --path <nb> list --compact`; `variables --path <nb> preview --name <varname> --compact`; `contents --path <nb> --compact`; `edit --path <nb> insert …`; `edit --path <nb> replace-source …`; `edit --path <nb> delete …`; plus `curl -s -X POST http://127.0.0.1:8888/api/sessions …` to create a session.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Executed cells and persisted notebook files; a running JupyterLab server on 127.0.0.1:8888.
- **Config / env:** `uv` on PATH; JupyterLab installed as a uv tool.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Documented Timeout Defaults section; `--compact` output everywhere to keep the model's context small; verification requires restart + run all, because live-kernel state hides broken notebooks.
- **Rebuild notes:** The "when to use this vs `execute_code` vs `terminal`" table is the most valuable part — routing is what an agent gets wrong.

### Skill: actual-setup  `id: optional.skill-actual-setup`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/devops/actual-setup/SKILL.md` + `references/opencode.md`; `hermes skills install official/devops/actual-setup`; catalog row "Set up Actual Computer (actual.inc) inference in Hermes."
