- **How it works:** v2.0.0, authors "shl0ms + Hermes Agent", MIT, all platforms, tags `actual, actual-inc, provider, local-inference, relay, gguf, setup`, `category: devops`. 148 lines. Actual "turns the user's own hardware into a private inference cluster and exposes an OpenAI-compatible API two ways: a hosted end-to-end-encrypted relay at `https://api.actual.inc` (authenticated with an `ac_` key), and a local on-device daemon at `http://127.0.0.1:8080` (no auth on loopback)". Sections: When to Use · Prerequisites · How to Run (Relay / API mode; Local mode) · Quick Reference · Pitfalls · Verification.
- **Inputs / options:** `curl -s https://api.actual.inc/v1/models -H "Authorization: Bearer $ACTUAL_API_KEY"` (model discovery); `hermes config set model.provider actual`; `hermes config set model.default "MODEL_ID_FROM_DISCOVERY"` / `"INSTALLED_MODEL_NAME"`; `hermes chat -Q -q "Reply with exactly: ACTUAL_OK" --provider actual -m MODEL_ID`; `hermes chat -Q -q "Reply with exactly: LOCAL_OK" --provider actual -m INSTALLED_NAME -t file,web` (local small model with a reduced toolset); `hermes status`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** `model.provider` / `model.default` written to config; requests routed to Actual.
- **Config / env:** `ACTUAL_API_KEY` (`ac_…`), `model.provider`, `model.default`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Local no-auth mode shows `key_source=local-offline` in provider status. Small local models need a reduced toolset (`-t file,web`) or they fail. Troubleshooting covers "cryptic 400s or empty streams".
- **Rebuild notes:** Two verification one-liners (relay + local) that assert an exact reply string is a cheap, decisive provider smoke test.

### Skill: docker-management  `id: optional.skill-docker-management`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/devops/docker-management/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/devops/docker-management`; catalog row "Manage Docker containers, images, volumes, and Compose."
- **What it does:** Gives the agent a full Docker operating manual — containers, images, Compose stacks, volumes, networks, disk usage and Dockerfile review.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "sprmn24", MIT, all platforms, tags `docker, containers, devops, infrastructure, compose, images, volumes, networks, debugging`, `category: devops`, `requires_toolsets: [terminal]`. 283 lines, no scripts. Sections: When to Use · Prerequisites · Quick Reference · Procedure (1. Identify the domain; 2. Container operations — detached service with port mapping, with environment variables, with persistent data via named volume, development bind mount, interactive debugging with auto-remove, resource limits and restart policy; 3. Image management — build, pull and push, inspect, cleanup; 4. Docker Compose — start/stop, monitoring, interaction, validation; 5. Volumes and networks; 6. Disk usage and cleanup — check what's using space, targeted cleanup (safe), aggressive cleanup (confirm with user first!)) · Pitfalls · Verification · Dockerfile Optimization Tips.
- **Inputs / options:** `docker --version && docker compose version`; `docker run -d --name web -p 8080:80 nginx`; `docker run -d -e POSTGRES_PASSWORD=secret -e POSTGRES_DB=mydb --name db postgres:16`; `docker run -d -v pgdata:/var/lib/postgresql/data --name db postgres:16`; `docker run -d -v $(pwd)/src:/app/src -p 3000:3000 --name dev my-app`; `docker run -it --rm ubuntu:22.04 /bin/bash`; `docker run -d --memory=512m --cpus=1.5 --restart=unless-stopped --name app my-app`; `docker ps`, `docker ps -a`, `docker stop NAME`, `docker start NAME`, `docker restart NAME`, plus the build/pull/push/inspect/cleanup, compose, volume, network and disk-usage command sets in the Quick Reference.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Real container/image/volume/network state changes on the host.
- **Config / env:** Docker Engine running; user in the `docker` group (or `sudo`); Compose v2.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Aggressive cleanup is explicitly gated on confirming with the user first.
- **Rebuild notes:** Split destructive cleanup into "targeted (safe)" and "aggressive (confirm first)" so an agent can act without a blanket approval prompt.

### Skill: hermes-s6-container-supervision  `id: optional.skill-hermes-s6-container-supervision`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/devops/hermes-s6-container-supervision/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/devops/hermes-s6-container-supervision`; catalog row "Modify or debug s6 services in the Hermes Docker image."
- **What it does:** Explains and debugs the s6-overlay supervision tree inside the Hermes Docker image — static services, per-profile gateway services, `cont-init.d` boot scripts and the profile reconciler.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Hermes Agent", MIT, `platforms: [linux]`, top-level `environments: [s6]`, tags `docker, s6, supervision, gateway, profiles`, `related_skills: [hermes-agent]`. 180 lines. Sections: When to use this skill · Architecture at a glance · Key files · Why Architecture B (CMD as main program, not s6-supervised) · Quick recipes (Verify s6 is PID 1 in a running container; Inspect a profile gateway service — "/command/ isn't on docker-exec PATH — use absolute path", with svstat readings "up (pid …) … seconds" = running, "down (exitcode N) … seconds, normally up, want up, …" = s6 wants it up but the process keeps exiting (crash loop), "down … normally up, ready …" = user stopped it; Bring a service up/down manually; Watch the cont-init reconciler log with sample lines `2026-05-21T06:18:05+0000 profile=coder prior_state=running action=started` and `… profile=writer prior_state=stopped action=registered`; Add a new static service; Change the per-profile gateway run command; Run the docker test harness — "Expect 19 passed, 0 xfailed against the s6 image") · Common pitfalls ("command not found" via `docker exec`; Profile directory ownership; Files written by `docker exec` are root-owned; Service slot exists but s6-svstat says "s6-supervise not running"; Gateway starts then immediately exits (`down (exitcode 1)` in svstat); Reconciler skipped a profile; "Help, the container exits 143!") · Related skills.
- **Inputs / options:** `docker exec <c> sh -c 'cat /proc/1/comm; readlink /proc/1/exe'`; `docker exec <c> /command/s6-svstat /run/service/gateway-<name>`; `docker exec <c> /command/s6-svc -u /run/service/gateway-<name>` (up); `-d` (down); `-t` (SIGTERM/restart); `docker exec <c> tail -n 50 /opt/data/logs/container-boot.log`; `docker build -t hermes-agent-harness:latest .`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Services started/stopped inside a running container; test-harness results.
- **Config / env:** Container CMD `/opt/hermes/docker/main-wrapper.sh`; boot log `/opt/data/logs/container-boot.log`; service dir `/run/service/gateway-<name>`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Linux + s6 environment only. `/command/` is not on the `docker exec` PATH, so absolute paths are mandatory. Exit code 143 is SIGTERM, not a crash.
- **Rebuild notes:** Document the three distinct svstat readings and what each means — that mapping is what turns a supervision tree from opaque to debuggable.

### Skill: pinggy-tunnel  `id: optional.skill-pinggy-tunnel`
- **Surface:** Skill

Sections: When to Use · Prerequisites · Quick Reference (plain HTTP/HTTPS tunnel for port 8000 on the free tier; TCP tunnel for databases and raw SSH; TLS tunnel — "Pinggy can't decrypt — bring your own certs at origin"; Basic auth gate `b:user:pass`; Bearer token gate `k:token`; IP whitelist `w:CIDR`; Enable CORS + force HTTPS redirect; Pro tier — persistent URL, no 60-min cap) · Procedure — Start a Tunnel and Get the URL (1. Confirm a local origin is up — expect HTTP/1.x 200 or any non-connection-refused response; 2. Launch the tunnel as a background process; 3. Parse the URL out of the log; 4. Verify; 5. Teardown, including the fallback if the pid file is lost) · Access Control via Username Keywords · Web Debugger (optional) · Pitfalls · Recipes (1 — Receive a webhook callback: a tiny capturing server appending every request to `/tmp/webhook-hits.log`, tunnelled behind a bearer-token gate "so randos can't pollute the capture log", then watch hits land; 2 — Expose an MCP server over HTTP/SSE, e.g. a FastMCP server on port 8765, with a bearer token because "MCP traffic should not be open to the internet"; 3 — Expose a local LLM endpoint (Ollama / vLLM / llama.cpp) with the model server already on `127.0.0.1:11434`; 4 — Share a dev server with a one-shot password, URL printed to the terminal, Ctrl-C to tear down) · Verification (end-to-end: spin up a trivial origin, tunnel it, hit it, tear down).
- **Inputs / options:** SSH-based tunnel invocations with username keywords `b:user:pass` (basic auth), `k:token` (bearer), `w:CIDR` (IP whitelist), CORS and HTTPS-redirect flags; `curl -sI http://127.0.0.1:8000/ | head -1`; `curl -sI https://<the-url>/ | head -3`; `curl -s "$URL/api/tags" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" | head`; `python -m http.server 18000 --bind 127.0.0.1 >/tmp/origin.log 2>&1 &`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A public HTTPS/TCP/TLS endpoint pointing at a local port; a background ssh process and a pid file.
- **Config / env:** `PINGGY_TOKEN` for Pro features (persistent subdomain, custom domain, multiple tunnels, no 60-minute cap); free tier needs no credentials.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Only `ssh` on PATH is required — no binary install. Free-tier tunnels expire after 60 minutes; if the host already has `cloudflared`, the skill defers to the `cloudflared-quick-tunnel` skill because Cloudflare quick tunnels do not expire. Pinggy returns a URL even before the origin is up, which then 502s.
- **Rebuild notes:** Encoding access control in the SSH username keyword is the trick that makes a zero-install tunnel safe enough to use for webhooks.

### Skill: setup-wizard-generator  `id: optional.skill-setup-wizard-generator`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/devops/setup-wizard-generator/SKILL.md` + `templates/template.sh`; `hermes skills install official/devops/setup-wizard-generator`; catalog row "Generate a bash wizard guiding a human through manual setup."
- **What it does:** Generates an interactive bash wizard that walks a human through a manual procedure — opening each URL, saying exactly what to click and copy, capturing values, writing them where they belong, confirming at every stage, and showing how many stages remain.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Matt Pocock (mattpocock/skills, wizard) + Hermes Agent", MIT, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `wizard, setup, onboarding, credentials, secrets, migration, bash, human-in-the-loop`. 117 lines + a bash template. Sections: When to Use · Prerequisites · Procedure (1. Scope the procedure — "Work out every manual step the human must take and every value captured along the way. Read the repo first, don't ask cold" starting from `.env`, `.env.example`, …; 2. Map each stage's journey; 3. Author the wizard; 4. Verify and hand off) · Pitfalls · Verification.
- **Inputs / options:** The procedure to encode; `templates/template.sh` as the scaffold; target sinks such as `.env` and GitHub secrets.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A runnable `wizard.sh` that writes captured values into `.env` / CI secrets.
- **Config / env:** n/a.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "Do NOT use for steps the agent can perform itself — do those directly." Intended for provisioning third-party services (Stripe, Supabase, DNS, OAuth apps), credentials/CI secrets, and irreversible human-gated cutovers, or anything the user will hand to a teammate.
- **Rebuild notes:** Generating a human-runnable script (rather than a checklist) makes the handoff reproducible and the captured values land in the right file automatically.

### Skill: watchers  `id: optional.skill-watchers`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/devops/watchers/SKILL.md` + `scripts/{_watermark,watch_github,watch_http_json,watch_rss}.py`; `hermes skills install official/devops/watchers`; catalog row "Poll RSS, JSON APIs, and GitHub with watermark dedup."
- **What it does:** Polls RSS/Atom feeds, GitHub repos and arbitrary JSON endpoints on a cron schedule and reports only genuinely new items, using a persisted watermark for deduplication.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Hermes Agent", MIT, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `cron, polling, rss, github, http, automation, monitoring`, `category: devops`, `requires_toolsets: [terminal]`. 113 lines. Sections: When to Use · Mental model · Ready-made scripts · Usage · Wiring into cron · State files · Writing your own · Common Pitfalls. `scripts/_watermark.py` is the shared dedup helper; each watcher stores its high-water mark in a state file so a re-run reports nothing when nothing changed.
- **Inputs / options:** `python $HERMES_HOME/skills/devops/watchers/scripts/watch_rss.py …`; `… watch_github.py …` (issues / pulls / releases / commits); `… watch_http_json.py …`; plus the cron wiring described in "Wiring into cron".
- **Outputs / side effects:** State files holding watermarks; notifications for new items only.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_HOME` for script and state paths; cron entries.
- **Edge cases / guards:** The whole point of the watermark is that a cron-driven watcher must be idempotent; the "Writing your own" section shows how to reuse `_watermark.py` for a new source.
- **Rebuild notes:** Ship the dedup primitive separately from the sources — that is what makes adding a fourth watcher trivial.

Sections: Why This Works · How to Use · Step 1: Define the Persona (Good Persona Example; Bad Persona Example) · Step 2: Become the Asshole (Browse as the Persona) · Step 3: The Rant (Write Feedback in Character) · **Step 4: The Pragmatism Filter (Critical — Do Not Skip)** with explicit Filter Criteria · Step 5: Create Tickets · Step 6: Report · Tips · Example Personas by Industry · Rules.
- **Inputs / options:** A product/URL to test; a persona definition; the filter criteria; a ticket sink.
- **Outputs / side effects:** An in-character rant, a filtered issue list, and created tickets plus a report.
- **Config / env:** n/a (browsing via the browser toolset).
- **Edge cases / guards:** Step 4 is marked "Critical — Do Not Skip" precisely because an unfiltered adversarial rant produces unactionable noise; the skill ships both a good and a bad persona example.
- **Rebuild notes:** Pair every adversarial generation step with a mandatory pragmatism filter, or the output is unusable.

### Skill: dcf-model  `id: optional.skill-dcf-model`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/finance/dcf-model/SKILL.md` (1,271 lines — the largest bundled skill) + `TROUBLESHOOTING.md`, `requirements.txt`, `scripts/validate_dcf.py`; `hermes skills install official/finance/dcf-model`; catalog row "Build discounted cash flow valuation workbooks in Excel."
- **What it does:** Builds an institutional-quality DCF equity valuation in Excel, from data retrieval through WACC, terminal value, the enterprise-to-equity bridge and a sensitivity table.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Anthropic (adapted by Nous Research)", Apache-2.0, all platforms, tags `finance, valuation, dcf, excel, openpyxl, modeling, investment-banking`, `related_skills: [excel-author, pptx-author, comps-analysis, lbo-model, 3-statement-model]`. Sections: Environment · Overview · Tools · Critical Constraints - Read These First · DCF Process Workflow (Step 1 Data Retrieval and Validation; Step 2 Historical Analysis 3-5 years; Step 3 Build Revenue Projections; Step 4 Operating Expense Modeling; Step 5 Free Cash Flow Calculation; Step 6 Cost of Capital (WACC) Research; Step 7 Discount Rate Application 5-10 Year Forecast; Step 8 Terminal Value Calculation; Step 9 Enterprise to Equity Value Bridge; Step 10 Sensitivity Analysis) · a set of **Correct** patterns (Scenario Block Selection Pattern; Revenue Projection Pattern; FCF Formula Pattern; Cell Comment Format; Assumption Table Structure; Row Planning Process; Sensitivity Table Implementation with pseudocode) · a matching set of **WRONG** anti-patterns (Simplified Sensitivity Table Approximations or Placeholder Text; Missing Cell Comments; Formula Row References Off; Single Row for Each Assumption Across Scenarios; No Borders; Wrong Font Colors or No Font Color Distinction; Operating Expenses Based on Gross Profit) · TOP 5 ERRORS SUMMARY · WACC Calculation Errors · Growth Assumption Flaws · Terminal Value Mistakes · Cash Flow Projection Errors · Excel File Creation · Quality Rubric · Input Requirements (Minimum Required Inputs) · Excel Model Structure (Sheet Architecture; Formula Recalculation (MANDATORY); Formatting Standards; Border Standards (REQUIRED for Professional Appearance); DCF Sheet Detailed Structure; WACC Sheet Structure; Sensitivity Analysis at the bottom of the DCF sheet).
- **Inputs / options:** `python recalc.py [path_to_excel_file] [timeout_seconds]`, e.g. `python recalc.py AAPL_DCF_Model_2025-10-12.xlsx 30`; `scripts/validate_dcf.py` for formula validation; the Minimum Required Inputs list.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A `.xlsx` DCF model with a DCF sheet, a WACC sheet and an embedded sensitivity table, recalculated headlessly before delivery.
- **Config / env:** `requirements.txt` dependencies; openpyxl + a headless recalc engine.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Formula recalculation is MANDATORY (openpyxl writes formulas but not cached values); the WRONG-pattern gallery exists because these are the failure modes an LLM actually produces; a Quality Rubric grades the output.
- **Rebuild notes:** Pair every "correct pattern" with the concrete wrong version — showing the failure mode is what stops it recurring. Mandatory headless recalculation before delivery is non-negotiable for openpyxl output.

- **Inputs / options:** `python $SCRIPT quote AAPL`; `quote AAPL MSFT GOOGL TSLA`; `search "Tesla"`; `history NVDA --range 6mo`; `compare AAPL MSFT GOOGL`; `crypto BTC ETH SOL`; installed path `python ~/.hermes/skills/finance/stocks/scripts/stocks_client.py quote AAPL`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Printed quotes/history; read-only.
- **Config / env:** Optional `ALPHA_VANTAGE_KEY` to enrich `market_cap`, `pe_ratio` and 52-week levels "when Yahoo's crumb-protected fields come back null" (free key at https://www.alphavantage.co/support/#api-key).
- **Edge cases / guards:** Yahoo's crumb protection makes some fields null; the Alpha Vantage fallback exists specifically for those three field groups.

Configure server.properties with three sizing profiles — "2 players, beefy machine", "4-6 players, moderate machine", "8+ players or weaker hardware"; 6. Tune JVM Args in `user_jvm_args.txt`; 7. Open Firewall; 8. Create Launch Script; 9. Set Up Automated Backups) · Pitfalls · Verification.

### Skill: pokemon-player  `id: optional.skill-pokemon-player`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/gaming/pokemon-player/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/gaming/pokemon-player`; catalog row "Play Pokemon via headless emulator + RAM reads."
- **What it does:** Plays Game Boy Pokémon titles autonomously through a headless PyBoy emulator, reading game state from RAM, taking screenshots, and recording progress to memory.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, authors "Teknium (teknium1), Hermes Agent", MIT, top-level `tags: [gaming, pokemon, emulator, pyboy, gameplay, gameboy]`, all platforms. 221 lines. Backed by `NousResearch/pokemon-agent` on GitHub (clone, Python 3.10+ venv via `uv` preferred with the `pyboy` extra installed editable, falling back to `python -m venv` + pip; reuses an existing checkout such as `~/pokemon-agent`). Sections: When to Use · Startup Procedure (1. First-time setup — clone, venv, install; 2. Start the game server; 3. Set up live dashboard for user to watch) · Save and Load (When to save; How to save; How to load; List available saves; Loading on server startup) · **The Gameplay Loop** (Step 1 OBSERVE — check state AND take a screenshot; Step 2 ORIENT; Step 3 DECIDE; Step 4 ACT — move 2-4 steps max, then re-check; Step 5 VERIFY — screenshot after every move sequence; Step 6 RECORD progress to memory with a `PKM:` prefix; Step 7 SAVE periodically) · Action Reference · Critical Tips from Experience (USE VISION CONSTANTLY; Warp Transitions Need Extra Wait Time; Building Exit Trap; Dialog Handling; Ledges Are One-Way; Navigation Strategy; Running from Wild Battles; Battling (FIGHT)) · Battle Strategy (Decision Tree; Gen 1 Type Chart key matchups; Gen 1 Quirks) · Memory Conventions · Progression Milestones · Stopping Play · Pitfalls.
- **Inputs / options:** A ROM file (`.gb`, `.gbc`, `.gba`); the action reference (button presses); save/load commands; the live dashboard.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Emulator save states, screenshots, memory entries prefixed `PKM:`, and a watchable dashboard.
- **Config / env:** Python 3.10+, `uv`, PyBoy.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Move 2-4 steps maximum before re-checking; screenshot after every move sequence; warps need extra wait; the "Building Exit Trap" and one-way ledges are documented failure modes; Gen 1 type-chart quirks are encoded.
- **Rebuild notes:** An explicit OBSERVE→ORIENT→DECIDE→ACT→VERIFY loop with a hard step budget is what keeps a vision-driven game agent from drifting.

### Skill: fitness-nutrition  `id: optional.skill-fitness-nutrition`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/health/fitness-nutrition/SKILL.md` + `references/FORMULAS.md`, `scripts/{body_calc,nutrition_search}.py`; `hermes skills install official/health/fitness-nutrition`; catalog row "Workout planning, macros, and body metrics via wger/USDA."
- **What it does:** Looks up exercises (wger) and food nutrition (USDA FoodData Central), and computes body metrics — BMI, body fat, TDEE, one-rep max, macro ratios — offline.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, authors "Hailey Marshall (haileymarshall), Hermes Agent" (plus an `authors: [haileymarshall]` list), MIT, all platforms, tags `health, fitness, nutrition, gym, workout, diet, exercise`, `category: health`, `metadata.hermes.prerequisites.commands: [curl, python]`, and a `required_environment_variables` entry for `USDA_API_KEY` — prompt "USDA FoodData Central API key (free)", help "Get one free at https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/api-key-signup/ — or skip to use DEMO_KEY with lower rate limits", `required_for: "higher rate limits on food/nutrition lookups (DEMO_KEY works without signup)"`, `optional: true`. 252 lines. Sections: When to Use · Procedure (Exercise Lookup via the wger API — search by name, get full details for an exercise, list exercises filtered by muscle/category/equipment, combine filters; Nutrition Lookup via USDA FoodData Central — search foods by name, detailed nutrient profile by FDC ID; Offline Calculators) · Pitfalls · Verification · Quick Reference.
- **Inputs / options:** `curl -s "https://wger.de/api/v2/exercise/search/?term=${ENCODED}&language=english&format=json"`; `curl -s "https://wger.de/api/v2/exerciseinfo/${EXERCISE_ID}/?format=json"`; `curl -s "https://wger.de/api/v2/exercise/?${FILTER}&language=2&status=2&limit=20&format=json"` (filter example `?muscles=4&equipment=1&language=2&status=2`); `curl -s "https://api.nal.usda.gov/fdc/v1/foods/search?api_key=${API_KEY}&query=${ENCODED}&pageSize=5&dataType=Foundation,SR%20Legacy"`; `curl -s "https://api.nal.usda.gov/fdc/v1/food/${FDC_ID}?api_key=${API_KEY}"`; `scripts/body_calc.py` and `scripts/nutrition_search.py`; formulas in `references/FORMULAS.md`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Printed exercise/nutrition data and computed metrics; no writes.
- **Config / env:** `USDA_API_KEY` (optional — `DEMO_KEY` works with lower limits).
- **Edge cases / guards:** wger filtering requires `language=2&status=2` to get usable English, reviewed entries; USDA `dataType=Foundation,SR Legacy` avoids branded-food noise.
- **Rebuild notes:** Two free public APIs plus local formulas covers the whole domain without any paid dependency.

Checking Current State (Get Live Metrics; Key Fields in the Response; Interpreting the Output) · 2. Session Analysis (Single Session Breakdown; List All Sessions) · 3. Historical Search (Neural Similarity Search; Semantic Label Search; Cross-Modal Graph Search) · 4. Session Comparison (sort metrics by improvement percentage) · 5. Sleep Data · 6. Labeling Moments · 7. Real-Time Streaming · 8. UMAP Visualization · 9. Proactive State Awareness (Session Start Check; When to Proactively Mention State) · 10. Suggesting Protocols · 11. Additional Tools (Focus Timer; Calibration; OS Notifications; Raw JSON Passthrough) · Error Handling · Example Interactions · References.

### Skill: mcp-oauth-remote-gateway  `id: optional.skill-mcp-oauth-remote-gateway`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/mcp/mcp-oauth-remote-gateway/SKILL.md` + `references/stripe-mcp-oauth-revocation.md`, `scripts/diagnose-oauth-mcp.py`; `hermes skills install official/mcp/mcp-oauth-remote-gateway`; catalog row "Manual OAuth for remote MCP servers on headless gateways."
- **What it does:** Performs the MCP OAuth 2.1 flow by hand when Hermes runs as a remote gateway, where the built-in loopback-redirect flow cannot work, and writes the resulting tokens in Hermes's exact on-disk schema.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, authors "Ben Barclay (benbarclay), Hermes Agent", MIT, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `MCP, OAuth, PKCE, Remote-Deployment`, `related_skills: [hermes-agent, mcporter, fastmcp]`. 372 lines. The problem, verbatim: "Hermes' built-in MCP OAuth client runs a one-shot HTTP listener on `127.0.0.1:<port>` inside the Hermes process and registers that loopback address as the OAuth `redirect_uri`. That works perfectly for a local CLI on the user's own machine. It breaks completely when Hermes runs as a remote gateway (container, VPS, messaging bot), because the user's browser resolves `127.0.0.1` to the user's own laptop, not the remote container — so the authorization code never reaches Hermes." Sections: Overview · When to Use · Why the Built-in OAuth Flow Fails on a Remote Gateway · Cheap First Fallbacks: the Built-in Flow's Own Escape Hatches · **Preferred Front Door: the Hermes Dashboard (try this BEFORE manual token surgery)** · The Workaround (1. Confirm it's a remote gateway; 2. Find HERMES_HOME and the config path; 3. Discover OAuth metadata from the MCP server — expect `Bearer realm="mcp", resource_metadata="https://mcp.example.com/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource"`; 4. Dynamic Client Registration per RFC 7591; 5. Build the authorize URL with PKCE; 6. Give the user the authorize URL; 7. Exchange the code for tokens; 8. Write tokens in Hermes' exact schema; 9. Add the server to config.yaml; 10. Smoke-test the token BEFORE asking the user to reload — POST to the MCP URL with `Authorization: Bearer <access_token>`, `Accept: application/json, text/event-stream`, `Content-Type: application/json`, `MCP-Protocol-Version: 2025-06-18`, `User-Agent: python-httpx/0.27`; 11. Tell the user to run `/reload-mcp`) · Pitfalls & Lessons Learned · **What NOT to do** · Quick Reference Files · Related.
- **Inputs / options:** `curl -sI https://mcp.example.com | grep -i www-authenticate`; `curl -s -A "python-httpx/0.27" "https://mcp.example.com$p" | head -c 400`; `scripts/diagnose-oauth-mcp.py`; the `/reload-mcp` slash command.
- **Outputs / side effects:** OAuth client registration, a token file in Hermes's schema, and an `mcp_servers.<name>` entry in `config.yaml`.
- **Config / env:** `HERMES_HOME`, `config.yaml`, Hermes's MCP token store.
- **Edge cases / guards:** The skill insists on trying the built-in escape hatches and then the Hermes Dashboard front door *before* manual token surgery, and on smoke-testing the token before telling the user to reload. Some servers require a specific User-Agent. `references/stripe-mcp-oauth-revocation.md` documents a provider-specific revocation gotcha.
- **Rebuild notes:** Any OAuth client that binds a loopback redirect needs a documented remote path; better still, offer a device-code or dashboard-mediated flow so manual token surgery is never needed.

Sections: CLI Command · What this skill does · Path resolution · Default workflow · User interaction protocol · Decision-to-command mapping · Post-run reporting rules · Migration presets · Commands · Important rules · Expected result.
- **Inputs / options:** `hermes claw migrate` (full interactive migration); `hermes claw migrate --dry-run` (preview what would be migrated); `hermes claw migrate --preset user-data` (migrate without secrets); `hermes claw migrate --overwrite` (overwrite existing conflicts); `hermes claw migrate --source /custom/path/.openclaw`; direct script use `python ~/.hermes/skills/migration/openclaw-migration/scripts/openclaw_to_hermes.py`, `… --preset user-data`, `… --execute --preset user-data --skill-conflict skip`, `… --execute --preset full --migrate-secrets --skill-conflict skip`.

DeepSpeed ZeRO integration — same code, `hf_ds_config` accepting a path to a DeepSpeed config JSON or a dict, `accelerate config` selecting DeepSpeed → ZeRO-2 and writing an accelerate YAML at `~/.cache/huggingface/accelerate/default_config.yaml`, or pointing at a specific YAML; 4. FSDP — select FSDP → Full Shard → No CPU Offload, `sharding_strategy="FULL_SHARD"` as the ZeRO-3 equivalent; 5. Gradient accumulation) · When to use vs alternatives · Common issues (with WRONG/CORRECT pairs — Accelerate handles device placement automatically after `prepare()`; save only on the main process; load on all processes) · Advanced topics · Hardware requirements · Resources.

Launcher requirements — DDP uses built-in `torch.distributed.run`, DeepSpeed needs `pip install deepspeed`, FSDP needs PyTorch 1.12+.

### Skill: evaluating-llms-harness  `id: optional.skill-evaluating-llms-harness`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/mlops/evaluation/evaluating-llms-harness/SKILL.md` + `references/{api-evaluation,benchmark-guide,custom-tasks,distributed-eval}.md`; `hermes skills install official/mlops/evaluation/evaluating-llms-harness`; catalog row "lm-eval-harness: benchmark LLMs (MMLU, GSM8K, etc.)."
- **What it does:** Benchmarks LLMs with EleutherAI's lm-evaluation-harness — standard academic tasks, training-progress tracking, multi-model comparison and fast vLLM-backed evaluation.
- **How it works:** v1.0.1, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [lm-eval, transformers, vllm]`, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `Evaluation, LM Evaluation Harness, Benchmarking, MMLU, HumanEval, GSM8K, EleutherAI, Model Quality, Academic Benchmarks, Industry Standard`. 499 lines. Sections: What's inside · Quick start · Common workflows (1. Standard benchmark evaluation — full MMLU across 57 subjects; multiple benchmarks at once; 2. Track training progress — an `eval_checkpoint.sh` called from the training loop, then load all results and plot; 3. Compare multiple models — a `models.txt` plus `eval_all_models.sh`; 4. Evaluate with vLLM for faster inference — "Standard HF: ~2 hours for MMLU on 7B model" vs "vLLM: ~15-20 minutes for MMLU on 7B model") · When to use vs alternatives · Common issues · Advanced topics · Hardware requirements · Resources.
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install lm-eval`; `pip install vllm`; task selection (MMLU, HellaSwag, GSM8K, HumanEval, …); the two shell drivers.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Result JSON per checkpoint/model; plots.
- **Config / env:** CUDA 11.8+.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Hardware requirements — GPU NVIDIA (CUDA 11.8+), CPU works but very slow; VRAM 7B: 16 GB bf16 or 8 GB 8-bit, 13B: 28 GB or 14 GB, 70B: multi-GPU or quantization. Timings on a single A100 for a 7B model — HellaSwag 10 min, GSM8K 5 min, MMLU (full) 2 h, HumanEval 20 min. Alternatives: HELM for broader evaluation (fairness, efficiency, calibration), AlpacaEval for instruction-following with LLM judges, MT-Bench for multi-turn.
- **Rebuild notes:** Publish concrete per-benchmark runtimes and VRAM numbers — that is what makes an evaluation plan schedulable.

### Skill: faiss  `id: optional.skill-faiss`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/mlops/faiss/SKILL.md` + `references/index_types.md`; `hermes skills install official/mlops/faiss`; catalog row "Fast vector similarity search at billion scale."
- **What it does:** Uses FAISS for high-throughput vector similarity search at million-to-billion scale, with a choice of index types and optional GPU acceleration.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [faiss-cpu, faiss-gpu, numpy]`, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `RAG, FAISS, Similarity Search, Vector Search, Facebook AI, GPU Acceleration, Billion-Scale, K-NN, HNSW, High Performance, Large Scale`. 226 lines. Sections: When to use FAISS · Quick start (Installation CPU-only / GPU; Basic usage — 1000 vectors × 128 dimensions, create index, search) · Index types (1. Flat exact search — L2 Euclidean, inner product for cosine when normalized; slowest, most accurate; 2. IVF inverted file, fast approximate — create quantizer, IVF index with 100 clusters, train on data, add vectors, search with `nprobe` clusters; 3. HNSW Hierarchical NSW — best quality/speed, no training needed; 4. Product Quantization — reduces memory 16-32×, train and add) · Save and load · GPU acceleration (single GPU; multi-GPU; "10-100× faster than CPU") · LangChain integration (create vector store, save, load, search) · LlamaIndex integration · Best practices · Performance · Resources.
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install faiss-cpu`; `pip install faiss-gpu`; index type choice and its parameters (`nlist`, `nprobe`, HNSW `M`, PQ codes).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Serialized index files.
- **Config / env:** n/a.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Stated stats — 31,700+ GitHub stars, Meta/Facebook AI Research, handles billions of vectors, C++ with Python bindings. Alternatives: Chroma/Pinecone when metadata filtering is needed, Weaviate for full-text hybrid.
- **Rebuild notes:** The index-type decision table is the whole skill; everything else is boilerplate.

### Skill: flash-attention  `id: optional.skill-flash-attention`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/mlops/flash-attention/SKILL.md` + `references/{benchmarks,transformers-integration}.md`; `hermes skills install official/mlops/flash-attention`; catalog row "Speed up long-sequence transformer training and inference."
- **What it does:** Enables FlashAttention (via PyTorch SDPA or the `flash-attn` library) to make long-sequence transformer training and inference 2-4× faster without accuracy loss.
- **How it works:** v1.0.1, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [flash-attn, torch, transformers]`, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `Optimization, Flash Attention, Attention Optimization, Memory Efficiency, Speed Optimization, Long Context, PyTorch, SDPA, H100, FP8, Transformers`. 375 lines. Sections: Quick start (PyTorch automatically uses Flash Attention if available; `q, k, v: [batch, seqlen, nheads, headdim]`) · Common workflows (1. Enable in an existing PyTorch model — torch ≥ 2.2.0, before/after attention code, benchmark, compare outputs, difference should be < 1e-3 for float16; 2. Use the flash-attn library for advanced features — NVIDIA GPUs CUDA 12.0+, verify installation, input shape `[batch_size, seq_len, num_heads, head_dim]` with transposition from `[batch, heads, seq, dim]`, GQA with fewer KV heads, sliding-window attention over 256 tokens before/after, warmup then benchmark; 3. H100 FP8 optimization with FlashAttention-3 — check for "H100"/"H800", note that compilation is heavy and needs a CUDA toolchain + Hopper GPU, FP8 forward casts to `float8_e4m3fn` for inference/forward-only, FP16/BF16 forward+backward also supported) · When to use vs alternatives · Common issues (compute capability should be ≥ (7, 5) for Turing+) · Advanced topics · Hardware requirements · Resources.
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install flash-attn --no-build-isolation`; `python -c "import torch; print(torch.__version__)"`; `pip install --upgrade torch`; `python -c "from flash_attn import flash_attn_func; print('Success')"`; source build via `git clone https://github.com/Dao-AILab/flash-attention.git` + `python setup.py install`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Faster attention; no output change beyond float tolerance.
- **Config / env:** CUDA 12.0+ (11.8 minimum).
- **Edge cases / guards:** Hardware — NVIDIA Ampere+ (A100, A10, A30) or AMD MI200+; VRAM unchanged versus standard attention. Use standard attention below 256 tokens (overhead not worth it); xFormers when more attention variants are needed. Numerical check: difference < 1e-3 for float16.
- **Rebuild notes:** Shipping the exact numerical-equivalence check turns "is it working?" into a one-line assertion.

### Skill: guidance  `id: optional.skill-guidance`
- **Surface:** Skill

- **What it does:** Uses Microsoft Research's Guidance to constrain LLM generation with regex and grammars so output is guaranteed valid (JSON, dates, emails, choices) and multi-step workflows use Pythonic control flow.
- **How it works:** v1.0.1, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [guidance, transformers]`, all platforms, tags `Prompt Engineering, Guidance, Constrained Generation, Structured Output, JSON Validation, Grammar, Microsoft Research, Format Enforcement, Multi-Step Workflows`. 581 lines. Sections: When to Use This Skill · Installation (base; with specific backends) · Quick Start (Basic Example: Structured Generation — loads a model supporting OpenAI, Transformers or llama.cpp, generates with constraints; Chat format with a local model using context managers) · Core Concepts (1. Context Managers — system/user/assistant blocks; 2. Constrained Generation — constrain to a valid email format, a `YYYY-MM-DD` date, a phone number, specific choices, multiple-choice selection; 3. **Token Healing** — worked example where the last prompt token is `" is "` and the first generated token would be `" Par"` giving `"The capital of France is  Paris"` with a double space; token healing is enabled by default and fixes it; 4. Grammar-Based Generation — JSON via a Pydantic schema compiled to a grammar by `guidance.json`, or composing grammar functions directly; 5. Guidance Functions) · Backend Configuration (OpenAI — remote, unconstrained only; Local Models via Transformers; Local Models via llama.cpp) · Common Patterns (1. JSON Generation; 2. Classification; 3. Multi-Step Reasoning; 4. ReAct…).
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install guidance`; `pip install guidance[transformers]` (Hugging Face models); `pip install guidance[llama_cpp]` (llama.cpp models).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Constrained generations.
- **Config / env:** backend model paths / API keys.
- **Edge cases / guards:** OpenAI as a backend is **unconstrained only** — real grammar enforcement requires a local model. Stated stats: 18,000+ GitHub stars, from Microsoft Research.
- **Rebuild notes:** Token healing is the non-obvious correctness feature — document it with a concrete double-space example as this skill does.

### Skill: huggingface-tokenizers  `id: optional.skill-huggingface-tokenizers`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/mlops/huggingface-tokenizers/SKILL.md` + `references/{algorithms,integration,pipeline,training}.md`; `hermes skills install official/mlops/huggingface-tokenizers`; catalog row "Fast BPE/WordPiece tokenization and custom vocab training."
- **What it does:** Loads, trains and integrates fast Rust-backed tokenizers — BPE, WordPiece and Unigram — with alignment tracking back to the original text.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [tokenizers, transformers, datasets]`, all platforms, tags `Tokenization, HuggingFace, BPE, WordPiece, Unigram, Fast Tokenization, Rust, Custom Tokenizer, Alignment Tracking, Production`. 521 lines. Sections: When to use · Quick start (Installation; Load pretrained tokenizer from the Hub, encode, decode; Train a custom BPE tokenizer — initialize with the BPE model, configure the trainer, train on files, save; Batch encoding with padding, showing padded id rows `[101, 7592, 2088, 102, 3, 3, 3]` vs `[101, 2023, 2003, 1037, 2936, 6251, 102]`) · Tokenization algorithms (BPE; WordPiece; Unigram) · Tokenization pipeline (Normalization — `"Héllo WORLD"` → `"hello world"`; Pre-tokenization — `"Hello, world!"` → `["Hello", ",", "world", "!"]`; Post-processing — BERT-style `[CLS] sentence [SEP]`, GPT-2 `sentence <|endoftext|>`, RoBERTa `<s> sentence </s>`) · Alignment tracking (token offsets, e.g. `hello → [ 0, 5): 'Hello'`, `, → [ 5, 6): ','`, `world → [ 7, 12): 'world'`, `! → [12, 13): '!'`) · Integration with transformers (AutoTokenizer automatically uses fast tokenizers; check whether the fast tokenizer is in use; access the underlying `tokenizers.Tokenizer`; convert a custom tokenizer for transformers).
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install tokenizers`; `pip install tokenizers transformers`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Saved tokenizer JSON files; encoded batches.
- **Config / env:** n/a.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Performance claims — under 20 s to tokenize 1 GB on CPU; Rust core with Python/Node.js bindings; 10-100× faster than pure Python.
- **Rebuild notes:** Alignment offsets are the feature most tokenizer wrappers forget; document them with a worked offset table.

### Skill: serving-llms-vllm  `id: optional.skill-serving-llms-vllm`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/mlops/inference/serving-llms-vllm/SKILL.md` + `references/{optimization,quantization,server-deployment,troubleshooting}.md`; `hermes skills install official/mlops/inference/serving-llms-vllm`; catalog row "vLLM: high-throughput LLM serving, OpenAI API, quantization."
- **What it does:** Deploys and tunes vLLM for high-throughput LLM serving with OpenAI-compatible endpoints, quantization and tensor parallelism, plus offline batch inference.
- **How it works:** v1.0.1, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [vllm, torch, transformers]`, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `vLLM, Inference Serving, PagedAttention, Continuous Batching, High Throughput, Production, OpenAI API, Quantization, Tensor Parallelism`. 374 lines. Sections: When to use · Quick start (query with the OpenAI SDK) · Common workflows (1. Production API deployment — 7B-13B on a single GPU, 30B-70B with tensor parallelism, production with caching where "Prometheus metrics are exposed automatically at /metrics on the API port", load testing with locust via a `test_load.py`, and running vLLM in Docker; 2. Offline batch inference — load prompts from a file, process all prompts in one call, vLLM handles batching internally with no manual chunking, extract generated text, save to file; 3. Quantized model serving — search for AWQ models e.g. `TheBloke/Llama-2-70B-AWQ`, use the pre-quantized model, "Results: 70B model in ~40GB VRAM", compare quantized vs non-quantized responses and verify task-specific performance is unchanged) · When to use vs alternatives · Common issues · Advanced topics · Hardware requirements · Resources.
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install vllm`; `pip install locust`; `locust -f test_load.py --host http://localhost:8000`; `curl http://localhost:8000/metrics | grep vllm`; `docker run --gpus all -p 8000:8000 …`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A running OpenAI-compatible server on port 8000 with Prometheus metrics; batch inference outputs.
- **Config / env:** tensor-parallel size, quantization method (GPTQ/AWQ/FP8).
- **Edge cases / guards:** Hardware — small models (7B-13B) 1× A10 (24 GB) or A100 (40 GB); medium (30B-40B) 2× A100 (40 GB) with tensor parallelism; large (70B+) 4× A100 (40 GB) or 2× A100 (80 GB) using AWQ/GPTQ. Supported platforms: NVIDIA (primary), AMD ROCm, Intel GPUs, TPUs. Use when serving 100+ req/sec.
- **Rebuild notes:** Pair every quantization recipe with an explicit "verify task-specific performance unchanged" step.

### Skill: instructor  `id: optional.skill-instructor`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/mlops/instructor/SKILL.md` + `references/{examples,providers,validation}.md`; `hermes skills install official/mlops/instructor`; catalog row "Structured LLM outputs validated with Pydantic."
- **What it does:** Extracts structured, Pydantic-validated data from LLM responses across providers, retrying automatically when validation fails, with streaming support.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [instructor, pydantic, openai, anthropic]`, all platforms, tags `Prompt Engineering, Instructor, Structured Output, Pydantic, Data Extraction, JSON Parsing, Type Safety, Validation, Streaming, OpenAI, Anthropic`. 745 lines. Sections: When to Use This Skill · Installation (base; with specific providers) · Quick Start (Basic Example: Extract User Data — define the output structure, create the instructor client, extract; With OpenAI) · Core Concepts (1. Response Models in Pydantic — optional fields the LLM need not provide; 2. Validation — invalid data triggers an automatic retry; 3. Automatic Retrying — up to 3 times, feeding the LLM the validation error e.g. "Validation error: age - field required" so it retries with better extraction; 4. Streaming — stream partial updates as the LLM generates, and stream list items as they are produced) · Provider Configuration (Anthropic Claude; OpenAI; Local Models via Ollama by pointing at the local server) · Common Patterns (1. Data Extraction from Text; 2. Classification; 3. Multi-Entity Extraction; 4. Structured Analysis; 5. Batch Processing) · Advanced Features (Union Types — the LLM chooses the appropriate type based on content; Dynamic Models created at runtime; Custom Modes for providers without native structured output, with `Mode.ANTHROPIC_TOOLS` recommended for Claude, `Mode.JSON` as fallback, `Mode.TOOLS`, …).
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install instructor`; `pip install "instructor[anthropic]"`; `pip install "instructor[openai]"`; `pip install "instructor[all]"`; mode selection.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Validated Pydantic objects.
- **Config / env:** provider API keys.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Retries are bounded at 3 by default and the validation error text is fed back to the model. Stated stats: 15,000+ GitHub stars, 100,000+ developers.
- **Rebuild notes:** Feeding the *validation error message* back to the model is the mechanism; a retry without it is wasted.

### Skill: lambda-labs  `id: optional.skill-lambda-labs`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/mlops/lambda-labs/SKILL.md` + `references/{advanced-usage,troubleshooting}.md`; `hermes skills install official/mlops/lambda-labs`; catalog row "On-demand GPU cloud instances for ML training."
- **What it does:** Launches, connects to and manages Lambda Labs GPU instances with persistent filesystems, SSH tunnelling and JupyterLab, for long training runs.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [lambda-cloud-client>=1.0.0]`, all platforms, tags `Infrastructure, GPU Cloud, Training, Inference, Lambda Labs`. 550 lines. Sections: When to use · Quick start (Account setup — create an account at https://lambda.ai, add a payment method, generate an API key, add an SSH key which is required before launching; Launch via console; Connect via SSH with the instance IP or a specific key) · GPU instances (Available GPUs; Instance configurations; Launch times) · Lambda Stack (included software; Verify installation — check GPU, PyTorch, CUDA version) · Python API (Installation; Authentication with the API key; List available instances; Launch instance; List running instances; Terminate instance; SSH key management — add, list, delete) · CLI with curl (List instance types; Launch; Terminate) · Persistent storage (Filesystems; mount location; save checkpoints; Create filesystem; Attach to instance; Best practices — store on the filesystem which persists, local SSD is faster but ephemeral) · SSH configuration (Add SSH key — generate locally, add the public key in the console or via API; Multiple keys; Import from GitHub; SSH tunnelling — forward Jupyter, forward TensorBoard, multiple ports) · JupyterLab (Launch from console; Manual access with a tunnel to `http://localhost:8888`) · Training workflows (Single-GPU training — SSH, clone, install, train …).
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install lambda-cloud-client`; `curl -u $LAMBDA_API_KEY: …`; `python -c "import torch; print(torch.cuda.is_available())"`; `python train.py --checkpoint-dir /lambda/nfs/my-storage/checkpoints`; `git clone …` + `pip install -r requirements.txt` + `python train.py --epochs 100 --checkpoint-dir /lambda/nfs/storage/checkpoints`; `pip install transformers accelerate peft`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Running (billed) GPU instances; persistent filesystem contents; SSH keys registered on the account.
- **Config / env:** `LAMBDA_API_KEY`; SSH keys.
- **Edge cases / guards:** GPU variety B200, H100, GH200, A100, A10, A6000, V100; Lambda Stack pre-installs PyTorch/TensorFlow/CUDA/NCCL; an SSH key must exist before any instance can launch; checkpoints belong on `/lambda/nfs/...` because local SSD is ephemeral.
- **Rebuild notes:** "Persistent path vs ephemeral path" is the one fact a training agent must not get wrong on any GPU cloud.

### Skill: segment-anything-model  `id: optional.skill-segment-anything-model`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/mlops/models/segment-anything-model/SKILL.md` + `references/{advanced-usage,troubleshooting}.md`; `hermes skills install official/mlops/models/segment-anything-model`; catalog row "SAM: zero-shot image segmentation via points, boxes, masks."
- **What it does:** Segments any object in an image zero-shot using point, box or mask prompts, or generates every mask automatically.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [segment-anything, transformers>=4.30.0, torch>=1.7.0]`, all platforms, tags `Multimodal, Image Segmentation, Computer Vision, SAM, Zero-Shot`. 507 lines. Sections: When to use SAM · Quick start (Installation from GitHub; optional dependencies; or HuggingFace transformers; **Download checkpoints** — ViT-H largest/most accurate 2.4 GB, ViT-L medium 1.2 GB, ViT-B smallest/fastest 375 MB; Basic usage with `SamPredictor` — load model, create predictor, set image which computes embeddings once, predict with point prompts, select the best mask; HuggingFace Transformers path — load model and processor, process image with a point prompt, generate masks, post-process to original size) · Core concepts (Model architecture; Model variants; Prompt types) · Interactive segmentation (Point prompts — single foreground point; multiple points foreground + background; Box prompts `[x1, y1, x2, y2]`; Combined prompts — box + points for precise control; Iterative refinement — initial prediction then refine with an additional point using the previous mask) · Automatic mask generation (Basic — create the generator, generate all masks, each mask containing `segmentation` binary mask, `bbox [x, y, w, h]`, `area` pixel count, `predicted_iou` quality score, `stability_score` robustness score, `point_coords` generating point; Customized generation; Filtering masks — sort by area largest first, filter by predicted IoU, filter by stability score).
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install git+https://github.com/facebookresearch/segment-anything.git`; `pip install opencv-python pycocotools matplotlib`; `pip install transformers`; `python scripts/export_onnx_model.py …`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Binary masks and mask metadata; downloaded checkpoints.
- **Config / env:** n/a.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Setting the image computes embeddings once — repeated prompts on the same image are cheap, which is what makes interactive refinement viable.
- **Rebuild notes:** Document the exact mask-record fields (`predicted_iou`, `stability_score`) — filtering on them is how automatic mask generation becomes usable.

### Skill: nemo-curator  `id: optional.skill-nemo-curator`
- **Surface:** Skill

- **How it works:** v1.0.1, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [nemo-curator, cudf, dask, rapids]`, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `Data Processing, NeMo Curator, Data Curation, GPU Acceleration, Deduplication, Quality Filtering, NVIDIA, RAPIDS, PII Redaction, Multimodal, LLM Training Data`. 402 lines. Sections: When to use · Quick start (Installation — "NeMo Curator 1.x installs with uv. Extras use hyphens (PyPI-normalized): text-cuda12 / text-cpu (and image/video/audio/math variants), or `all`."; Basic text curation pipeline — define/compose stages load → filter → dedupe → classify → write, each stage declaring its own Resources (CPU cores, GPU memory, replicas), then run with a Ray-backed executor) · Data curation pipeline (Stage 1 Quality filtering — 30+ heuristic filters, word count, repetitive content removal, URL ratio; Stage 2 Deduplication — exact duplicates, MinHash + LSH, embedding-based; Stage 3 PII redaction; Stage 4 Classifier filtering — quality classification then filter low-quality documents) · GPU acceleration (GPU vs CPU performance; Multi-GPU scaling — initialize a GPU cluster, process with 8 GPUs) · Multi-modal curation (Image — aesthetic scoring, NSFW detection, CLIP embeddings; Video — scene detection, clip extraction, embeddings; Audio — ASR transcription, filter by word error rate, duration filtering) · Common patterns (Web scrape …).

- **Outputs / side effects:** Curated dataset files.
- **Config / env:** RAPIDS/CUDA 12 environment; Ray executor.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Performance claims — 16× faster fuzzy deduplication on 8 TB RedPajama v2, 40% lower TCO vs CPU alternatives, near-linear scaling across GPU nodes. Extras must use hyphens (PyPI-normalized names).
- **Rebuild notes:** Per-stage Resource declarations are what let one pipeline mix CPU and GPU stages without manual placement.

### Skill: obliteratus  `id: optional.skill-obliteratus`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/mlops/obliteratus/SKILL.md` + `references/{analysis-modules,methods-guide}.md`, `templates/{abliteration-config,analysis-study,batch-abliteration}.yaml`; `hermes skills install official/mlops/obliteratus`; catalog row "OBLITERATUS: abliterate LLM refusals (diff-in-means)."
- **What it does:** Removes an open-weight LLM's refusal behaviour by projecting out refusal directions from its weights (abliteration), then verifies refusal rate and coherence.
- **How it works:** v2.0.0, author "Hermes Agent", MIT, `dependencies: [obliteratus, torch, transformers, bitsandbytes, accelerate, safetensors]`, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `Abliteration, Uncensoring, Refusal-Removal, LLM, Weight-Projection, SVD, Mechanistic-Interpretability, HuggingFace, Model-Surgery`, `related_skills: [serving-llms-vllm, llama-cpp, huggingface-tokenizers]`. 343 lines. Sections: What's inside · Video Guide · When to Use This Skill · Step 1 Installation · Step 2 Check Hardware (VRAM Requirements with 4-bit quantization) · Step 3 Browse Available Models & Get Recommendations (browse models by compute tier; get architecture info for a specific model; get a telemetry-driven recommendation for the best method and params) · Step 4 Choose a Method (Method Selection Guide; **9 CLI Methods**; Direction Extraction Methods via the `--direction-method` flag; **4 Python-API-Only Methods**) · Step 5 Run Abliteration (Standard usage — default method "advanced" recommended for most models; with 4-bit quantization to save VRAM; large models 70B+ with conservative defaults; Fine-tuning parameters; Key flags; Other execution modes — interactive guided mode hardware → model → preset, Web UI (Gradio), run a full ablation study from a YAML config, tournament pitting all methods against each other) · Step 6 Verify Results (If refusals persist > 10%; If coherence is damaged, perplexity > 15% increase) · Step 7 Use the Abliterated Model (test locally with transformers; upload to HuggingFace Hub; serve with vLLM) · CLI Command Reference · Analysis Modules (quick analysis commands).
- **Inputs / options:** `obliteratus --version 2>/dev/null && echo "INSTALLED" || echo "NOT INSTALLED"`; `git clone https://github.com/elder-plinius/OBLITERATUS.git` + `pip install -e .`; `pip install -e ".[spaces]"` for the Gradio web UI; the 9 CLI methods, the `--direction-method` extraction choices, the 4 Python-API-only methods, YAML configs (`abliteration-config.yaml`, `analysis-study.yaml`, `batch-abliteration.yaml`), 4-bit quantization flags, interactive guided mode and tournament mode.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Modified model weights written as safetensors; optional Hub upload; analysis reports.
- **Config / env:** GPU VRAM per the documented table (with 4-bit quantization).
- **Edge cases / guards:** Two explicit failure branches after the run — refusals persisting above 10%, and coherence damage measured as a perplexity increase above 15%. Trigger phrases include "uncensor", "abliterate", "refusal removal", "weight projection".
- **Rebuild notes:** Pair the surgery with two quantitative acceptance gates (refusal rate, perplexity delta) and a tournament mode that compares methods empirically.

### Skill: peft  `id: optional.skill-peft`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/mlops/peft/SKILL.md` + `references/{advanced-usage,troubleshooting}.md`; `hermes skills install official/mlops/peft`; catalog row "Fine-tune large LLMs with LoRA on limited GPU memory."
- **What it does:** Fine-tunes 7B-70B models with LoRA/QLoRA adapters on consumer GPUs, then loads, merges and serves multiple adapters from one base model.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [peft>=0.13.0, transformers>=4.45.0, torch>=2.0.0, bitsandbytes>=0.43.0]`, all platforms, tags `Fine-Tuning, PEFT, LoRA, QLoRA, Parameter-Efficient, Adapters, Low-Rank, Memory Optimization, Multi-Adapter`. 436 lines. Sections: When to use PEFT · Quick start (Installation — basic, with quantization support (recommended), full stack; **LoRA fine-tuning (standard)** — load base model, LoRA configuration, apply LoRA producing e.g. "trainable params: 13,631,488 || all params: 8,043,307,008 || trainable%: 0.17%", prepare dataset, training, save adapter only "6MB vs 16GB"; **QLoRA fine-tuning** — 4-bit quantization config, load quantized model, prepare for training which enables gradient checkpointing, LoRA config for QLoRA, "70B model now fits on single 24GB GPU!") · LoRA parameter selection (Rank `r` — capacity vs efficiency; Alpha `lora_alpha` — scaling factor, "Rule of thumb: alpha = 2 * rank"; **Target modules by architecture** — Llama/Mistral/Qwen, GPT-2/GPT-Neo, Falcon, BLOOM, plus auto-detect all linear layers) · Loading and merging adapters (Load trained adapter — Option 1 via `PeftModel`, Option 2 load directly (recommended); Merge adapter into base model for deployment with no adapter overhead, save merged model, push to Hub; Multi-adapter serving — load base with the first adapter, load additional adapters, switch between them).
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install peft`; `pip install peft bitsandbytes`; `pip install peft transformers accelerate bitsandbytes datasets`; `r`, `lora_alpha`, `target_modules`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Adapter files (~6 MB) and optionally a merged full model.
- **Config / env:** n/a.
- **Edge cases / guards:** QLoRA trades roughly 5% quality for the memory win; the target-modules table per architecture is what makes LoRA actually attach to the right layers.
- **Rebuild notes:** Print the trainable-parameter ratio after applying LoRA — it is the fastest way to catch a misconfigured `target_modules`.

### Skill: pinecone  `id: optional.skill-pinecone`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/mlops/pinecone/SKILL.md` + `references/deployment.md`; `hermes skills install official/mlops/pinecone`; catalog row "Managed vector DB for production RAG and search."
- **What it does:** Uses Pinecone as a managed serverless vector database for production RAG — indexes, namespaces, metadata filtering and hybrid dense+sparse search.
- **How it works:** v1.0.1, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [pinecone]`, all platforms, tags `RAG, Pinecone, Vector Database, Managed Service, Serverless, Hybrid Search, Production, Auto-Scaling, Low Latency, Recommendations`. 382 lines. Sections: When to use Pinecone · Quick start (Installation; Basic usage — initialize, create index, connect, upsert, query) · Core operations (Create index — serverless (recommended) or pod-based for consistent performance; Upsert vectors — single, batch (recommended); Query vectors — basic, with metadata filtering, namespace query, access results; Metadata filtering — exact match, comparison, logical operators, `in` operator) · Namespaces (partition data, query a specific namespace, list namespaces) · **Hybrid search (dense + sparse)** — upsert with sparse vectors, hybrid query, with the explicit note: "index.query() does NOT accept an `alpha` kwarg. Pinecone stores a single sparse-dense vector, so weighting must be applied by pre-scaling the query vectors before sending them. Use the hybrid_score_norm helper below (alpha * dense + (1 - alpha) * sparse; alpha=1 → pure dense, 0 → pure sparse)." · LangChain integration (create vector store, query, with metadata filter, as retriever) · LlamaIndex integration · Index management.
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install pinecone`; index/namespace parameters; the `hybrid_score_norm` helper.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Indexes and vectors in the Pinecone service.
- **Config / env:** Pinecone API key.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "the old `pinecone-client` package is deprecated. Install `pinecone` (v5+; current 9.x). The import stays `from pinecone import Pinecone`." Stated stats: fully managed SaaS, auto-scales to billions of vectors, p95 latency < 100 ms, 99.9% uptime SLA. Alternatives: Chroma (self-hosted, open source), FAISS.
- **Rebuild notes:** Document the *absence* of an expected parameter (`alpha`) — that is the kind of thing that silently produces wrong results.

Sections: Quick start (create environment; install PyTorch 2.2.2; install alignment-handbook; install Flash Attention 2) · Common workflows (1. Train from a base model (Mistral 7B) — model, dataset, SimPO hyperparameters, training, output; 2. Fine-tune an instruct model (Llama 3 8B); 3. Reasoning-intensive tasks with a lower learning rate) · When to use vs alternatives · Common issues · Advanced topics · Hardware requirements · Resources.
- **Inputs / options:** `git clone https://github.com/huggingface/alignment-handbook.git` + `python -m pip install .`; `python -m pip install flash-attn --no-build-isolation`; `scripts/run_simpo.py training_configs/mistral-7b-base-simpo.yaml`; `scripts/run_simpo.py training_configs/llama3-8b-instruct-simpo.yaml`; launched under `ACCELERATE…`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** An aligned model checkpoint.
- **Config / env:** PyTorch 2.2.2 specifically; Flash Attention 2.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Algorithm selection guidance — SimPO is simplest with the best performance and no reference model; DPO when a reference-model baseline is needed and more conservative behaviour is wanted; PPO for maximum control with a reward model and more complexity.
- **Rebuild notes:** n/a.

Sections: When to Use slime · Key Features · Architecture Overview · Installation (Recommended: Docker; inside the container; From Source) · Quick Start: GRPO Training (source model configuration; launch training) · Workflow 1 Standard GRPO Training (Prerequisites Checklist — Docker environment or Megatron-LM + SGLang installed, a model checkpoint in HuggingFace or Megatron format, training data in JSONL; Step 1 Prepare Data with a `data.jsonl` format; Step 2 Configure Model — list available models, e.g. `glm4-9B.sh`, `qwen3-4B.sh`, `qwen3-30B-A3B.sh`, `deepseek-v3.sh`, `llama3-8B.sh`, then source your model; Step 3 Launch Training; Step 4 Monitor Training) · Workflow 2 Asynchronous Training (When to Use Async; Launch Async Training; Async-Specific Parameters) · Workflow 3 Multi-Turn Agentic Training (Prerequisites — a custom generate function for multi-turn logic and a tool/environment; Step 1 Define a Custom Generate Function in `custom_generate.py`; Step 2 Launch with the Custom Function) · Configuration Reference (Three Argument Categories — resource allocation, data, training loop, algorithm; Key Constraints) · Data Buffer System (Basic Data Source; Buffered Data Source for off-policy) · Common Issues and Solutions (SGLang Engine Crash — enable fault tolerance, increase memory allocation, reduce batch size …).

### Skill: stable-diffusion  `id: optional.skill-stable-diffusion`
- **Surface:** Skill

- **What it does:** Generates and edits images locally with Diffusers — text-to-image, img2img, inpainting, ControlNet conditioning, LoRA adapters and memory optimization.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [diffusers>=0.30.0, transformers>=4.41.0, accelerate>=0.31.0, torch>=2.0.0]`, all platforms, tags `Image Generation, Stable Diffusion, Diffusers, Text-to-Image, Multimodal, Computer Vision`. 524 lines. Sections: When to use · Quick start (Installation; Basic text-to-image with `DiffusionPipeline.from_pretrained` auto-detecting the model type, then `pipe.to("cuda")`; Using SDXL for higher quality via `AutoPipelineForText2Image`, enabling memory optimization with `pipe.enable_model_cpu_offload()`) · Architecture overview (Three-pillar design; Pipeline inference flow) · Core concepts (Pipelines; Schedulers; Swapping schedulers — e.g. `pipe.scheduler = DPMSolverMultistepScheduler.from_config(pipe.scheduler.config)` for faster generation with fewer steps) · Generation parameters (Key parameters; Reproducible generation; Negative prompts) · Image-to-image (`AutoPipelineForImage2Image`) · Inpainting · ControlNet (load a ControlNet for edge conditioning; use a Canny edge image as control; Available ControlNets) · LoRA adapters (load LoRA weights; generate with the LoRA style; adjust LoRA strength; unload LoRA; Multiple LoRAs — load several and set weights for each) · Memory optimization (CPU offloading — model CPU offload moves models to CPU when not in use, sequential CPU offload is more aggressive and slower; Attention slicing — compute attention in chunks, or a specific chunk size; xFormers memory-efficient attention requiring the `xformers` package; VAE slicing for large images).
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install diffusers transformers accelerate torch`; `pip install xformers` (optional).
- **Outputs / side effects:** Generated image files; downloaded model weights.
- **Config / env:** CUDA/MPS device.
- **Edge cases / guards:** The memory-optimization ladder (model offload → sequential offload → attention slicing → xFormers → VAE slicing) is ordered by cost.
- **Rebuild notes:** n/a.

### Skill: tensorrt-llm  `id: optional.skill-tensorrt-llm`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/mlops/tensorrt-llm/SKILL.md` + `references/{multi-gpu,optimization,serving}.md`; `hermes skills install official/mlops/tensorrt-llm`; catalog row "High-throughput LLM inference on NVIDIA GPUs."
- **What it does:** Runs and serves LLMs on NVIDIA GPUs with TensorRT-LLM for maximum throughput, including FP8/INT4 quantization, tensor parallelism and in-flight batching.
- **How it works:** v1.0.1, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [tensorrt-llm, torch]`, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `Inference Serving, TensorRT-LLM, NVIDIA, Inference Optimization, High Throughput, Low Latency, Production, FP8, INT4, In-Flight Batching, Multi-GPU`. 194 lines. Sections: When to use · Quick start (Installation — "Docker (recommended) — images are on NGC (nvcr.io), not Docker Hub. Replace x.y.z with the desired version (e.g. 1.2.1). Browse tags on NGC: https://catalog.ngc.nvidia.com/orgs/nvidia/teams/tensorrt-llm/containers/release/tags"; pip install current stable GA, "Requires CUDA 13.2.1, TensorRT 10.x, Python 3.10-3.12"; Basic inference — initialize model, configure sampling, generate; Serving with `trtllm-serve` — start the server with automatic model download and compilation, then a client request) · Key features (Performance optimizations; Parallelism; Advanced features) · Common patterns (Quantized model FP8 — "Load FP8 quantized model (2× faster, 50% memory)", inference unchanged; Multi-GPU deployment — tensor parallelism across 8 GPUs; Batch inference — process 100 prompts with automatic in-flight batching for maximum throughput) · Performance benchmarks · Supported models · References · Resources.
- **Inputs / options:** `docker pull nvcr.io/nvidia/tensorrt-llm/release:x.y.z`; `pip install tensorrt_llm`; `trtllm-serve`; `curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/v1/chat/completions …`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Compiled engines and a serving endpoint on port 8000.
- **Config / env:** CUDA 13.2.1, TensorRT 10.x, Python 3.10-3.12.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Images live on NGC, not Docker Hub — a common pull failure. NVIDIA-only (A100, H100, GB200); throughput claim 24,000+ tokens/sec on Llama 3. Use vLLM instead for a simpler Python-first setup, PagedAttention without TensorRT compilation, or AMD GPUs.
- **Rebuild notes:** n/a.

### Skill: torchtitan  `id: optional.skill-torchtitan`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/mlops/torchtitan/SKILL.md` + `references/{checkpoint,custom-models,float8,fsdp}.md`; `hermes skills install official/mlops/torchtitan`; catalog row "Pretrain LLMs at scale with PyTorch 4D parallelism."
- **What it does:** Pretrains LLMs from scratch (8B to 405B+) with TorchTitan's PyTorch-native composable 4D parallelism (FSDP2, tensor, pipeline, context) and Float8 on H100s.
- **How it works:** v1.0.1, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [torch>=2.6.0, torchtitan>=0.2.0, torchao>=0.5.0]`, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `Model Architecture, Distributed Training, TorchTitan, FSDP2, Tensor Parallel, Pipeline Parallel, Context Parallel, Float8, Llama, Pretraining`. 389 lines. Sections: Quick start (from PyPI stable; from source for latest features requiring PyTorch nightly; get an HF token from https://huggingface.co/settings/tokens; **"Configs are selected by name from the Python config registry (torchtitan/models/llama3/config_registry.py), not by TOML path"**) · Common workflows (1. Pretrain Llama 3.1 8B on a single node — fields for a llama3 8B run registered in `config_registry.py` or passed as `--overrides`, 8 GPUs on one node selecting the config by registry name, overriding individual fields on the command line, or explicitly with `torchrun` which `run_train.sh` wraps; 2. Multi-node training with SLURM; 3. Enable Float8 training for H100s — the Float8 config is baked into the registered config in your `model_registry(...)` call, so just select it and enable compile; 4. 4D parallelism for 405B models — 64 nodes × 8 GPUs = 512 GPUs) · When to use vs alternatives · Common issues (converting a sharded checkpoint to a single file) · Supported models · Performance benchmarks (H100) · Advanced topics · Resources.
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install torchtitan`; `git clone https://github.com/pytorch/torchtitan` + `pip install -r requirements.txt`; `python scripts/download_hf_assets.py --repo_id meta-llama/Llama-3.1-8B --assets tokenizer --hf_token=...`; `pipeline_parallel_degree = 1` (no PP for 70B) / `= 8` (PP across nodes); `export TORCH_NCCL_AVOID_RECORD_STREAMS=1`; `python -m torch.distributed.checkpoint.format_utils …`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Sharded checkpoints (convertible to a single file); training logs.
- **Config / env:** `config_registry.py` entries; `TORCH_NCCL_AVOID_RECORD_STREAMS`; HF token.
- **Edge cases / guards:** The config-registry-by-name (not TOML path) note is the single biggest gotcha. Alternatives: Megatron-LM for maximum NVIDIA-only performance; DeepSpeed for broader ZeRO options. Checkpoints interoperate with torchtune/HuggingFace.
- **Rebuild notes:** n/a.

Sections: Quick start · Common workflows (1. **Full RLHF pipeline (SFT → Reward Model → RLOO)** — load model, load instruction dataset, configure training, train; then load the SFT model as the base, load preference data (chosen/rejected pairs), configure, train the reward model; then load the aligned model and test; 2. Simple preference alignment with DPO — or load your own data via `load_dataset("json", data_files="preferences.json")`; 3. Memory-efficient online RL with GRPO — load a prompt-only dataset) · When to use vs alternatives · Common issues ("Higher beta = more conservative (stays closer to reference)", "Lower beta = more aggressive alignment"; verify the dataset has a clear chosen > rejected signal) · Advanced topics · Hardware requirements · Resources.

- **Config / env:** CUDA required.
- **Edge cases / guards:** VRAM by method for a 7B model — SFT 16 GB (with LoRA), DPO 24 GB (stores the reference model), RLOO 40 GB (policy + reward model), GRPO 24 GB (more memory efficient); multi-GPU via `accelerate`. Method selection: SFT for prompt-completion pairs and basic instruction following; DPO for preferences without a reward model; RLOO when you have a reward model.
- **Rebuild notes:** Publishing per-method VRAM alongside the method-selection table is what makes RLHF plannable.

### Skill: mpp-agent  `id: optional.skill-mpp-agent`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/payments/mpp-agent/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/payments/mpp-agent`; catalog row "Pay HTTP 402 APIs via Machine Payments Protocol (MPP)."
- **What it does:** Actually pays an API that answers HTTP 402 — inspecting the challenge, paying it via `mppx` or a Tempo wallet, and verifying the receipt.
- **How it works:** v0.1.0, authors "Teknium (teknium1), Hermes Agent", MIT, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `Payments, MPP, HTTP-402, Tempo, Stripe`, `related_skills: [stripe-link-cli, stripe-projects]`. 125 lines. Sections: When to Use · Choosing a client · Prerequisites · Procedure (mppx, fastest path) — 1. Install + create an account; 2. Inspect the merchant's 402 challenge; 3. Pay the request; 4. Verify the receipt · Procedure (Tempo Wallet) · Pitfalls · Verification.
- **Inputs / options:** `npm install -g mppx`; `curl -i <url>` to read the `www-authenticate` challenge; the Tempo-wallet path.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Real money spent; a payment receipt; a funded agent wallet or `mppx` account.
- **Config / env:** Node.js 20+ on PATH; a funded wallet (Tempo / Privy / AgentCash) or an `mppx` account. Onboarding for the wallets lives at `https://tempo.xyz/SKILL.md`, `https://agents.privy.io/skill.md`, `https://agentcash.dev/skill.md`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** When a Stripe Link spend has produced a Shared Payment Token (SPT), prefer `link-cli mpp pay…` from the `stripe-link-cli` skill rather than this one. Triggered only when the user wants to *actually pay*, not merely log the 402.
- **Rebuild notes:** Separate "read the challenge" from "pay the challenge" so the agent can always report cost before spending.

### Skill: stripe-link-cli  `id: optional.skill-stripe-link-cli`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/payments/stripe-link-cli/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/payments/stripe-link-cli`; catalog row "Agent payments via Stripe Link — cards, SPT, approvals."
- **What it does:** Lets the agent make real purchases through Stripe Link — evaluating the merchant, creating a spend request, retrieving a card credential or Shared Payment Token securely, using it, then cleaning up.
- **How it works:** v0.1.0, authors "Teknium (teknium1), Hermes Agent", MIT, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `Payments, Stripe, Link, Checkout, MPP`, `related_skills: [mpp-agent, stripe-projects]`. 185 lines. Sections: When to Use · Prerequisites · Install · How to Run · Procedure (1. Check / establish auth; 2. **Evaluate the merchant before creating a spend request**; 3. List payment methods + shipping; 4. Create the spend request; 5. **Retrieve the credential — SECURELY**; 6. Use the credential; 7. Clean up) · Optional: run as an MCP server instead · Pitfalls · Verification.
- **Inputs / options:** `npm install -g @stripe/link-cli`; `hermes mcp add stripe-link --command "npx" --args "@stripe/link-cli --mcp"` to run it as an MCP server instead; trigger phrases "buy X", "pay for X", "make a purchase", "complete checkout", "get me a card", "I need a payment method", "log in to Link", "connect my Link wallet", or an HTTP 402 with `www-authenticate: ... method="stripe"`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Real charges; a Link account, payment method and spend-approval app created on first run; short-lived card credentials or SPTs.
- **Config / env:** Node.js 20+ on PATH (`node --version`); US-based account (a Link requirement).
- **Edge cases / guards:** "If the user wants a paid API call (HTTP 402, no checkout form), the `card` path is wrong — use SPT via this same skill, or hand off to the `mpp-agent` skill." The Link account, payment method and spend-approval app do **not** need to exist beforehand — the CLI walks the user through them on first run. Credential retrieval has its own SECURELY-marked step and a mandatory clean-up step.
- **Rebuild notes:** Merchant evaluation before spend creation, and an explicit credential clean-up step, are the two controls that make agent payments defensible.

- **Outputs / side effects:** Printed course/assignment lists; read-only.

- **Edge cases / guards:** Token setup is spelled out click-by-click: log in to the Canvas instance in a browser → **Account → Settings** (profile icon, then Settings) → scroll to **Approved Integrations** → **+ New Access Token** → name it (e.g. "Hermes Agent"), set an optional expiry, **Generate Token**.
- **Rebuild notes:** Include the exact UI path for token creation; every LMS hides it somewhere different.

### Skill: decision-questionnaire  `id: optional.skill-decision-questionnaire`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/productivity/decision-questionnaire/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/productivity/decision-questionnaire`; catalog row "Turn an unanswerable decision into a questionnaire doc."
- **What it does:** Converts a decision blocked on someone else's knowledge into a well-structured questionnaire document that person can answer asynchronously.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Matt Pocock (mattpocock/skills, to-questionnaire) + Hermes Agent", MIT, all platforms, tags `questionnaire, decision, async, stakeholder, discovery, communication`, `related_skills: [meeting-action-items, document-to-action-items]`. 116 lines. Sections: When to Use · **Core Principle: Interview the Send, Not the Subject** · Document Structure (`# <Questionnaire title>` → `## Context` → `## How to answer` → `## <Theme heading>` → `### <One question — a single idea, never compound>` → `## Anything else?`) · Pitfalls · Verification.
- **Inputs / options:** The blocked decision and the intended recipient.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A questionnaire document.
- **Config / env:** n/a.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "Do NOT use when the answer is discoverable from the environment (codebase, docs, web) — find it yourself first." Every question must be a single idea, never compound.
- **Rebuild notes:** The one-idea-per-question rule plus a trailing "Anything else?" is what makes async answers usable.

### Skill: here-now  `id: optional.skill-here-now`
