Sections: Current docs · Requirements · Create a site · Update an existing site · Use a Drive · API key storage · Getting an API key · State file · What to tell the user · `publish.sh` options · Beyond `publish.sh`.
- **Inputs / options:** `bash "$PUBLISH" {file-or-dir} --client hermes`; `bash "$PUBLISH" {file-or-dir} --slug {slug} --client hermes`; `bash "$DRIVE" default`; `bash "$DRIVE" ls "My Drive"`; `bash "$DRIVE" put "My Drive" notes/today.md --from ./notes/today.md`; `bash "$DRIVE" cat "My Drive" notes/today.md`; `bash "$DRIVE" share "My Drive" --perms write --prefix notes/ --ttl 7d`; device-code auth via `curl -sS https://here.now/api/auth/agent/request-code …` then `curl -sS https://here.now/api/auth/agent/verify-code …`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A public site at `{slug}.here.now`; files in a Drive; share links with permissions and TTL; a local state file and stored API key.

- **Edge cases / guards:** Requires `curl`, `file` and `jq`. A "What to tell the user" section standardizes the reported URL. Share links take explicit `--perms` and `--ttl`.
- **Rebuild notes:** Device-code auth plus a helper script means the agent never handles a password.

### Skill: memento-flashcards  `id: optional.skill-memento-flashcards`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/productivity/memento-flashcards/SKILL.md` + `scripts/{memento_cards.py,youtube_quiz.py}`; `hermes skills install official/productivity/memento-flashcards`; catalog row "Spaced-repetition flashcards: create, review, quiz, export."
- **What it does:** Runs a local, file-based spaced-repetition flashcard system where the user answers in free text and the agent grades the response before scheduling the next review.

Sections: Overview · When to Use · Quick Reference · Card Storage · Procedure (Creating Cards from Facts; **Activation Rules**; Manual Card Creation; Reviewing Due Cards; Spaced Repetition Algorithm; YouTube Quiz Generation; Export/Import CSV; Statistics) · Pitfalls · Verification.

Sections: Setup · **IMPORTANT: Shopping flow** · Commands (Catalog; Checkout — create from a variant or from an existing cart; Orders; Auth) · Sign in · Search rules · Showing products · Visualization · Checkout (Spending budget) · Orders · General rules · **Security — CRITICAL, follow all of these** · Safety & legal.

- **Rebuild notes:** Mirroring every CLI call as a documented direct-API call is a strong pattern for locked-down environments.

### Skill: shopify  `id: optional.skill-shopify`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/productivity/shopify/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/productivity/shopify`; catalog row "Query Shopify Admin/Storefront GraphQL APIs via curl."
- **What it does:** Queries and mutates a Shopify store through the Admin and Storefront GraphQL APIs using plain `curl` + `jq` — products, orders, customers, inventory, metafields, bulk operations and webhooks.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "community", MIT, all platforms, `prerequisites.env_vars: [SHOPIFY_ACCESS_TOKEN, SHOPIFY_STORE_DOMAIN]`, `prerequisites.commands: [curl, jq]`, tags `Shopify, E-commerce, Commerce, API, GraphQL`, `related_skills: [airtable, xurl]`, `homepage: https://shopify.dev/docs/api/admin-graphql`. Declares three `required_environment_variables`: `SHOPIFY_ACCESS_TOKEN` (prompt "Shopify Admin API access token (starts with shpat_)", help "Shopify admin → Settings → Apps and sales channels → Develop apps → Create an app → API credentials. Token shown ONCE on install."), `SHOPIFY_STORE_DOMAIN` (prompt "Your shop subdomain without protocol (e.g. my-store.myshopify.com)", help "The permanent myshopify.com domain, not your custom domain."), `SHOPIFY_API_VERSION` (prompt "Shopify API version (default 2026-01)", help "Stable quarterly version. Override if you need an older one."). 374 lines. Sections: Prerequisites · API Basics · Discovery (Shop info + current API version; List all supported API versions) · Products (Search products — first 20 matching a query; Paginate products by cursor, passing the previous `endCursor`; Get a product with variants + metafields; Create a product with one variant, adding variants after creation; Update price / SKU) · Orders (List recent orders — last 30 by default without `read_all_orders`; Fetch a single order with shipping address) · Customers (Search; Create) · Inventory (Get inventory for a variant across all locations) · Metafields & Metaobjects (Read; Write, works for any owner type) · Storefront API (public read-only) · Bulk Operations (1. Start bulk query; 2. Poll status; 3. When `status=COMPLETED`, download the JSONL file) · Webhooks (compare to the `X-Shopify-Hmac-Sha256` header) · Pitfalls · Safety.
- **Inputs / options:** a `shop_gql() { curl -sS -X POST … }` shell helper; queries such as `shop_gql '{ shop { name myshopifyDomain primaryDomain { url } currencyCode plan { displayName } } }' | jq`; `shop_gql '{ publicApiVersions { handle supported } }' | jq '.data.publicApiVersions[] | select(.supported)'`; `shop_gql '{ currentBulkOperation { id status errorCode objectCount fileSize url partialDataUrl } }'`; `curl -sS "$URL" > products.jsonl`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Real store mutations (products, customers, metafields); bulk JSONL downloads.
- **Config / env:** `SHOPIFY_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `SHOPIFY_STORE_DOMAIN`, `SHOPIFY_API_VERSION`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Token setup path spelled out — Settings → Apps and sales channels → Develop apps → Create an app → Configure Admin API scopes → Install app, with the token shown **once**. Orders are capped at the last 30 without the `read_all_orders` scope. A Safety section governs mutations.
- **Rebuild notes:** A single `shop_gql` shell function plus documented queries beats a bespoke client for an API the model already knows.

### Skill: telephony  `id: optional.skill-telephony`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/productivity/telephony/SKILL.md` + `scripts/telephony.py`; `hermes skills install official/productivity/telephony`; catalog row "Provision Twilio numbers, SMS/MMS, and AI outbound calls."
- **What it does:** Gives the agent a real phone presence — buying a Twilio number, sending SMS/MMS, polling inbound texts without a webhook server, placing direct TTS calls, navigating IVRs, and making AI voice calls via Bland.ai or Vapi.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Nous Research", MIT, all platforms, tags `telephony, phone, sms, mms, voice, twilio, bland.ai, vapi, calling, texting`, `related_skills: [maps, google-workspace, agentmail]`, `category: productivity`. 419 lines + `scripts/telephony.py`. Sections: What this solves · **Safety rules — mandatory** · Decision tree — which service to use? (1 "I want Hermes to own a real phone number"; 2 "I only need the easiest outbound AI phone call right now"; 3 "I want the best conversational AI voice quality"; 4 "I want to call with a custom prerecorded voice message") · Files and persistent state (`${HERMES_HOME:-~/.hermes}/.env`; `~/.hermes/telephony_state.json`) · Locate the helper script · Install · Provider setup (Twilio — owned number, SMS/MMS, direct calls, inbound SMS polling; Bland.ai — easiest outbound AI calling; Vapi — better conversational voice quality) · Diagnose current state · Common workflows (A. Buy an agent number and keep using it later; B. Send a text from the agent number; C. Check inbound texts later with no webhook server; D. Make a direct Twilio call with built-in TTS; E. Call with a prerecorded / custom voice message; F. Navigate a phone tree / IVR with Twilio direct calling; G. Outbound AI phone call with Bland.ai; H. Outbound AI phone call with Vapi on your owned number) · Suggested agent procedure · What this skill still does.
- **Inputs / options:** `hermes skills search telephony`; `hermes skills install official/productivity/telephony`; `python "$SCRIPT" save-twilio ACXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX your_auth_token_here`; `python "$SCRIPT" twilio-search --country US --area-code 702 --limit 5`; `python "$SCRIPT" twilio-buy "+17025551234" --save-env`; `python "$SCRIPT" twilio-owned`; `python "$SCRIPT" twilio-set-default "+17025551234" --save-env`; `python "$SCRIPT" twilio-set-default PNXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX --save-env`; `python "$SCRIPT" save-bland your_bland_api_key --voice mason`; plus the send-SMS, poll-inbox, call and Vapi sub-commands.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A purchased (billed) phone number; real SMS/MMS sent; real outbound calls placed; credentials in `.env` and state in `~/.hermes/telephony_state.json`.
- **Config / env:** Twilio account SID + auth token, Bland.ai key, Vapi key — all in `${HERMES_HOME:-~/.hermes}/.env`; default number in `telephony_state.json`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** A "Safety rules — mandatory" section leads the file (this skill can call and text real people and spend money). Inbound SMS is handled by polling so no public webhook server is required. The decision tree routes between owning a number vs one-off AI calls vs voice quality vs prerecorded audio.
- **Rebuild notes:** Polling for inbound messages instead of requiring a webhook is what makes telephony usable from a laptop; keep the safety rules above the fold.

### Skill: darwinian-evolver  `id: optional.skill-darwinian-evolver`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/research/darwinian-evolver/SKILL.md` + `scripts/{parrot_openrouter.py,show_snapshot.py}`, `templates/custom_problem_template.py`; `hermes skills install official/research/darwinian-evolver`; catalog row "Evolve prompts/regex/SQL/code with Imbue's evolution loop."
- **What it does:** Runs an evolutionary search loop over prompts, regexes, SQL or code — mutating candidates and selecting on a user-supplied scorer.
- **How it works:** v0.1.0, authors "Bihruze (Asahi0x), Hermes Agent", MIT, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `evolution, optimization, prompt-engineering, research`, `related_skills: [arxiv, jupyter-notebook]`. 200 lines. Sections: When to Use · Prerequisites · Install (One-Time) · Quick Start — The Built-In Parrot Example · Quick Start — OpenRouter Driver (No Anthropic Key) · Defining a Custom Problem · Hyperparameters That Actually Matter · Pitfalls · Verification · References.
- **Inputs / options:** `uv run darwinian_evolver parrot …`; `uv run --with openai python "$SKILL_DIR/scripts/parrot_openrouter.py" …`; `uv run --with openai python "$SKILL_DIR/scripts/show_snapshot.py" …`; `templates/custom_problem_template.py` for a new problem definition.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Evolution snapshots and the best-scoring organism; 50–500 LLM calls per run.

- **Edge cases / guards:** "If you don't have a scorer, stop and define one first — that's the hard part." Cost guidance: a typical run is 50–500 LLM calls — pennies on gpt-4o-mini, more on Claude Sonnet.
- **Rebuild notes:** Refuse to start without a scorer, and always print the cost envelope before running an evolutionary loop.

### Skill: drug-discovery  `id: optional.skill-drug-discovery`
- **Surface:** Skill

Sections: Core Workflows (1 Bioactive Compound Search (ChEMBL) — search compounds by target name e.g. "EGFR", "COX-2", "ACE"; get bioactivity data for a ChEMBL target ID; look up a molecule by ChEMBL ID; 2 Drug-Likeness Calculation (Lipinski Ro5 + Veber); 3 Drug Interaction & Safety Lookup (OpenFDA); 4 PubChem Compound Search; 5 Target & Disease Literature (OpenTargets)) · Reasoning Guidelines · Important Notes · Quick Reference.
- **Inputs / options:** `curl -s "https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chembl/api/data/target/search?q=${ENCODED}&format=json"`; `curl -s "https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chembl/api/data/activity?target_chembl_id=${TARGET_ID}&pchembl_value__gte=6&limit=10&format=json"`; `curl -s "https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chembl/api/data/molecule/${MOL_ID}?format=json"`; `curl -s "https://api.fda.gov/drug/label.json?search=drug_interactions:\"${ENCODED}\"&limit=3"`; `curl -s "https://api.fda.gov/drug/event.json?search=patient.drug.medicinalproduct:\"${ENCODED}\"&count=patient.reaction.reactionmeddrapt.exact&limit=10"`; `curl -s "https://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/rest/pug/compound/cid/${CID}/property/IsomericSMILES,InChIKey,IUPACName/JSON"`; `curl -s -X POST "https://api.platform.opentargets.org/api/v4/graphql" …`; plus `scripts/chembl_target.py` and `scripts/ro5_screen.py`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Printed compound/bioactivity/safety reports; read-only public APIs.
- **Config / env:** none (all five APIs are keyless).
- **Edge cases / guards:** A "Reasoning Guidelines" and an "Important Notes" section bound clinical interpretation; `pchembl_value__gte=6` filters to meaningfully potent activities.
- **Rebuild notes:** Five keyless public APIs cover the whole early-discovery loop; the ADMET reference file is what turns raw numbers into a verdict.

### Skill: duckduckgo-search  `id: optional.skill-duckduckgo-search`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/research/duckduckgo-search/SKILL.md` + `scripts/duckduckgo.sh`; `hermes skills install official/research/duckduckgo-search`; catalog row "Free keyless web, news, and image search via ddgs."
- **What it does:** Provides keyless web, news, image and video search through the `ddgs` package/CLI, usable as a fallback when the `web` toolset has no configured provider.
- **How it works:** v1.3.0, author "gamedevCloudy", MIT, all platforms, tags `search, duckduckgo, web-search, free, fallback`, `related_skills: [arxiv]`, and the notable field **`fallback_for_toolsets: [web]`** — declaring the skill as a stand-in for the `web` toolset. 239 lines. Sections: Detection Flow (check CLI availability) · Installation (Python package + CLI entrypoint; verify CLI) · **Method 1: CLI Search (Preferred)** — text, news, image and video search, region filter, recent-results filter (`d=day`, `w=week`, `m=month`, `y=year`), JSON output for parsing; CLI Flags · **Method 2: Python API (Only After Verification)** — Text/News/Image/Video Search; Quick Reference · Workflow: Search then Extract · Limitations · Troubleshooting · Pitfalls · Validated With.
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install ddgs`; the CLI search sub-commands and the documented flag list; the Python API as a second option.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Search results (JSON when requested).
- **Config / env:** none — keyless.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "Install `ddgs` only when DuckDuckGo search is specifically needed and the runtime does not already provide it." The Python API is gated behind CLI verification ("Only After Verification"). A "Validated With" section pins the versions the recipes were tested against.
- **Rebuild notes:** `fallback_for_toolsets` is a useful frontmatter idea — a skill that declares which built-in capability it can substitute for.

### Skill: gitnexus-explorer  `id: optional.skill-gitnexus-explorer`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/research/gitnexus-explorer/SKILL.md` + `scripts/proxy.mjs`; `hermes skills install official/research/gitnexus-explorer`; catalog row "Serve an interactive codebase knowledge graph web UI."
- **What it does:** Indexes a repository with GitNexus and serves an interactive codebase knowledge-graph web UI, optionally tunnelled for remote sharing.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Hermes Agent + Teknium", MIT, all platforms, tags `gitnexus, code-intelligence, knowledge-graph, visualization`, `related_skills: [hermes-agent, codebase-inspection]`. 215 lines. Sections: When to Use · Prerequisites · **Size Warning** · Steps (1. Clone and Build GitNexus — one-time setup; 2. Patch the Web UI for Remote Access — set `export const DEFAULT_BACKEND_URL = 'http://localhost:4747';`; 3. Index the Target Repo; 4. Create the Proxy Script; 5. Start the Services — Terminal 1 the GitNexus backend API, Terminal 2 the proxy serving web UI + API on one port; 6. Tunnel with Cloudflare (optional, for remote access) — install `cloudflared` if needed with no sudo, start the tunnel with `--config /dev/null` to avoid conflicts with existing named tunnels; 7. Cleanup — stop services, remove the index from the target repo) · Pitfalls.
- **Inputs / options:** `git clone https://github.com/abhigyanpatwari/GitNexus.git "$GITNEXUS_DIR"`; `npx gitnexus analyze --skip-agents-md`; `npx gitnexus serve &`; `node "$GITNEXUS_DIR/proxy.mjs" "$GITNEXUS_DIR/gitnexus-web/dist" 8888 &`; `curl -sL https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflared/releases/latest/download/cloudflared-linux-amd64 …`; `export PATH="$HOME/.local/bin:$PATH"`; `npx gitnexus clean`.
- **Outputs / side effects:** An index written into the target repo (removed by `gitnexus clean`), a backend on port 4747, a proxy on 8888, and optionally a public Cloudflare tunnel.
- **Config / env:** `GITNEXUS_DIR` (default `$HOME/…`); Node.js v18+; `git`; `cloudflared` auto-installed to `~/.local/bin`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** A Size Warning section bounds which repos are worth indexing; the proxy exists because the UI and API must be same-origin for remote access; cleanup explicitly removes the index from the user's repo.
- **Rebuild notes:** Always ship the teardown step for a skill that writes into the user's repository.

### Skill: osint-investigation  `id: optional.skill-osint-investigation`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/research/osint-investigation/SKILL.md` + 11 source references and 16 scripts; `hermes skills install official/research/osint-investigation`; catalog row "Follow the money via public records and sanctions data."
- **What it does:** Cross-references public records — SEC filings, federal contracts, lobbying disclosures, sanctions, offshore leaks, property deeds, corporate registries, courts, web archives, encyclopaedias and global news — to build an evidenced investigation.
- **How it works:** v0.1.0, author "Hermes Agent (adapted from ShinMegamiBoson/OpenPlanter, MIT)", MIT, all platforms, tags `osint, investigation, public-records, sec, sanctions, corporate-registry, property, courts, due-diligence, journalism`, `category: research`, `related_skills: [domain-intel, arxiv]`. 279 lines. Sections: When to use this skill · Workflow (1. Identify which sources apply — federal financial/regulatory vs identity/property/litigation/archives/news; 2. Acquire data; 3. Resolve entities across sources — e.g. match lobbying clients from Senate LDA against contract recipients from USAspending; 4. Statistical timing correlation (optional); 5. Build the findings). Sources documented in `references/sources/`: `sec-edgar.md`, `usaspending.md`, `senate-ld.md`, `ofac-sdn.md`, `icij-offshore.md`, `nyc-acris.md`, `opencorporates.md`, `courtlistener.md`, `wayback.md`, `wikipedia.md`, `gdelt.md`. Scripts: `_http.py`, `_normalize.py`, `build_findings.py`, `entity_resolution.py`, and one fetcher per source (`fetch_sec_edgar.py`, `fetch_usaspending.py`, `fetch_senate_ld.py`, `fetch_ofac_sdn.py`, `fetch_icij_offshore.py`, `fetch_nyc_acris.py`, `fetch_opencorporates.py`, `fetch_courtlistener.py`, `fetch_gdelt.py`, `fetch_wayback.py`, …).
- **Inputs / options:** `python SKILL_DIR/scripts/fetch_sec_edgar.py --cik 0000320193 …`; `fetch_usaspending.py --recipient "EXAMPLE CORP" …`; `fetch_senate_ld.py --client "EXAMPLE CORP" …`; `fetch_ofac_sdn.py --out data/ofac_sdn.csv`; `fetch_icij_offshore.py --entity "EXAMPLE CORP" …`; `fetch_nyc_acris.py --name "SMITH, JOHN" …` and `--address "571 HUDSON" …`; `fetch_opencorporates.py --query "Example Corp" …` (with `--token` or `OPENCORPORATES_API_TOKEN`); `fetch_courtlistener.py --query "Smith v. Example Corp" …`; plus the GDELT and Wayback fetchers.
- **Outputs / side effects:** Downloaded datasets (the ICIJ bulk CSV is ~70 MB on first use, cached 30 days under `$HERMES_OSINT_CACHE/icij/`, default `~/.cache/hermes-osint/icij/`), resolved entity tables and a findings document.
- **Config / env:** `OPENCORPORATES_API_TOKEN` (free token required for OpenCorporates' 130+ jurisdiction registry); `HERMES_OSINT_UA=your-app/1.0 (your@email)` to identify yourself to the APIs; `HERMES_OSINT_CACHE`.
- **Edge cases / guards:** Sources have very different rate/identification requirements — hence the mandatory user-agent env var. Entity resolution is a separate explicit step so cross-source matches are auditable rather than assumed.
- **Rebuild notes:** One fetcher per source plus a shared normalizer and an explicit entity-resolution stage is the right shape; cache bulk downloads with a TTL.

### Skill: parallel-cli  `id: optional.skill-parallel-cli`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/research/parallel-cli/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/research/parallel-cli`; catalog row "Agent-native web search, deep research, and enrichment."
- **What it does:** Drives the Parallel CLI for agent-native search, extraction, asynchronous deep research, structured enrichment, FindAll entity discovery and monitoring.

Sections: When to use it · Installation (Homebrew; npm; Python package; Standalone installer) · Authentication · **Core rule set** · Quick reference · Common flags and patterns · Search · Extraction · Deep research (Synchronous; Async launch + poll; Context chaining / follow-up) · Enrichment (Suggest columns; Plan a config; Inline data; Non-interactive file run; YAML config run; Status / polling) · FindAll · Monitor · Recommended Hermes usage patterns (Fast answer with citations; URL investigation; Long research workflow; Structured enrichment workflow) · Error handling and exit codes · Maintenance · Pitfalls.
- **Inputs / options:** `npm install -g parallel-web-cli`; `pip install "parallel-web-tools[cli]"`; `curl -fsSL https://parallel.ai/install.sh | bash`; `pipx install "parallel-web-tools[cli]"` + `pipx ensurepath`; `export PARALLEL_API_KEY="***"`; `pip install --upgrade parallel-web-tools`; the search/extract/deep-research/enrich/findall/monitor sub-commands and their YAML config form.

- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/research/pinecone-research/SKILL.md` + `scripts/{memory_manager.py,rag_pipeline.py}`; `hermes skills install official/research/pinecone-research`; catalog row "Agent RAG and long-term memory with Pinecone."

Sections: When to use this skill · Quick start (Setup; Basic RAG pipeline — initialize Pinecone, create or connect to an index, build the vector store, retrieve relevant context; Namespace-based session memory — store per-session memory, query across all sessions with no namespace filter) · Best practices · Resources.
- **Inputs / options:** `pip install pinecone-client langchain-pinecone langchain-openai`; `export PINECONE_API_KEY="your-api-key"`; the two bundled scripts.

### Skill: qmd  `id: optional.skill-qmd`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/research/qmd/SKILL.md`; `hermes skills install official/research/qmd`; catalog row "Hybrid local search over notes, docs, and transcripts."
- **What it does:** Indexes local markdown/text collections and searches them with BM25, vector search, hybrid search with reranking, HyDE and query expansion — over CLI or MCP.
- **How it works:** v1.0.0, author "Hermes Agent + Teknium", MIT, `platforms: [macos, linux]`, tags `Search, Knowledge-Base, RAG, Notes, MCP, Local-AI`, `related_skills: [obsidian, hermes-agent, arxiv]`. 442 lines. Sections: When to Use · Prerequisites (Node.js ≥ 22 required — check version, macOS install/upgrade via Homebrew, Linux via NodeSource or nvm; SQLite with Extension Support, macOS only; Install qmd, or with Bun; Verify Installation) · Quick Reference · Setup Workflow (1. Add Collections — a notes directory, project docs, meeting transcripts; list all collections; 2. Add Context Descriptions; 3. Generate Embeddings; 4. Verify) · Search Patterns (Fast Keyword Search (BM25); Semantic Vector Search; **Hybrid Search with Reranking (Best Quality)**; Structured Multi-Mode Queries — BM25 for exact terms plus vector for concepts, with query expansion; Query Syntax for lex/BM25 mode; HyDE (Hypothetical Document Embeddings); Scoping to Collections; Output Formats) · **MCP Integration (Recommended)** (Option A Stdio Mode — simple; Option B HTTP Daemon Mode — fast, recommended for heavy use, persists across agent restarts, runs on `http://localhost:8181` by default; Keeping the Daemon Running; MCP Tools Reference) · CLI Usage (Without MCP) · How the Search Pipeline Works · Best practices.
- **Inputs / options:** `node --version` (must be ≥ 22); `brew install node@22`; `curl -fsSL https://deb.nodesource.com/setup_22.x | sudo -E bash -`; `npm install -g @tobilu/qmd` (or Bun); `qmd --version`; `qmd status`; `export QMD_EMBED_MODEL="your-multilingual-model"`; the collection/embed/search sub-commands.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A local index and embeddings database; an optional HTTP daemon on port 8181.
- **Config / env:** `QMD_EMBED_MODEL`; Node ≥ 22; SQLite with extension support on macOS.
- **Edge cases / guards:** macOS needs a SQLite build with extension support (the system one may not qualify). The daemon mode exists because re-loading embeddings per query is slow.
- **Rebuild notes:** Offering the same index over CLI and MCP, with a persistent daemon for the MCP path, is the right shape for local search.

### Skill: research-paper-writing  `id: optional.skill-research-paper-writing`
- **Surface:** Skill
- **Where:** `optional-skills/research/research-paper-writing/SKILL.md` (1,628 lines) + 10 references and 7 conference LaTeX template sets; `hermes skills install official/research/research-paper-writing`; catalog row "Write ML papers for NeurIPS/ICML/ICLR: design→submit."
- **What it does:** Runs an end-to-end ML research paper pipeline — project setup, literature review with verified citations, experiment design and execution, drafting, and submission to a specific venue.
- **How it works:** v1.1.0, `title: Research Paper Writing Pipeline`, author "Orchestra Research", MIT, `dependencies: [semanticscholar, arxiv, habanero, requests, scipy, numpy, matplotlib, SciencePlots]`, `platforms: [linux, macos]`, tags `Research, Paper Writing, Experiments, ML, AI, NeurIPS, ICML, ICLR, ACL, AAAI, COLM, LaTeX, Citations, Statistical Analysis`, `category: research`, `related_skills: [arxiv, subagent-driven-development]`, `requires_toolsets: [terminal, files]`. Sections: When To Use This Skill · Core Philosophy (Proactivity and Collaboration) · **Phase 0: Project Setup** (0.1 Explore the Repository; 0.2 Organize the Workspace; 0.3 Set Up Version Control; 0.4 Identify the Contribution; 0.5 Create a TODO List; 0.6 Estimate Compute Budget with a simple cost-tracker pattern; 0.7 Multi-Author Coordination) · **Phase 1: Literature Review** (1.1 Identify Seed Papers; 1.2 Search for Related Work via `web_search` and `web_extract`; 1.2b Deepen the Search — breadth-first then depth; **1.3 Verify Every Citation** — fetch BibTeX via DOI; 1.4 Organize Related Work) · **Phase 2: Experiment Design** (2.1 Map Claims to Experiments; 2.2 Design Baselines; 2.3 Define Evaluation Protocol; 2.4 Write Experiment Scripts, saving after each problem/task; 2.5 Design Human Evaluation if applicable) · **Phase 3: Experiment Execution & Monitoring** (3.1 Launch Experiments; 3.2 Set Up Monitoring with a cron pattern; 3.3 Handle failures …) and subsequent phases through drafting and submission, including a venue table (e.g. "NeurIPS | Paper checklist in appendix, lay summary if accepted"). References: `autoreason-methodology.md`, `checklists.md`, `citation-workflow.md`, `experiment-patterns.md`, `human-evaluation.md`, `paper-types.md`, `phase5-paper-drafting.md`, `reviewer-guidelines.md`, `sources.md`, `writing-guide.md`. Conference LaTeX templates bundled for **aaai2026** (`aaai2026-unified-template.tex`, `aaai2026-unified-supp.tex`, `aaai2026.sty`, `aaai2026.bst`, `aaai2026.bib`, README), **acl** (`acl_latex.tex`, `acl_lualatex.tex`, `acl.sty`, `acl_natbib.bst`, `custom.bib`, `anthology.bib.txt`, `formatting.md`, README), **icml2026**, **iclr2026**, **neurips2025**, **colm2025**, plus a top-level `templates/README.md`.
- **Inputs / options:** `git init`; `git remote add origin <repo-url>`; `git checkout -b paper-draft`; `git add -A`; `git commit -m "Add <experiment name>: <key finding in 1 line>"`; `git push`; `pip install arxiv`; the per-venue template directories.
- **Outputs / side effects:** A version-controlled paper workspace, experiment scripts and results, a verified BibTeX file, and a camera-ready LaTeX build.
- **Config / env:** `terminal` and `files` toolsets; the listed Python dependencies.
- **Edge cases / guards:** "Verify Every Citation" is its own numbered step with a DOI→BibTeX fetch, because fabricated citations are the characteristic LLM failure here. A compute budget is estimated in Phase 0, before any experiment runs. Commit messages are specified to carry the key finding in one line.
- **Rebuild notes:** Phase 0 (budget + contribution statement) and citation verification are the two steps that make an LLM-written paper defensible; everything else is scaffolding.
